# Best guesses

Generated by `cargo xtask guesses` from the `guesses` field of `spec/inventory/*.toml`; do not edit. `cargo xtask ci` fails when it is out of date.

Each entry is a choice FontCraft made where no written source says what the reference does and nobody has observed it. Revisit an entry when a source or an observation settles it: change the behaviour if it differs, then remove the entry. Behaviour that follows the handbook but is not yet observed is not a guess; the row's `status` and `notes` say so.

To add a guess, append a sentence to the row's `guesses` list and run `cargo xtask guesses`. A live command whose status is `inferred` or `memory` must have at least one entry, unless its row says `own = true` (no counterpart in the reference app). **Live** is yes when the row is a command registered in the engine or the shell; it is – for other row kinds.

**1714 guesses on 1109 rows (401 of them live commands).**

## `00-app-basics.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `edit.undo` | M0 | memory | yes | The menu label and Cmd+Z are recalled from the reference, not read in a source. |
| `edit.redo` | M0 | memory | yes | The menu label and Cmd+Shift+Z are recalled from the reference, not read in a source. |
| `edit.cut` | M3 | inferred | yes | What Cut puts on the clipboard (our JSON text with an SVG flavour) is our format; the reference's clipboard content is not documented. |
| `edit.copy` | M3 | inferred | yes | The clipboard text is our JSON format with an SVG flavour; what the reference puts on the clipboard is not documented. |
| `edit.paste` | M3 | inferred | yes | Paste reads our JSON clipboard format or SVG; which other clipboard data the reference accepts is not documented. |
| `file.close` | M0 | memory | yes | The Save, Don't Save and Cancel question and Cmd+W are recalled from the reference, not read in a source. |
| `file.openRecent` | M4 | inferred | yes | The list holds the last ten documents opened or saved, newest first; a file that no longer exists is dropped when the list is shown. Settled by observing the Start Window. |
| `file.openRecent` | M4 | inferred | yes | File › Open Recent without an index or a path shows the Start Window's list of recent documents, since the menu bar has no submenu of files. Settled when the menu bar can list them. |
| `file.openRecent` | M4 | inferred | yes | A path is kept once, as an absolute path; opening or saving it again moves it to the top. Settled by observing the Start Window. |
| `path.convertToCubic` | M3 | handbook | yes | New handles are not rounded to the grid, so the outline stays exact; the handbook does not say whether the reference rounds them. |
| `dialog.livePreview` (behavior) | M3 | inferred | – | Every filter dialog previews its result live and OK keeps it as one undo step; a tutorial video shows this for Transformations only. |
| `dialog.livePreview` (behavior) | M3 | inferred | – | Dialogs remember their last values for the session, and their first values are ours (11-filters open questions). |
| `dialog.fieldKeys` (behavior) | M3 | inferred | – | Dialog number fields follow the Info box's editing and arrow-key rules (infoBox.fieldEditing, infoBox.arrowKeyStepping); the handbook documents them for the Info box only. |

## `03a-edit-view-drawing.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `file.newFromGlyphSets` | M4 | handbook | yes | Phonetics and TransLatin sets are listed under latin, Coptic and the other Greek sets under greek. Settled by observing the reference's chooser. |
| `file.newFromGlyphSets` | M4 | handbook | yes | A script picked without naming sets takes its Core set (Latin, Cyrillic, Greek and Arabic Core); a script without a default takes its first set. Settled by observing what the reference's chooser preselects when a script is switched on. |
| `file.newFromGlyphSets` | M4 | handbook | yes | An encoded glyph takes the glyph database's name for its code point, an unencoded one the database's nice name for its source name; the source name is kept when the database knows neither. Settled by comparing a new document from the reference's chooser with the database names. |
| `file.newFromGlyphSets` | M4 | handbook | yes | The new document keeps New's drawn .notdef first and adds no other glyph than the sets'; glyphs follow the default sort order. Settled by observing a new document from the reference's chooser. |
| `glyphData.glyphSets` | M4 | inferred | yes | A script id that stands for several Unicode scripts (`kana`) or none (`math`) reads as the id with a capital (`Kana`, `Math`). Settled by observing the reference's script names in its chooser. |
| `view.editSelectedGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | An open tab that holds exactly these glyphs is shown instead of a new tab. |
| `view.showNextTab` | M3 | handbook | yes | After the last tab comes Font View, and before Font View the last tab. |
| `view.closeTab` | M3 | handbook | yes | The tab to the left of a closed showing tab shows next. |
| `view.closeTab` | M3 | handbook | yes | The last 32 closed tabs are kept for Reopen Last Closed Tab. |
| `edit.selectAll` | M3 | handbook | yes | In Font View it selects every glyph; the handbook describes edit mode only. |
| `selection.selectNext` | M3 | handbook | yes | Tab steps through nodes in path order, then the next path, then anchors, then guides, then back to the start (03a question 8). |
| `selection.set` | M3 | inferred | yes | The selection belongs to the showing tab and is cleared when another glyph is edited; the reference's rule is not documented. |
| `selection.selectInRegion` | M3 | inferred | yes | A turned region rotates about its corner (x, y), our reading of the marquee turned with Ctrl. |
| `path.toggleNodeType` | M3 | handbook | yes | Turning a corner smooth adds missing handles a third of the way along the segment and turns handles that are not collinear to the bisector of their directions, keeping their lengths (03a question 9). |
| `path.tidyUpPaths` | M3 | handbook | yes | Points within 0.01 units count as on a line or as one point. |
| `path.tidyUpPaths` | M3 | handbook | yes | A node counts as smooth when a curve meets it and its two directions are within 1° (03a question 9). |
| `infoBox.setSizeLock` | M3 | inferred | yes | The lock starts open; the handbook gives no default. |
| `path.alignSelection` | M3 | handbook | yes | When width equals height the selection aligns on one X, and the transformation origin's column or row picks min, centre or max (03a question 13). |
| `edit.delete` | M3 | handbook | yes | The refitted segment keeps its end nodes and the directions it leaves and enters them in, and fits the two handle lengths by least squares over the removed segments (03a question 10). |
| `edit.delete` | M3 | handbook | yes | With only handles selected the segment becomes a line; with every node of a path selected the path is removed. |
| `edit.delete` | M3 | handbook | yes | In text mode, Delete run from a menu or the control channel removes the selected text, else the item after the cursor, as the Delete key does; the Edit menu of the running reference app in text mode settles it. |
| `edit.deleteInAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | Nodes are matched across masters by address; a layer whose structure differs is skipped and listed (03a question 11). |
| `edit.deleteInAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | In text mode it is refused with a message, so it never deletes nodes the person cannot see; the Edit menu of the running reference app in text mode settles it. |
| `path.cutWithLine` | M3 | inferred | yes | Crossings are paired within each stretch where the line enters and leaves the filled area, so a cut through overlapping paths joins their pieces; no source shows the Knife on overlapping paths. |
| `path.openCorner` | M3 | handbook | yes | A master with stems of one direction only uses that stem for both directions, and a master without stems uses an overlap of 4% of the em. |
| `path.openCorner` | M3 | handbook | yes | Each end node moves along its own segment's direction, so the handles keep their directions. |
| `path.openCorner` | M3 | handbook | yes | Corners whose two directions are within 1° of straight or reversed are left alone. |
| `path.reconnectNodes` | M3 | handbook | yes | Pairs go nearest first, ties to the lower address (03a question 15). |
| `path.reconnectNodes` | M3 | handbook | yes | An open-corner pair becomes one node where the incoming direction of the first and the outgoing direction of the second meet; any other pair is rewired with straight segments. |
| `path.makeNodeFirst` | M3 | handbook | yes | The context menu offers it only when every selected on-curve node lies on a closed path. |
| `path.reverseContoursForAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | The label "Reverse Contours for All Masters" is ours, and paths are matched across masters by address (03a questions 11 and 18). |
| `path.correctPathDirection` | M3 | handbook | yes | A path is inside another when that one is larger, holds its bounding box and holds most of its sample points; paths that only overlap are both outer. |
| `path.correctPathDirection` | M3 | handbook | yes | Shapes are sorted by the bottom-left corner of their bounds, and the start node is the lowest, then leftmost, on-curve node (03a question 16). |
| `path.addExtremes` | M3 | handbook | yes | A node within 2 units and 2° of an extreme keeps its place, and its handles within 2° of the axis are made exactly horizontal or vertical (03a question 17). |
| `path.addExtremes` | M3 | handbook | yes | An extreme is skipped when the chord from it to the end of its segment, or to the extreme before it, is shorter than 3 units. |
| `path.forceExtremes` | M3 | handbook | yes | Force Extremes adds every extreme that rounds to a new point, however short the segment it leaves. |
| `path.addInflections` | M3 | inferred | yes | An inflection within 1 unit of a segment end gets no node, the distance under which check.outlines.inflections ignores one. |
| `path.focusOnSelectedPath` | M3 | handbook | yes | Path focus belongs to the tab and is cleared when the tab's edited glyph changes (03a question 19). |
| `glyph.toggleLocked` | M3 | handbook | yes | Locks are stored in the file, and a glyph lock refuses every change to the glyph's own data (layers, outlines, anchors, guides, width, metrics keys) but not kerning (03a question 20). |
| `path.setStroke` | M3 | inferred | yes | The axis-aligned cap is stored as cap code 4; not confirmed against the reference's files. |
| `path.setStroke` | M3 | inferred | yes | A forced cap axis is kept in the native key `lineCapAxis`, which .glyphs files do not hold, so saving as .glyphs loses it (a known loss). |
| `path.setStroke` | M3 | inferred | yes | A UFO keeps no stroke or mask attributes, so saving as UFO loses strokes and masks (a known loss). |
| `path.setStroke` | M3 | inferred | yes | Stroke positions and mask values keep the stored form of the document's .glyphs version, so converting a document between versions 3 and 4 changes where strokes sit and what masks do (a known loss until the reference's conversion is observed). |
| `path.setMask` | M3 | handbook | yes | Only paths take the mask flag, though a mask cuts components below it too (03a question 23). |
| `anchor.add` | M6 | handbook | yes | When `name` is omitted the anchor is named new anchor, and a name already on the layer, given or not, gets underscores appended until it is unique, as Option-drag duplication does (anchors.uniqueNamesPerLayer); adding two anchors without renaming in the reference app settles it. |
| `anchor.rename` | M6 | handbook | yes | Renaming an anchor to a name already on the layer is refused with a message; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.setAnchors` | M6 | handbook | yes | Set Anchors adds only the default anchors the layer lacks and leaves existing anchors where they are; Set Anchors on a glyph with a moved anchor in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.setAnchors` | M6 | handbook | yes | The default anchors come from our table in `components::default_anchors` (M6.3): letters get top (cap height for uppercase; x-height for lowercase, or the ascender when the outline rises above the middle between the two; the outline's top without a case) and bottom (baseline), centred on the outline and slanted by the italic angle, plus ogonek for A E I O U a e i idotless o u, center for D H L O T d h l o t and topright for L O U d l o t u; marks above get _top at the x-height (cap height for .case) and top on the outline's top, marks below _bottom on the baseline and bottom on the outline's bottom, ogonekcomb _ogonek, horncomb and caroncomb.alt _topright; Han, Hangul, Kana, Yi and Bopomofo letters get none; the anchors stored in corpus sources saved by the reference app measure the table (M6 plan § 5). |
| `glyph.setAnchors` | M6 | handbook | yes | A ligature named after its parts gets caret_1 to caret_(n-1) on the baseline, dividing the advance width equally; Set Anchors on f_f_l in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.resetAnchors` | M6 | handbook | yes | Reset Anchors keeps locked anchors, since a lock refuses every other change to an anchor; resetting a glyph with a locked anchor in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.resetAnchorsForAllMasters` | M6 | handbook | yes | The shortcut is Cmd+Alt+Shift+U, Reset Anchors with Option added as for the other Option variants; the Glyph menu of the reference app with Option held settles it. |
| `guide.setProperties` | M3 | inferred | yes | Both sidebearings are written as the guide's `alignment = center` and the right one as `right`; our reading of the format, not confirmed. |
| `guide.remove` | M3 | inferred | yes | Delete removes a selected guide; the chapter does not say how the reference deletes one (03a question 27). |
| `guide.displayLines` | M3 | inferred | yes | A display-lines parameter without a script prefix applies to every glyph, since the glyph data that marks CJK glyphs is not built yet. |
| `guide.displayLines` | M3 | inferred | yes | The CJK grid divides the box from x 0 to the width and from the descender to the ascender. |
| `guide.displayLines` | M3 | inferred | yes | The design grid parameter is read as a horizontal and a vertical step in units, drawn over the CJK box, or over the glyph's width and metric span when no CJK grid is set; the handbook does not explain it. |
| `start.shownAtLaunchWithoutDocuments` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | With Show window on startup off, the app opens a new document at launch, as File › New does. Settled by observing a launch with the checkbox cleared. |
| `tools.sharedButtonCycling` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Holding a shared toolbar button for 0.4 s opens its menu; the reference's hold time is not documented. |
| `nodes.smoothAndCornerTypes` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Selected nodes, handles and segments are drawn in orange; no source names the reference's selection colour. |
| `selection.marqueeSelectsNodesAndHandles` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A marquee selects the handles inside it whatever their node (03a question 7). |
| `selection.segmentCountsAsSelected` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Clicking a segment selects its two end nodes (03a question 7). |
| `move.nodesCarryHandles` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Alt with an arrow key moves only the selected points (03a question 1). |
| `move.nudge` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Ctrl+Alt with an arrow key nudges, by the arrow increments 1, 10 and 100 (03a question 2). |
| `nodes.metricLineHighlight` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | "Vertical metric lines" are read as the master's height lines: ascender, cap height, x-height, baseline, descender and any other metric but the italic angle. |
| `infoBox.selectionGeometryFields` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The width and height lock starts open; the handbook gives no default. |
| `align.anchorToPoints` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | With two points the anchor keeps its height and moves horizontally onto the line at the italic angle through the points' midpoint, and a locked anchor refuses it; aligning an anchor between two nodes of different heights on an italic master in the reference app settles it. |
| `align.componentOriginPoint` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The left sidebearing line is x = 0 of the base glyph, and on a slanted master it is slanted about half the x-height, so without an origin anchor the origin point is the base glyph's (0, 0) on every master; aligning a component on an italic master in the reference app settles it. |
| `duplicate.optionDrag` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Alt held at mouse-down on a selection drags a copy, and Alt pressed after the drag started switches to nudge (03a question 1). |
| `stroke.joinsAndCaps` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Every join and cap shape is our construction on offset curves (docs/spec/03a § 17): miter up to 4 half widths, then bevel. |
| `stroke.joinsAndCaps` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Round full is round on the outer side and fills the inner corner with an arc of half the stroke width; round inset is a round cap that ends at the end node. |
| `stroke.joinsAndCaps` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The axis-aligned cap cuts through the middle of the band at the end node and is a butt end when the path ends within about 6° of a forced axis. |
| `compositing.shapesCombineInOrder` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Export applies strokes and masks before overlap removal, so a glyph whose layer has a stroke or a mask is compiled as contours only; the handbook does not say how the reference exports them. |
| `anchors.ccmpAlongsideMark` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | ccmp follows from the glyph names alone, whether or not the marks carry anchors for the mark feature; Update in the reference app on a font with idotless and an anchorless acutecomb would settle it. |
| `snapping.toNodesAndHandles` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The snap distance is 4 screen points, with the priority nodes, handles, guides, metric lines (03a question 28). |
| `snapping.toNodesAndHandles` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Ctrl suspends snapping as well as magnetic guides. |
| `guides.knobAndSelection` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A drag on the guide away from its knob turns it about the knob, the pressed side following the pointer (03a question 27). |
| `guides.knobAndSelection` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A double-click on the guide away from its knob opens dialog.guideProperties, our addition. |
| `guides.angleLock` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A drag on an angle-locked guide away from its knob moves the guide, as a knob drag does (03a question 27). |
| `guides.sidebearingReference` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Left keeps the guide's x, right moves it with the width, and both keeps its fraction of the width (03a question 27). |
| `guides.globalScopeFilter` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A filter that does not read as a predicate leaves the guide shown in every glyph. Settled by opening a file whose guide filter the reference cannot read. |
| `window.start` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Each glyph set's checkbox shows its glyph count after its name. Settled by observing the chooser. |
| `window.start` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Without earlier picks, Latin is included with its default sets. Settled by observing the chooser on a fresh install. |
| `window.start` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A script switched on takes its default sets (its Core set) checked; the checkboxes of a script that is not included are greyed out. Settled by observing the chooser. |
| `window.start` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | While there are recent documents the chooser has a Recent Documents button that goes back to the list. Settled by observing the window. |
| `window.start` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The window closes when a document is opened or created. Settled by observing the window. |
| `dialog.primitiveMeasurements` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The width and height fields run from 1 to 2,000,000 units and open at 100. |
| `dialog.primitiveMeasurements` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The click is the shape's bottom-left corner (03a question 5). |
| `editView.toolbar` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | A secondary click on a shared button also opens its menu; no source shows the reference doing so. |
| `dialog.shapeOrder` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The first row is the bottom shape (shape 0, drawn first). |
| `dialog.shapeOrder` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | Rows read `<index>: Path (<n> nodes)`, `<index>: Component <name>` or `<index>: Image`; what the reference's rows show and which end is the top are not observed (03a open question 24). |
| `dialog.guideProperties` (panel) | M3 | inferred | – | The dialog is our own form over guide.setProperties; the reference edits a guide in the Info box. |
| `start.showAtLaunch` (setting) | M4 | handbook | – | On by default, since the Start Window is described as appearing at launch. Settled by observing a fresh install. |
| `draw` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | A click on a segment places the new node on the segment and leaves the clicked path alone (03a question 3). |
| `draw` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | Shift+click on a segment inserts at an extremum or inflection even while drawing. |
| `draw` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | A click on an open end continues that path, and while drawing joins the two paths. |
| `draw` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | A missing handle of a curved segment lies a third of the way from its node to the other handle. |
| `draw` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | Shift keeps nodes and handles to 45° steps (03a question 4). |
| `draw` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | Escape or another tool ends the path. |
| `pencil` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | The stroke is fitted with cubic curves within one screen point, so it stays node-heavy. |
| `pencil` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | A stroke that ends on its start, within the hit tolerance, gives a closed path. |
| `select` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | The canvas context menu's sections, item targets and what a right-click selects first are our design (docs/spec/03a § 6). |

## `03b-edit-view-display.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `infoBox.setReferencePoint` | M3 | handbook | yes | The reference point starts at bottom left; the handbook gives no default. |
| `selection.getInfo` | M3 | inferred | yes | Its default reference point, bottom left, is ours; the handbook gives none for the Info box. |
| `component.setTransform` | M6 | handbook | yes | The command refuses a position edit on an aligned component (only `y` on one aligned horizontally) and any edit on a locked component, and accepts slants within ±89°, rotations within ±360° and scales from −10,000 % to 10,000 % except 0; typing such values into the Info box of the reference app settles it. |
| `view.zoomIn` | M3 | handbook | yes | One step multiplies the scale by √2, and the zoom value stays between 50 and 64,000 (03b question 8). |
| `view.zoomToActiveLayer` | M3 | handbook | yes | The descender-to-ascender span, or the advance width when that is the tighter limit, fills the window less 8% on each side. |
| `view.zoomToRect` | M3 | inferred | yes | The rectangle fills the window less 8% on each side, the margin of Zoom to Active Layer. |
| `view.showAnchors` | M6 | handbook | yes | Show Anchors is on by default; the View menu of a fresh install of the reference app settles it. |
| `view.showMeasurementLine` | M5 | handbook | yes | The numbers sit just above the line, each centred between the advance edge and the crossing it measures, and a glyph narrower than 24 screen points gets none; watching the reference app with a known glyph would settle it. |
| `view.showMeasurementLine` | M5 | handbook | yes | In top-to-bottom text the line is not drawn; the handbook only describes horizontal text. |
| `path.editBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | The edited side belongs to the tab and stays when another glyph, layer or master is edited. |
| `path.editBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | Audit markers are not drawn on the background. |
| `path.selectionToBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | A selected point or segment copies its whole path, and the other side's guides, hints, annotations and image stay; the handbook does not say what is replaced. |
| `path.addSelectionToBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | A copied anchor replaces the background's anchor of the same name. |
| `path.swapWithBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | Swap exchanges shapes, anchors, guides, hints and annotations but not the placed image, and removes a background the swap leaves empty. |
| `path.clearBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | Clear Background is its own Path menu item and acts on the active layer of the targeted glyphs, image included (03b question 2). |
| `path.assignBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | Glyphs are matched by name and masters by name, then by position; a source with fewer masters gives its first (03b question 3). |
| `editView.toggleTextMode` | M5 | handbook | yes | Leaving text mode makes the Select tool active, and returning puts the text cursor before the glyph that was edited (at the end of the text, when the last glyph was edited, the cursor stays at the end); no source names either, and the toolbar after Escape, in the running app or a video, settles it. |
| `editView.insertText` | M5 | handbook | yes | A `/name` escape ends at the next slash, space or line break, and one space after it is consumed; the corpus files show the escape but not how it ends. |
| `editView.setCursor` | M5 | inferred | yes | Shift+click extends the selection to the click, keeping the end of the selection away from it; the reference app settles it. |
| `editView.setCursor` | M5 | inferred | yes | A double-click on a glyph in text mode also makes the Select tool active; the reference app settles it. |
| `edit.find` | M5 | handbook | yes | The pop-up opens with an empty search field each time; whether the reference keeps the last query would settle it. |
| `edit.find` | M5 | handbook | yes | Up and Down in the search field move the selection to the result above or below; the handbook names only clicks. |
| `edit.find` | M5 | handbook | yes | The selected results go into the text in list order, not in the order they were picked. |
| `glyph.search` | M5 | inferred | yes | The default mode is Name; the handbook does not say which mode the pop-up starts in. |
| `glyph.search` | M5 | inferred | yes | For a query of one term, the glyph encoded with it or named exactly by it comes first, then names that differ only in case, then the rest in glyph order; the reference's order of results is not documented. |
| `glyph.search` | M5 | inferred | yes | An empty query lists nothing. |
| `glyph.search` | M5 | inferred | yes | A Unicode term may start with U+. |
| `editView.previousGlyph` | M5 | handbook | yes | At the first glyph of the order Previous Glyph does nothing (no wrap), and with several glyphs selected in the text it replaces only the current glyph. |
| `editView.nextGlyph` | M5 | handbook | yes | At the last glyph of the order Next Glyph does nothing (no wrap). |
| `editView.previousVisibleGlyph` | M5 | handbook | yes | For a current glyph that Font View does not list, Previous Glyph in Font View takes the last glyph listed; stepping from such a glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `editView.nextVisibleGlyph` | M5 | handbook | yes | For a current glyph that Font View does not list, Next Glyph in Font View takes the first glyph listed; stepping from such a glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `edit.addPlaceholder` | M5 | handbook | yes | A placeholder is written `/Placeholder` in display strings and copied to the plain-text clipboard as the text of the current glyph; no source shows either. |
| `edit.addPlaceholder` | M5 | handbook | yes | A placeholder goes in place of the selection, as a typed character does; adding one with text selected in the reference app settles it. |
| `editView.setWritingDirection` | M5 | handbook | yes | Vertical text in text mode draws no horizontal metric lines, since every column has its own; a vertical tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `textPreview.setStyle` | M5 | handbook | yes | Until instances are interpolated (M9) the style list holds the masters and the instances that sit at a master location. |
| `textPreview.setFontSize` | M5 | handbook | yes | A new preview draws at 36 points, and sizes from 4 to 1,000 points are allowed; the reference app's field would settle both. |
| `textPreview.togglePinned` | M5 | handbook | yes | The Text Preview is a window inside the app window; pinned, it stays in front of the other windows there, unpinned it goes behind the one clicked last. A separate pinned window in the reference app is the model. |
| `textPreview.setLayoutOrientation` | M5 | handbook | yes | Vertical text runs in columns from right to left, with the vertical widths and origins Edit View uses; the reference app on our own vertical font would settle it. |
| `textPreview.setWritingDirection` | M5 | handbook | yes | The default direction of the text comes from its first letter: right to left for Hebrew, Arabic and the other right-to-left scripts, else left to right. The reference follows the Unicode bidirectional algorithm; a mixed text in the reference app would settle how far we differ. |
| `textPreview.setWritingDirection` | M5 | handbook | yes | A selected range with the other direction is an embedding: its base direction changes, one level inside the text's, and its runs are ordered by it; it keeps its direction while text is typed around it; directions do nothing in vertical text. The reference app on a mixed text would settle it. |
| `textPreview.setWritingDirection` | M5 | handbook | yes | Digits, Arabic-Indic digits included, form left-to-right runs; a neutral between two runs of the same direction takes that direction, else the base direction of the text or its range; a combining mark follows the character before it. Full UAX #9 at M8 would settle the cases where this differs. |
| `textPreview.setWritingDirection` | M5 | handbook | yes | Digits count as left to right, so "12 34" in right-to-left text shows as "12 34" where full Unicode bidi gives "34 12"; M8's UAX #9 would settle it. |
| `textPreview.setWritingDirection` | M5 | handbook | yes | Line breaking measures a trailing blank with the kern it has before its level is reset to the paragraph's, so a line can break up to one kern early; full bidi in M8 would settle how the reference measures it. |
| `textPreview.find` | M5 | handbook | yes | The search options are Ignore Case (on at first) and Contains, Starts With or Full Word, as the system find bar of the reference offers them; matches do not overlap. |
| `textPreview.find` | M5 | handbook | yes | Replace changes the current match, All changes every match; a replacement that would take the text past 1,000,000 characters is refused. |
| `edit.selectSampleText` | M5 | handbook | yes | Choosing a sample string replaces the whole text of the tab; the handbook says the string goes into the text without saying where. |
| `edit.selectNextSampleString` | M5 | handbook | yes | Next and Previous wrap around at the ends of the list. |
| `edit.selectNextSampleString` | M5 | handbook | yes | Next and Previous step through the strings of all groups as one list, from the string chosen last in any tab; with none chosen yet, Next takes the first string and Previous the last. |
| `measure.ruler` | M3 | inferred | yes | Crossings follow the nonzero winding rule, and a line that only touches a contour does not cross it; the handbook does not say how the reference merges overlapping contours. |
| `measure.ruler` | M3 | inferred | yes | Components are measured like paths, and the ruler stays until the next click or tool change (03b question 10). |
| `view.setMeasurementLineHeight` | M5 | handbook | yes | The text-mode measurement line height is kept per tab and starts at half the x-height of the tab master; no source gives the default or where it is kept. |
| `view.setMeasurementLineHeight` | M5 | handbook | yes | A click or drag sets a whole-unit height, and Cmd+Ctrl+Alt only moves the line while it is shown (otherwise the click is an ordinary one); the reference app would settle both. |
| `annotation.add` | M3 | inferred | yes | New annotations get a text box 200 units wide, an arrow at 45° and a circle 100 units across; the handbook gives no defaults (03b question 11). |
| `glyph.addImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | A stored image path that starts with two separators (`//host/share`, `\\host\share`) is refused on every platform, not only on Windows: a document comes from anywhere, and on other systems such a path is either a network share or one whose meaning the system decides. Settled by opening, in the reference app on a Mac, a file whose image path starts with `//`. |
| `glyph.addImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | Two files that name the same glyph (A.png and A.jpg) refuse the call instead of one winning. Settled by Add Image with both files in the reference. |
| `glyph.addImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | A layer holds one placed image, as the version 3 format stores one: Add Image on a layer that has an image replaces it. Image shapes of the version 4 format are read, drawn and written back but not created. Settled by adding a second image to a glyph in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `image.setTransform` | M11 | handbook | yes | Width and height are the size of the whole image, so a crop does not change them. Settled by cropping an image in the reference and reading the Info box. |
| `image.setTransform` | M11 | handbook | yes | Width and height are the displayed size in font units; rotation turns the image about its position point (its bottom-left corner before rotation), as the stored `pos`, `scale` and `angle` suggest. Settled by rotating an image in the reference's Info box and reading the saved file. |
| `image.setCrop` | M11 | handbook | yes | The crop rectangle is in image pixels, measured from the image's bottom-left corner, as origin and size. Settled by cropping an image in the reference and reading `crop` in the saved file. |
| `image.setCropToLayerBounds` | M11 | handbook | yes | The layer bounds are the rectangle from 0 to the advance width and from the master's descender to its ascender. Settled by Set Crop to Layer Bounds on an image larger than the glyph in the reference. |
| `view.togglePreviewArea` | M5 | handbook | yes | Each tab opens and closes its own Preview Area, while all tabs share one height; a second tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `view.togglePreviewArea` | M5 | handbook | yes | The area is 140 points tall until its separator is dragged; the reference app's first height settles it. |
| `window.previewPanel` | M5 | handbook | yes | The Preview Panel keeps Invert, Flip, Blur, centring and offset of its own, apart from the Preview Area; the reference app settles it. |
| `preview.flip` | M5 | handbook | yes | Flip turns the strip half a circle, as a printed proof turned upside down, rather than mirroring it top to bottom; the reference app settles it. |
| `preview.setBlur` | M5 | handbook | yes | The blur amount runs from 0 to 10 screen points of blur radius; the handbook gives no range or unit. |
| `preview.alwaysCenterActiveGlyph` | M5 | handbook | yes | Off, a line wider than the strip sits as near to centring the edited glyph as it can without leaving a gap at either edge; the reference app with a long text settles it. |
| `preview.setOffset` | M5 | inferred | yes | The offset is in screen points, added to where the centring puts the line, and stays when the edited glyph changes; dragging in the reference app settles it. |
| `infoBox.showsColorLabels` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The split square sits at the left of the Info box and is drawn only when the glyph or the active layer has a colour label; the Info box itself shows only while something is selected. Settled by observation. |
| `background.snapping` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The background's nodes, then its handles, are snap targets after the edited layer's nodes and handles and before guides; its outlines between nodes are not targets. |
| `text.lineWidth` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Lines wrap at 10,000 units unless the setting says otherwise; no source gives the default. |
| `text.lineWidth` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A line that overflows breaks after its last space, else before the glyph that overflows, and a space may hang past the width; wrapped text in the reference app settles it. |
| `text.lineWidth` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In top-to-bottom text the lines are columns, placed from right to left one line height apart; a vertical tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `text.currentGlyphAfterCursor` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | At the end of the text or of a line the current glyph is the glyph before the cursor, and next to a placeholder or a missing character it stays what it was; moving the cursor there in the reference app settles it. |
| `text.clipboard` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph without a character is copied as `/name`, as display strings write it, and pasted text reads `/name` as that glyph, so a copy pastes back unchanged; copying unencoded glyphs from the reference app into a plain-text editor settles it. |
| `text.displayStrings` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | Changing a tab's text is not an undo step and does not mark the document modified; no source says, and the window's edited mark after typing in a tab of the reference app settles it. |
| `text.displayStrings` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | A `/` with an empty name is the slash character, and a glyph is written as the character it was typed as only while the font still maps that character to it; saving such a string in the reference app settles it. |
| `text.displayStrings` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | A `/name` the font lacks and a character no glyph encodes are kept in the text and take half the em with nothing drawn; opening such a string in the reference app settles it. |
| `text.displayStrings` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | A stored string over the 10,000-item tab limit opens cut to its first 10,000 items, with a warning in the log and the status bar, and a string that cannot be read (a `/name` longer than a glyph name may be) opens no tab; a file from the reference app with such a string settles it. |
| `text.displayStrings` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | Opening a document adds its tabs after the open ones and leaves Font View showing; opening a file with display strings in the reference app settles which tab shows. |
| `text.findUnicodeMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A Unicode term matches when a code point's hexadecimal form contains it, so `228` finds U+228E; whether the reference needs the whole code point would settle it. |
| `textPreview.fallbackFont` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Characters the font lacks are drawn in the interface font of the app. |
| `textPreview.fallbackFont` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Each character the font lacks takes half an em, the advance the Edit View layout gives a missing character, whatever its width in the interface font. |
| `text.selectionTargets` (behavior) | M5 | inferred | – | Glyph commands in text mode take the glyphs of the text selection in the tab's master, as Font View's batch commands take the selected glyphs; the handbook does not say, and the reference app with a selected text would settle it. |
| `image.supportedFormats` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | PDF and PSD images are kept as references and drawn as an unsupported-image marker with a message, not decoded. Settled by the owner's answer to M11 § 7 question 3. |
| `image.storedAsRelativePath` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A file outside the document's folder is stored with a path that climbs out of it (`../scans/A.png`), as the handbook says only a relative path is stored. Settled by adding an image from another folder in the reference and reading the saved path. |
| `image.storedAsRelativePath` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An image added to a document that has never been saved keeps its absolute path until the first save, which rewrites it relative to the new file. Settled by adding an image to an untitled document in the reference and saving it. |
| `image.storedAsRelativePath` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A stored path that names a network share or a device (`\\host\share`, `//host/share`, `\\?\`, `\\.\`) is never read, on every platform, and the image counts as unreadable; absolute paths and paths climbing out with `..` are read. Settled by opening, in the reference, a file whose image path names a network share. |
| `image.defaultPlacement` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The image's size in points comes from the resolution stored in the file (PNG pHYs, JPEG JFIF or Exif, TIFF resolution tags); a file without one counts as 72 pixels per inch, one pixel per unit. Settled by adding a 144 dpi PNG in the reference. |
| `text.shaping` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Only the features active in the Features menu apply, with no default features, so Arabic text shows its positional forms only when init, medi and fina are on; typing Arabic in an Edit View tab of the reference app with no feature active would settle it. |
| `text.shaping` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A glyph typed by name is shaped as a private-use character that maps to that glyph, so substitutions apply to it as to typed characters; typing /a.sc next to a feature that substitutes it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `text.shaping` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Kerning is applied by the tab's kerning mode to the glyphs after substitution, and the compiled kern feature is not used in Edit View; a font whose hand-written kern feature has a contextual rule would settle it. |
| `textPreview.shaping` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The preview's font is rebuilt from the current feature code, kerning and anchors after edits, once editing pauses for half a second, without pressing Compile; editing a feature without compiling while the reference app's Text Preview is open would settle it. |
| `preview.singleLine` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | On the preview line a line break takes no room; a tab of several lines in the reference app's Preview Area settles it. |
| `preview.singleLine` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Vertical text previews on a horizontal line, and right-to-left text from the right; the reference app with a vertical tab settles it. |
| `preview.respectsInstanceData` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | The preview of an instance applies the instance's interpolation parameters (Local Interpolation, InterpolationWeightY, manual interpolation), Rename Glyphs and Remove Glyphs, and no filters; settle by previewing an instance with a filter parameter in the reference app. |
| `textPreview.exportedFonts` (behavior) | M14 | inferred | – | A watched file is read again when its modification time changes, at most once a second; a user report of missed or slow updates would settle it. |
| `editView.setLocalizedForms` | M8 | handbook | no | The menu offers the script and language pairs that the font's enabled locl entries name in their `script` and `language` statements, in order of appearance; the menu's entries in the reference app would settle it. |
| `textPreview.showExportedFonts` | M14 | inferred | no | The switch is a pop-up in the Text Preview window with no menu item; nothing in the reference app settles a place. |
| `infoBox.glyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | With several glyphs selected in the text, the Info box describes the glyph after the cursor only, and the Unicode value is shown, not edited. |
| `infoBox.glyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | At each outer edge the kern field sits on the sidebearing row and the group field with its lock on the width row; a screenshot of the reference app's Info box in text mode would settle it. |
| `infoBox.glyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The preceding glyph's box sits beside the glyph box, on the side where the preceding glyph is drawn; the reference app with the Text tool would settle it. |
| `editView.contextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The swatch row opens the glyph section of the menu, under a Glyph Color caption (Layer Color while Alt is held). Settled by observation. |
| `editView.contextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The glyph section offers Exports, Locked and Show Glyph in Font View, and the menu ends with Copy Glyph Names, as the Glyphs 3 video lists them; the other entries of that list follow with their milestones. Settled by observation. |
| `window.textPreview` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The text is edited in a plain field above the area that draws it in the font, since FontCraft has no text field that draws in the font being edited; the field edits texts of up to 100,000 characters, longer ones change through Set Preview Text and Find and Replace. The reference field both edits and draws; a FontCraft field that draws in the font would settle it. |
| `window.textPreview` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The style pop-up starts on the master of the showing Edit View tab and follows it until a style is picked; the reference app's first choice would settle it. |
| `window.textPreview` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Find opens with Cmd+F while the pointer is over the window and with the window's Find button, because Edit › Find… with Cmd+F belongs to the Edit View Find command; a focused Text Preview in the reference app would settle it. |
| `editView.sampleStrings` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Up and Down put the previous or next string into the text at once and keep the window open; a click closes it unless it is pinned; Return and Escape close it. |
| `text` (tool) | M5 | handbook | – | Return types a line break; the handbook allows several lines but does not name the key. |
| `text` (tool) | M5 | handbook | – | Up and Down move to the nearest place on the line above or below, and to the start or the end of the text from the first or the last line; in right-to-left text Left steps forward in the text; in top-to-bottom text Up and Down step along the column and Left and Right move between columns. Moving the cursor in such texts in the reference app settles it. |
| `text` (tool) | M5 | handbook | – | Backspace and Delete with Alt or Cmd do nothing (no word or line deletion); no source names them. |
| `measurement` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | The ruler's ends snap unless Ctrl is held, and Shift keeps it horizontal or vertical, whichever is nearer. |
| `measurement` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | The ruler stays until the next press, Escape or a tool change (03b question 9). |
| `measurement` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | G with any modifiers adds the measurement guide. |
| `annotation` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | Each annotation sub-tool is a tool of its own, and A returns to the sub-tool used last (Annotation Selection before any). |
| `annotation` (tool) | M3 | handbook | – | Handles show and work on selected annotations only. |

## `04-palette.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dimensions.getValues` | M3 | inferred | yes | The field keys per script come from the corpus files (Latin: HH, HV, OH, OV, nV, nd, oH, oV, tH; Arabic: arAlef, arBar); the reference does not document them. |
| `fitCurve.setHandleLength` | M3 | inferred | yes | A handle's percentage is its length over the distance from its node to the intersection of the two handles, or the chord when the handles are parallel (Palette question 2). |
| `fitCurve.increase` | M3 | inferred | yes | Plus moves to the next of the eight steps above the segment's mean percentage; the handbook gives no increment. |
| `fitCurve.decrease` | M3 | inferred | yes | Minus moves to the step below the segment's mean percentage; the handbook gives no increment. |
| `fitCurve.balanceHandles` | M3 | inferred | yes | Balance sets both handles to their mean percentage; the handbook gives no rule. |
| `layers.toggleVisibility` | M3 | handbook | yes | Visibility is stored with the layer and saved (Palette question 3). |
| `layers.addBackupLayer` | M3 | handbook | yes | Plus copies the active layer, and the backup is named by the date and time in UTC (Palette question 3). |
| `layers.useAsMaster` | M3 | handbook | yes | Use as Master takes backup layers only (Palette question 4). |
| `layers.list` | M3 | inferred | yes | Rows are ordered by master in master order, each master layer followed by that master's other layers in file order. |
| `transform.setOrigin` | M3 | handbook | yes | The metrics-point origin sits at half the advance width for every operation, not only mirroring (Palette question 5). |
| `transform.slantLeft` | M3 | handbook | yes | Slant is a shear by the tangent of the angle about the origin (Palette question 5). |
| `transform.slantDown` | M3 | inferred | yes | What lies right of the transformation origin moves down; the handbook does not say which side moves. |
| `transform.slantUp` | M3 | inferred | yes | What lies right of the transformation origin moves up; the handbook does not say which side moves. |
| `transform.alignLeft` | M3 | handbook | yes | Aligning a single object or point changes nothing and says so (Palette question 7). |
| `transform.booleanUnion` | M3 | handbook | yes | Open paths stay as they are, unlike Remove Overlap, and components are not changed (Palette question 8). |
| `transform.booleanSubtract` | M3 | handbook | yes | The frontmost path is the last closed path in shape order (Palette question 8). |
| `transform.booleanIntersect` | M3 | handbook | yes | The frontmost path is the last closed path in shape order (Palette question 8). |
| `transform.getSettings` | M3 | inferred | yes | The starting values are ours: the box centre, 110 % with the lock closed, 90° and 10°. |
| `transform.setSettings` | M3 | inferred | yes | The defaults, scale 110 % with the lock closed, rotation 90° and slant 10°, are ours; the tutorial video named in palette.transformations shows the second scale field greyed, which suggests a closed lock. |
| `transform.setSettings` | M3 | inferred | yes | The limits are ours: a scale is non-zero and within ±1000000 %, a rotation within ±3600°, a slant strictly between -90° and 90°. |
| `dimensions.perMaster` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Values are stored under `GSDimensionPlugin.Dimensions` in the font's user data, one dictionary per master id, the key the corpus files use (Palette question 1). |
| `fitCurve.percentRange` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Without the `Fit Curve Panel Settings` parameter the range is 48 to 74 %, the handbook's example, and values beyond the range are clamped to it (Palette question 2). |
| `transform.oppositeButtonsExact` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Opposite buttons are exact for mirroring and quarter turns, through exact sines and cosines and grid rounding; other angles are undone only up to grid rounding (Palette question 5). |

## `05-font-view.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fontView.setViewMode` | M4 | handbook | yes | The selection, the sidebar entries, the search and the sort carry over when the mode changes. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.setCellSize` | M4 | handbook | yes | Cells run from 16 to 512 points on a logarithmic slider, 64 by default. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.sortByColumn` | M4 | handbook | yes | Glyphs without a value in the sort column go last in both directions, and ties keep glyph order. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.sortByColumn` | M4 | handbook | yes | The sort belongs to the Font View tab and is not saved with the document. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.setColumns` | M4 | handbook | yes | At least one column stays shown; the header's context menu lists every column with a check mark. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.toggleInspector` | M4 | handbook | yes | The inspector is shown when a document opens. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.toggleInspectorDetails` | M4 | handbook | yes | The extra rows start collapsed. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.toggleMetricsOrientation` | M5 | handbook | yes | The orientation is a panel setting kept across documents and sessions, not stored per font; watching the reference app reopen a file would settle it. |
| `fontView.toggleMetricsOrientation` | M5 | handbook | yes | The switch shows the icon of the set on show (left to right, or top to bottom); a screenshot of the reference inspector would settle it. |
| `fontView.getState` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; there is no reference behaviour to settle. |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | A comma is not a separator: an item containing one is an invalid name and is reported. Settled by observation (05 question 7). |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | A range covers every code point from the first endpoint's to the second's; code points Unicode does not assign, and surrogates, are skipped, and one range holds at most 65,536 code points. Settled by observation (05 question 7). |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | An invalid item is reported in `skipped` and the valid items are still added. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | Replace puts a fresh empty glyph with database properties in the old glyph's place in the glyph order; kerning and other references by name stay. Settled by observation (05 question 8). |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | A range whose second endpoint comes before its first is reported in `skipped`, not reversed. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | With Use Custom Naming on, a typed character and the code points of a range are named uniXXXX or uXXXXX, as typed code-point names are kept. Settled by observing a font with the option on. |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | New glyphs that land between the same two existing glyphs are in sort-key order; the glyphs already in the font are never moved. Settled by observation (05 question 2). |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | Headless calls default to Keep Existing (05-font-view.md § 3.1); the dialog first calls with ask, which changes nothing when a name exists and returns the existing names. Our design; nothing to settle. |
| `glyph.newGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | When newGlyph is taken the new glyph is named newGlyph.001, newGlyph.002 and so on, the rule documented for duplicated and pasted glyphs. Settled by observation (05 question 6). |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | Each copy is placed directly after its original in the glyph order. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | A copy of a suffixed glyph gets one more suffix (A.ss01 gives A.ss01.001). Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | The copy keeps everything but the code points, so the original stays the glyph for its characters. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | The `suffix` parameter names the copy A.<suffix> (A.<suffix>.001 when taken), for scripts that want a feature suffix in one step. Our addition; nothing to settle. |
| `glyph.removeGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | Decompose replaces each component of a removed glyph with that glyph's shapes from the matching layer; components inside it that point at glyphs that stay remain components. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.removeGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | A metrics key points at a glyph when its formula starts with that glyph's name (=H, =\|H, =O-4); the longest leading name that is a glyph wins, since names may contain hyphens. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | Stored overrides of category, subcategory, script, production name, case and direction are dropped, so those properties follow the database again; the sort name, tags, colour labels, note and export flag stay. Settled by observing a glyph with a custom category before and after the command (05 question 5). |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | With Use Custom Naming on, names are kept and only the other properties are updated. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | A rename the command makes goes through glyph.rename, so references follow it; a new name that is already taken (by any glyph of the font before the command, or by an earlier glyph of the batch) leaves the glyph's name and Unicode values unchanged and is reported. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | The Unicode values follow the name whether or not it changed: the glyph data's code point for it, none for a name it knows without one (A.alt), and the stored values for a name it does not know. Settled by observation. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | The table adds a Description column (ours). Settled by observing the window. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | With an empty search field the table lists the whole database in the sorted order, up to the search's limit of 10,000 rows (the CJK ideographs past it are reached by searching). Settled by observing the window when it opens. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | Sorting is ascending only; a second click on the sorted header keeps the order. Settled by observing a second header click. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | Shift+click selects the rows from the last clicked one; Cmd+click adds or removes one row. Settled by observing the window. |
| `glyphInfo.addToFont` | M4 | handbook | yes | Names the font already has are left as they are and listed in `existing`; no Replace question is asked. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.listMissingGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | A Categories entry expects the glyphs that the languages of the listed scripts need and that the glyph data puts in that category, subcategory or case group; without language data it has no expected set. Settled by observing the badge of a category in a font with one language list shown. |
| `fontView.generateMissingGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | Generated glyphs are empty, as wide as the Default Layer Width parameter or the built-in width makes a new glyph (as New Glyph), take the code point the entry gives or the glyph data gives their name, and are placed by the default sort key; all of them are one undo step. Settled by observing Generate on a list filter. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | Selected Glyphs pairs the copied glyphs, in copy order, with the selected targets in glyph order. Settled by observation (05 question 18). |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | Content of Active Layer writes into the target's layer for the master Font View shows. Settled by observation (05 question 18). |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | Copied masters are matched to the target's by id, then by name, then by position; layers of an unmatched master are not pasted. Settled by pasting between fonts with different masters. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | All Data keeps the target's name and code points. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | In Edit View's text mode Paste Special (menu item or the paste key with Option) is refused with a message instead of typing the clipboard's text; the Edit menu of the running reference app in text mode settles it. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | LSB moves the outline and keeps the right side bearing; RSB sets the width from the outline's right edge. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | As Components adds each component at the origin, after the shapes already there, and leaves out a component that would make a glyph refer to itself. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.infoForSelection` | M4 | handbook | yes | The menu item opens the window and does not toggle it; `open: false` closes it. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.infoForSelection` | M4 | handbook | yes | In Edit View the window describes the edited glyph. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.getInfo` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; there is no reference behaviour to settle. |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | When the base name changes, the Unicode values become the glyph data's for the new name whenever the data knows the name, directly or through the suffix, ligature or code-point rules, so renaming B to A.alt clears them; a name the data does not know keeps the stored values. Settled by observation (05 question 4). |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | Rename updates the font's references by name: components (also in backgrounds), kerning pairs, UFO groups, the glyphOrder parameter of the font and its instances, and metrics keys of the glyph and its layers. Feature code, kerning contexts, other custom parameters, hints and display strings are not rewritten; the result lists where the old name is still used. Settled by observation (05 question 9). |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | A typed production, alternative or code-point name becomes the nice name (as for a new glyph), unless the font uses custom naming. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | A name that breaks the naming rules is refused, as Find and Replace refuses one. Settled by observing whether the reference accepts an illegal name typed in Font View. |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | When a locked glyph uses the renamed glyph as a component, the rename is refused, because a locked glyph refuses changes to its layers. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setCase` | M4 | inferred | yes | Case is edited in the Selection Info window and in List View's Case column; the handbook names no control for it. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `glyph.setDirection` | M4 | inferred | yes | Writing direction is edited in the Selection Info window and in List View's Direction column; the handbook names no control for it. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `glyph.setDirection` | M4 | inferred | yes | The vertical directions VTR and VTL, which source files may store, are accepted too. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setColorLabel` | M4 | handbook | yes | Label names are matched without case and spaces, and grey is accepted for gray (lightGreen, Light Green and lightgreen are the same). |
| `glyph.setLayerColorLabel` | M4 | handbook | yes | Locked glyphs, and glyphs without the layer, are skipped and listed, because a locked glyph refuses changes to its layers. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setTags` | M4 | handbook | yes | A tag is trimmed, and an empty tag or a repeated one is dropped. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.removeTag` | M4 | inferred | yes | Deleting a capsule in the Tags field removes that tag from every selected glyph. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setSortName` | M4 | handbook | yes | Null clears both stored sort names: the one used with Keep Alternates Next to Base Glyph on and the one used with it off. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setComponents` | M6 | handbook | yes | The cell lists component names separated by a comma and a space; a name added to the list appends a component at offset zero (aligned automatically when the layer allows it), a name removed deletes the last component with that name, and only the layer of the master shown in Font View changes; editing the cell in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.setComponents` | M6 | handbook | yes | Removing a locked component through the list is refused with a message naming it, and nothing changes; the layer's hints follow their shapes to their new places and a hint on a removed component goes with it; removing a locked component's name from the cell in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.copyGlyphNames` | M4 | handbook | yes | Names come in glyph order (the order Font View shows), not in the order they were selected. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.copyGlyphNames` | M4 | handbook | yes | One per Line and Python List put no line break after the last line; code points are written in uppercase hex. Settled by pasting the reference's output into a text editor. |
| `glyphData.lookup` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; its answers must equal what the reference shows in Glyph Info and the inspector for the same name, which the oracles' copies of the data (fontc, glyphsLib) check. |
| `glyphData.lookup` | M4 | inferred | yes | A ligature without a record takes the script that all its known parts share, and no case. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.search` | M4 | inferred | yes | A query matches as a case-insensitive substring of the name, the description, the hex code point, or equals the character. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.search` | M4 | inferred | yes | Results are sorted by name unless another field is asked for, and at most 10,000 are returned. An empty query matches every record. |
| `glyphData.productionName` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; its answers must equal the names the oracles write (fontc and fontmake with production names on). |
| `font.validateGlyphNames` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; it applies the handbook's naming rules and also lists names used by more than one glyph. There is no reference behaviour to settle. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | A batch that would give two glyphs the same name, or any glyph an invalid name, is refused as a whole and the clashes are listed. Each rename follows glyph.rename. Settled by observation (05 question 9). |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | The results are not converted to nice names: Find and Replace gives the names it computes. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | In Regex mode a group reference is a backslash and one digit (`\0` is the whole match, `\10` is group 1 followed by 0), `\\` is a backslash, and `$` is plain text. Settled by renaming with a two-digit group reference and a `$` in the reference app. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | Matching is case-sensitive unless `matchCase` is false, in plain and Regex mode alike; with it false, plain Find is still plain text. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | An empty Find appends Replace in Regex mode as in plain mode. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.setSearch` | M4 | handbook | yes | Glyphs that a new search hides are deselected, so batch commands never act on glyphs the view does not show. Settled by observing whether the reference keeps hidden glyphs selected after a search. |
| `fontView.setSearch` | M4 | handbook | yes | A term the matcher refuses (a regular expression that does not compile yet) is refused and the view stays as it was; the field keeps the typed text while it has the keyboard. Settled by typing an unfinished regular expression in the reference's field. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | Shift-click selects every entry listed between the last entry selected in the clicked entry's section and the clicked one, in any section (the handbook names filters only), and keeps the other sections' entries. Settled by Shift-clicking categories and languages in the reference. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | Without a selected entry in its section, a Shift-clicked entry is added as Cmd-click adds it. Settled by Shift-clicking an entry of an unused section in the reference. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | Glyphs that a new sidebar selection hides are deselected. Settled by observing whether the reference keeps hidden glyphs selected after choosing a sidebar entry. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | A selected entry that disappears (a removed or renamed filter, a category with no glyph left) is deselected. Settled by removing a selected filter in the reference. |
| `fontView.queryGlyphs` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; it must give the glyphs the sidebar and search would show for the same entries and text. |
| `fontView.addSmartFilter` | M4 | handbook | yes | Headless, a filter added without rules gets one rule every glyph matches (Glyph Name contains an empty text), standing for the editor's single empty rule. Settled by observing what an untouched new smart filter shows. |
| `path.roundCoordinates` | M3 | handbook | yes | A font whose grid is off (spacing 0) rounds to whole units. |
| `path.roundCoordinatesForAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | The label "… for All Masters" is ours; the handbook describes the Option key but not the item's label (03a question 18). |
| `path.removeOverlapForAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | The label "… for All Masters" is ours; the handbook describes the Option key but not the item's label (03a question 18). |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyphForAllMasters` | M6 | handbook | yes | The shortcut is Cmd+Alt+Shift+D, Decompose with Option added as for the other Option variants; the Glyph menu of the reference app with Option held settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyphForAllMasters` | M6 | handbook | yes | Only master layers are decomposed; intermediate and alternate layers keep their components. A composite with a brace layer in the reference app settles it. |
| `path.convertToQuadratic` | M3 | handbook | yes | New off-curve points are rounded to the grid. |
| `path.convertToQuadratic` | M3 | handbook | yes | With allMasters, master layers with the same node structure are converted together (one spline length for all), so they stay compatible. |
| `fontView.cell.followsMaster` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Font View's master is its own: choosing a master in a tab does not change it, and a tab opened from Font View starts in it. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.contentBySize` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Names and Unicode markers show from a cell size of 64 points up. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.unicodeIndicator` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | CJK glyphs are those whose first code point is in the Han, Hiragana, Katakana, Hangul or Bopomofo script; the character is drawn with the interface font, and the marker is drawn when that font lacks it. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.unicodeIndicator` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The marker is a small grey square. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeMetricsOutOfSync` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Any layer the out-of-sync scan covers counts, not only the layer of the master Font View shows; a font whose second master alone is out of sync, viewed on the first master in the reference app, would settle it. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeMetricsOutOfSync` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Unlinked keys and keys that cannot be evaluated give no triangle; a key naming a missing glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeAnnotation` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Drawn in the top-left corner; the handbook gives no position. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `fontView.cell.badgeCustomInfo` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Drawn in the bottom-left corner; the handbook gives no position. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `fontView.cell.badgeCustomInfo` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A glyph counts as custom when it stores a category, subcategory, script, production name, case, direction or sort name that differs from the database's, or Unicode values that differ from the database's for its name. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeNonExporting` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Drawn at the top centre of the cell; the handbook gives no position. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `fontView.cell.colorLayerOnly` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The cut-out is a triangle over 30% of the side at the top-left corner. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.column.char` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A character the interface font lacks is drawn with the glyph's own outline from the document, at row height in the text colour, instead of a system font's. Settled by observing the reference with a code point that the system fonts lack. |
| `fontView.column.components` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The cell shows and edits the layer of the master Font View shows, not the first master's; editing the cell with a second master selected in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontView.column.otherProperties` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Every column is shown by default, in this order: ID, Char, Name, Unicode, Production Name, Script, Category, Subcategory, Case, Direction, Exports, Color, Tags, Sort Name, Note, Components, Last Changed, LSB, RSB, Width, Left Group, Right Group. Settled by observation (05 question 14). |
| `fontView.column.otherProperties` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Besides the four cells the handbook calls editable (Name, Unicode, Note, Components), cells of properties that have a set command (Exports, colour, tags, production name, script, category, subcategory, case, direction, sort name) are editable too. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.mixedExport` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A click on the mixed box makes every selected glyph export. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A field shows the metrics key that governs it (the layer's own key first, written with ==), else the measured value with up to two decimals; a screenshot of the reference inspector on a glyph with a key would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The orientation icon sits after the first field; the vertical set puts TSB and BSB on the first row and vertical origin and vertical width on a second row; a screenshot of the reference inspector would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A field left empty commits nothing; trying it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The vertical origin shows 0 and the vertical width shows ascender minus descender when the layer stores none; the reference inspector on a new glyph would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.tagsField` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Leaving the Tags field also commits the typed text as a tag; Escape drops it. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.tagsField` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Each capsule has a cross that removes the tag from every selected glyph (glyph.removeTag). Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.tagsField` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Several tags pasted at once with commas are added one command each. Settled by observation. |
| `addGlyphs.recipe` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A recipe that names a glyph the font does not have adds nothing and is listed in `skipped` with the missing names; trying a recipe with a missing part in the reference app settles it. |
| `addGlyphs.recipe` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A recipe for a glyph the font already has follows the same Keep, Replace and Cancel answers as a plain name, and Replace rebuilds the glyph from the recipe, keeping its code points; a recipe for an existing glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `addGlyphs.recipe` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The name after `=` is kept as typed, without turning a legacy name into a nice name, and gets the code point the glyph data gives it unless another glyph has it; a recipe ending in `=uni00E9` in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontView.sidebar.missingBadge` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The badge counts expected glyphs present by name or by code point (a glyph with the character counts even under another name). Settled by observing a badge for a font that names a glyph differently. |
| `fontView.paste.glyphs` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Pasted glyphs take their place in the default sort order, like added glyphs. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.paste.nameClashSuffix` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A renamed pasted glyph has no code points, so the existing glyph stays the one for its characters. Settled by observation. |
| `removeGlyph.usedAsComponent` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Decompose reverses each path that a mirroring component transform (negative determinant) places, as export decomposition does, so the outline keeps its direction. Settled by decomposing a mirrored component in the reference app and checking the path direction. |
| `glyphData.caseAndDirectionEncoding` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | A script is right to left when most of its letters with a code point are strong right-to-left characters in Unicode 17.0: adlam, arabic, avestan, chorasmian, elymaic, hebrew, mandaic, nko, rovas, samaritan, syriac, thaana and yezidi. A glyph with neither a stated direction nor a script is right to left when the character its name stands for is. Settled by observing the reference's directions. |
| `glyphData.legacyUnicode` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | Adding by a legacy character (typing U+FB04) finds the record through unicodeLegacy and creates f_f_l without a code point. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.lookupOrder` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | When two built-in records share a code point or a production name, the first by name wins; the pinned data has no such pair. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.customFile` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A custom record replaces the built-in record of the same name as a whole: fields it leaves out are empty, not taken from the built-in record. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.customFile` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A file over a limit (16 MB, 200,000 records, 1,024 bytes per attribute value, two levels of elements) or with an entity declaration or a malformed record is rejected as a whole; attributes the format does not define are skipped with a warning. |
| `glyphData.customFile` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Beside a source file and in the Info folder beside it, every file named GlyphData*.xml is read, in name order, those beside the file first; at most 32 files of the user's and 32 of a document's. Settled by observation. |
| `export.productionNameLigature` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | A ligature part whose production name has a dot is joined as fontc joins it (bh_a-telugu → uni0C2D.half0C05), not replaced by the cleaned glyph name as glyphsLib does. Settled by exporting such a ligature from the reference app. |
| `glyphName.firstCharacter` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | .null is exempt like .notdef. |
| `glyphName.invalidBlocksExport` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Only glyphs that export are checked: a glyph that is not written to the font cannot block its export. Settled by exporting a font whose non-exporting glyph has an invalid name. |
| `glyphName.invalidBlocksExport` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The glyph's name is checked, not a production name it stores; a stored production name with bad characters is cleaned at export like any other. Settled by exporting a font with such a production name. |
| `glyphName.clickToEdit` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A click on the name of the glyph that is already the only one selected starts editing; a click on another glyph's name selects it. Return or a click elsewhere commits through glyph.rename, Escape cancels. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.invertSelection` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Invert Selection works among the visible glyphs, as Select All does. Settled by observation (05 question 12). |
