| `fontView.batch.addComponent` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | When the component cannot go into one selected glyph (it would contain itself), the whole batch is refused and no glyph changes; adding a glyph to a selection that includes it in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontView.batch.palette` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Fit Curve applies each glyph's remembered node indices to its layer of Font View's master; glyphs never edited, or whose nodes are gone, are skipped; the run is one undo step. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.search.fields` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A plain term matches names and notes by substring; it matches a code point when it is four to six hexadecimal digits (optionally after U+ or 0x) equal to it, and a character when it is that one character. Shorter hexadecimal terms such as FE only match names, so a term like a does not also find every code point containing A. Settled by observing a search for 00FE and for FE. |
| `fontView.search.regex` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | With Regex on, the pattern must match a whole field (name, hex code point or note) among the fields the magnifier menu selects; the handbook's examples only show names. Settled by observation (05 question 13). |
| `fontView.sidebar.categoryFilter` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A category lists case groups (Uppercase, Lowercase, Smallcaps, Minor: the glyphs with that case, or with the subcategory older files store under the same name) and then its subcategories, each only when the font has such glyphs or the shown languages expect some. Settled by observing the expanded Letter entry. |
| `fontView.sidebar.scriptAutoShown` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The default short list is latin, greek and cyrillic. Settled by observing the Languages section of a new user. |
| `smartFilter.rule.glyphName` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Text rules (Glyph Name, Tags, Script) offer is, is not, contains, does not contain, begins with, ends with and matches (a regular expression); number rules offer =, ≠, <, ≤, >, ≥; yes-or-no and choice rules (Case, Category & Subcategory, the colour labels, Custom) offer is and is not. The reference's stored predicates use ==, !=, <, >, CONTAINS, IN, BEGINSWITH and ENDSWITH (tutorial "Custom sidebar entries in Font View"), which this set covers. Settled by observing the rule editor (05 question 10). |
| `smartFilter.rule.categoryAndSubcategory` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The rule is a choice (is, is not) of a category or a category and subcategory, written Letter/Uppercase; the case groups Uppercase, Lowercase, Smallcaps and Minor also match glyphs by case, as data that stores case instead of those subcategories does. Settled by observing the rule's value menu. |
| `smartFilter.rule.case` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The rule offers all five case values, minor included, as the Case property has five; the rule's own description lists four. Settled by observation (05 question 10). |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasAnchors` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Only the layer's own anchors count, not those it gets from its components; filtering a composite without anchors of its own in the reference app settles it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.containsComponent` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The glyph name is compared whole, not as a substring; trying a partial name in the reference app settles it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasMetricsKeys` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A metrics key stored on one layer only (a local key) makes the rule true for the glyph; a glyph with a layer-local key in the reference app would settle it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.metricsKeysOutOfSync` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Keys that name a missing glyph or cannot be evaluated do not count as out of sync, and a key out of sync on any master counts; the reference app on a glyph with an unlinked key and on one out of sync in one master only would settle it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasKerningGroups` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A vertical kerning group (top or bottom) alone makes the rule true, as a left or right group does; the reference app on a glyph with only a vertical group would settle it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.colorLabel` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A glyph whose label is an RGBA colour, not one of the twelve, counts as set but equals none of the twelve. Settled by observing a file with an RGBA label. |
| `smartFilter.rule.custom` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The language adds matches (a regular expression over the whole value), which fea-rs refuses, because the Glyph Name rule offers matches and compiles to this language; && and \|\| stand for AND and OR, and a yes-or-no attribute alone means == true. A comparison on tags holds when it holds for one tag. Settled by trying these forms in the reference's Custom rule. |
| `smartFilter.live` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Results are recomputed from the current font whenever the filter is shown and after every edit, for the master Font View shows. Settled by observation (05 question 11). |
| `listFilter.order` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | When several filters are selected and one is a list filter, the result follows the first selected list filter; a folder shows glyph order even when it holds list filters. Settled by observing a list filter combined with a smart filter and with a folder. |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | An opened file keeps its stored glyph order, which is the order the oracles compile; only glyphs added later, renamed, or given a sort name move to the place the sort key gives. Settled by observing where the reference puts a new glyph (05 question 2). |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The published data has no sort names, so the key is ours: category in the order Letter, Number, Punctuation, Symbol, Separator, Mark, then glyphs without one; within a category the base glyph's first code point, then the name; suffixed glyphs after the run of base glyphs, or right after their base with Keep Alternates Next to Base Glyph on. Settled by observation (05 question 2). |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A sort name (from custom glyph data or the glyph itself) places the glyph after the others of its category, ordered by the sort name. |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | In a stored order that is not sorted by the key, a new glyph goes right after the last glyph whose key is not greater than its own (first when there is none), so it follows the glyphs it would follow in a sorted font. Settled by observing where the reference puts a new glyph in a hand-ordered font. |
| `fontView.image.batchByFileName` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Files with no glyph of their name are skipped and listed in the result; no glyph is created. Settled by Add Image with an unmatched file in the reference. |
| `fontView.image.caseSensitivity` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | File names are matched to glyph names exactly, case included, on every platform. Settled by Add Image with `a.png` in a font with `A` and `a` in the reference. |
| `glyphDataEditor.show` | M14 | inferred | no | The editor opens from the Window menu with no shortcut; the companion tool is a separate application, so nothing settles a menu place. |
| `fontView.grid` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Shift+click adds every visible glyph from the last clicked glyph to the clicked one; Cmd+click adds or removes one glyph; a click on empty space clears the selection. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.grid` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Dragging across the grid draws a rectangle that selects the cells it touches, added to the selection while Shift or Cmd is held. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.grid` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Section headers of the glyphOrder parameter show only while the view lists every glyph in glyph order (no filter, search or sort). Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.list` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A click on an editable cell of the only selected row, or a double-click on it, starts editing; a click on an Exports cell flips the flag; a double-click on a cell that cannot be edited opens the glyph. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.list` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Values that come from the glyph data rather than from the glyph are drawn dimmed. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | With several glyphs selected the Name field is read-only and shows Multiple Values, since glyph names are unique. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The metric and kerning group fields are shown read-only until their commands exist (M5). Settled by M5. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A Unicode value typed with several glyphs selected goes to all of them, as for any other field. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The orientation icon between the metric fields is left out until M5 (fontView.toggleMetricsOrientation). Settled by M5. |
| `window.selectionInfo` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Turning on the sort name check box only enables the field; the field's value is stored when it is committed. Turning it off removes the stored sort name (glyph.setSortName with null). Settled by observation. |
| `window.selectionInfo` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The window is not modal and follows the selection while it is open. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A right-click on a glyph that is not selected selects it alone first; on a selected glyph the menu acts on the whole selection. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The items come in this order: the colour swatches under a caption (Glyph Color, or Layer Color with Alt), then Tags, then Copy Glyph Names. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | With Alt held the swatches set the layer colour of Font View's master. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.sidebar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The sections start open; categories, scripts, folders and groups start closed. Settled by opening a new document in the reference. |
| `fontView.sidebar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The actions menu sits at the foot of the sidebar, below the entries. Settled by a screenshot of the reference's Font View. |
| `fontView.sidebar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A script entry shows the Unicode Script property value its glyph-data id stands for (`Latin`, `Tibetan`, `Canadian Aboriginal`), not the id; paths and parameters keep the id. Settled by a screenshot of the reference's Languages section. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A language requires its CLDR main exemplar characters only; the auxiliary, index, numbers and punctuation sets are not counted. Settled by comparing a language's badge total in the reference with its CLDR sets. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | CLDR lists letters in lowercase, so each character's single-character uppercase form is added (ß gets none; Georgian gets no Mtavruli capitals). Settled by observing whether the reference's badge for a Latin-script language counts capitals. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A language is listed under the glyph database's script that most of its exemplar characters belong to, ignoring Common and Inherited characters (Japanese falls under han, Korean under hangul). Settled by observing where the reference lists Japanese and other mixed-script languages. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Every CLDR locale with its own main exemplar set is a language entry, regional and script variants included (de-CH, sr-Latn); those that inherit their parent's set are left out. Settled by observing which entries the reference lists under one script. |
| `fontView.searchField` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The magnifier menu offers All Fields, Name Only, Unicode Value Only and Note Only as one choice, then Match Case and Regex. Settled by opening the reference's magnifier menu. |
| `fontView.statusBar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The counts read "N selected · N shown · N in font". Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.addGlyphsExisting` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The buttons sit Cancel, Replace, Keep Existing from left to right, Keep Existing the default (Return). Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.missingGlyphs` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Every missing glyph starts chosen, so Generate right away adds them all. Settled by opening the reference's list and pressing Generate. |
| `dialog.pasteSpecial` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The dialog opens on Glyphs With Same Name with All Data ticked. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.removeGlyph` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Decompose is the default button because it keeps other glyphs' outlines; observing the reference dialog would settle it. |
| `dialog.removeGlyph` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | With components to answer, the buttons sit Cancel, Remove, Decompose from left to right. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.removeGlyph` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The metrics-key question is a second pop-up menu in the same dialog (Dissolve first) instead of a second dialog. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.findAndReplaceGlyphNames` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A Match Case checkbox, on by default, as names are case-sensitive; the handbook names only Find, Replace and Regex. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.findAndReplaceGlyphNames` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Nothing is renamed until Replace; there is no live preview. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.smartFilterEditor` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Adding a nested group is a `…` button on each row, standing in for Option-click on plus. Settled by observing the reference's editor. |
| `dialog.smartFilterEditor` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A new smart filter is named New Filter, or New Filter 2 and so on when the name is taken. Settled by adding a smart filter in the reference. |
| `dialog.listFilterEditor` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A new list filter is named New List, or New List 2 and so on when the name is taken. Settled by adding a list filter in the reference. |
| `dialog.languagesList` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The checklist offers every script the language data lists and the default ones, alphabetically. Settled by opening the reference's list. |

## `06-font-info.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `font.info.selectTab` | M7 | inferred | yes | Our own command for scripts; with the window closed it opens Font Info on that tab. Nothing to observe: the reference app switches tabs only by clicking them. |
| `font.info.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Setting a field the scope offers but the target lacks adds it, and a text sets a localizable field's default language; typing into an empty field row in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.listFields` | M7 | inferred | yes | The Font tab offers familyNames, designers, designerURL, manufacturers, manufacturerURL, copyrights, versionString, vendorID, uniqueID, licenses, licenseURL, trademarks, descriptions, sampleTexts, postscriptFontName, postscriptFullNames, compatibleFullNames, WWSFamilyName, WWSSubfamilyName and variationsPostScriptNamePrefix; the Exports tab the same plus styleNames, styleMapFamilyNames, styleMapStyleNames, preferredFamilyNames, preferredSubfamilyNames and variableStyleNames; the Masters tab offers none and keeps master properties as read. The pickers of the reference app settle the three lists. |
| `font.info.addField` | M7 | handbook | yes | Adding a field that is already present on a target is refused with a message pointing to Set Localized Value; the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.addField` | M7 | handbook | yes | A new localizable field gets one entry in the default language (dflt) holding the given value, or else the built-in value it overrides (familyNames takes the Family Name, styleNames the Style Name), else empty text; adding the field in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.removeField` | M7 | handbook | yes | Built-in fields (Family Name, Units per Em, Version, Creation Date, Style Name, Weight Class, Width Class) cannot be removed; only added fields and custom parameters can; the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.setLocalizedValue` | M7 | handbook | yes | A language tag the name table cannot map to a Windows language id is refused, except dflt (the default); the language menu of the reference app settles which tags it offers. |
| `font.info.removeLocalizedValue` | M7 | handbook | yes | Removing the last language entry of a field removes the field; removing its default (dflt) entry while others remain is allowed, and export then uses the English entry as the default name; the reference app settles both. |
| `font.info.copyRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | Rows are copied as our own JSON (scope, kind, key, value, enabled) and, for custom parameters and fields, also as property-list text in the file format's spelling, so a text editor or another document can read them; copying rows in the reference app and pasting into a text editor settles the format. |
| `font.info.pasteRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | The mode append adds parameters after the others and, since a field cannot repeat, replaces a field the target has; skip leaves out every row the target already has; pasting in the reference app settles both. |
| `font.info.pasteRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | A pasted row whose field or single-valued parameter is already present replaces its value, a repeatable parameter is appended, a row not allowed in the target scope is skipped and listed, and pasting between tabs and between documents is allowed; pasting in the reference app settles all four. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | Scaling by new/old covers every layer of every glyph (paths, component offsets but not component scales, anchors, guides, hints, backgrounds, special layers), the advance widths and vertical metrics, master metric positions and zone sizes, stems, kerning, global guides, and the custom parameters whose catalogue entry is measured in units (typoAscender, hheaAscender, winAscent, underlinePosition and the like); metrics keys, italic angles and percentages are kept; the reference app on a font holding all of these settles the list. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | Scaled coordinates are rounded to the font's grid (none when the grid spacing is 0), and a scale that would put a coordinate beyond ±32768 is refused; the reference app settles the rounding. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | A placed image keeps its place over the scaled outline (its position is scaled and its own scale multiplied by the factor), stroke widths and the positions and widths of hints not attached to nodes are scaled, and named number values are kept; the reference app on a font holding these settles it. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | Shapes the model keeps as stored (shape groups, paths with node kinds it cannot read) are not scaled, and a font with a locked glyph is refused naming the glyph, as every other edit of a locked glyph is; the reference app settles whether it scales locked glyphs. |
| `font.info.stepVersion` | M7 | handbook | yes | One step is 1 on the minor version; a step above 999 or below 0 is refused with a message and nothing changes, and the major part has no stepper; the stepper of the reference app settles both ends. |
| `font.info.updateDate` | M7 | handbook | yes | A given date with a time-zone offset is converted to UTC before it is stored; the reference app has no such parameter, so nothing there settles it. |
| `font.info.updateDate` | M7 | handbook | yes | The update button stores the current time in UTC (+0000); the date the reference app saves after the button settles the time zone. |
| `font.customParameters.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | The result lists rows in stored order with `enabled` and `known` flags and the values as stored; the reference app's scripting access to custom parameters would settle the shape. |
| `font.customParameters.describe` | M7 | inferred | yes | The search matches the query against names, aliases and summaries, case ignored; the Font Info parameter picker's filter in the running app would settle it. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | One scope may hold the same parameter more than once; for a parameter that takes one value the first enabled row counts and the list marks the others; adding a duplicate in the reference app settles whether it is refused. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | Without a value the new row holds the kind's empty value (off, 0, empty text, empty list); the new rows of the reference app settle it. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A parameter whose appendix entry names its scopes is refused in other scopes; one whose entry names none is allowed in all three. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A name the catalogue does not know is added as given, its value checked only against the size limits, and listed with the unknown mark; whether the reference app's picker accepts a typed free name settles it. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A name property (designers, versionString and the others the format keeps as properties) added here is written to the General fields, refused on masters and refused where the field is already set; adding one from the reference app's picker settles where it goes. |
| `font.customParameters.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | A value is checked against the catalogue's kind before it is stored, and an unknown name is refused rather than stored as is; editing a row in the running app's Font Info would settle it. |
| `font.master.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | The list gives id, name, icon and axis coordinates only, our choice of the fields a script needs to pick a master; the reference app's scripting access to masters would settle it. |
| `font.master.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | Several masters report null for a value they do not share, as the Font Info window shows multiple values; the window with two masters selected would settle it. |
| `font.master.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | The new master is called Regular, takes the first master's axis coordinates and the file format's default metric values scaled to the UPM (ascender 800, cap height 700, x-height 500, descender −200 at 1000 UPM, zones 16 and −16 at 1000 UPM), has no stem values, number values or parameters, and goes to the end of the list; every glyph gets an empty layer for it; adding a master in the reference app settles name, coordinates, values and position. |
| `font.master.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | Each glyph's empty layer for the new master takes the advance width of its layer in the first master (0 without one); the width of a new master's layers in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.master.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A font with a locked glyph refuses adding, duplicating and removing masters until the glyph is unlocked, since those change the glyph's layers; doing so with a locked glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.master.duplicate` | M7 | handbook | yes | Each copy goes right after its original with a new id and the same name; the original's master layers, their backgrounds, the special layers tied to it and its kerning are copied and tied to the copy; duplicating in the reference app settles position and special layers. |
| `font.master.remove` | M7 | handbook | yes | Removing a master removes its layers in every glyph, the special layers tied to it and its kerning; instances stay; parameters that name it (Link Metrics With Master, Get Hints From Master, Variable Font Origin) stay and the command's result names them in a warning; removing such a master in the reference app settles both. |
| `font.master.remove` | M7 | handbook | yes | An instance's manual interpolation weight for a removed master is dropped with it; removing a master an instance with manual interpolation uses in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.master.setIcon` | M7 | handbook | yes | A glyph icon is stored in the master's iconName, as a built-in icon is; the command refuses an icon name outside the 25 and a glyph the font lacks; the iconName the reference app saves after typing a glyph name in the picker settles the first part. |
| `font.metrics.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | Metrics are listed in the font's order with category, name and scope, plus one master's positions when asked; the Masters tab in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.metrics.add` | M7 | inferred | yes | Add Metric appends a definition of the given category and gives every master position 0 and zone 0; the reference app settles whether the Metrics heading has a plus button and what it adds. |
| `font.metrics.add` | M7 | inferred | yes | A definition equal to an existing one (same category, name and scope) is refused, and a metric without a category needs a name; adding a second plain x-height in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.metrics.set` | M7 | handbook | yes | A category change that the masters' values do not fit (a baseline off 0, an italic angle beyond ±89°) is refused rather than changing the values; changing a metric with a value of 800 to Baseline in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.metrics.remove` | M7 | inferred | yes | Every metric can be removed, Baseline and Italic Angle included, and its values go from every master; the reference app settles whether the built-in metrics can be deleted. |
| `font.master.setMetric` | M7 | handbook | yes | Zone sizes may be negative (zones below a line, such as at the baseline and the descender); Italic Angle takes a position only, between −89 and 89 degrees; typing a negative zone in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.stems.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | Stems are listed in the font's order with name, orientation and scope, plus one master's values when asked; the Masters tab in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.stems.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A stem added without a name is called `Stem` with `.1`, `.2` appended while taken, names are unique, and every master gets 0 unless `values` gives its value; adding a stem in the reference app settles the default name and value. |
| `font.numbers.list` | M8 | inferred | yes | Numbers are listed in the font's order, plus one master's values when asked; the Masters tab in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.numbers.add` | M8 | handbook | yes | A number value name is letters, digits and `_`, not starting with a digit, so that a `$name` token can read all of it; a leading `.` is allowed for settings such as `.monospace`, which the handbook names. A name the font has is refused. Naming a number value with a space, a hyphen or an inner dot in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.numbers.rename` | M8 | inferred | yes | Renaming a number value leaves tokens in feature code unchanged, so they then name an unknown value and the next compile reports them; renaming one used in feature code in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.instance.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | An instance whose source states no class reports the format's defaults (400 and 5); the Exports tab showing such an instance in the running app would settle it. |
| `font.instance.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | The result includes every General field the entry has and its custom parameters in stored order; the Exports tab in the running app would settle which fields are shown. |
| `font.instance.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Several entries change in one undo step, and a value one of them cannot take refuses the whole call; editing several exports at once in the running app would settle it. |
| `font.instance.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | The new instance has Weight Class 400, Width Class 5, the first master's axis coordinates, Active on, no style linking, and goes to the end of the list; adding an instance in the reference app settles the defaults and the position. |
| `font.instance.setStyleLinking` | M7 | handbook | yes | An empty base field with Bold or Italic checked links to Regular; exporting such an instance from the reference app settles it. |
| `font.instance.setWidthClass` | M7 | handbook | yes | Values outside 1–9 are refused, typed or not; typing 10 into the field of the reference app settles it. |
| `font.features.list` | M8 | inferred | yes | Entries are listed prefixes, classes, then features, each in stored order, with a flag for current compile errors; the Features tab sidebar in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.features.get` | M8 | inferred | yes | Stylistic set names are returned as language and value pairs in stored order; the Features tab with a named stylistic set would settle it. |
| `font.features.listAvailable` | M8 | inferred | yes | A tag is offered as generatable when one of our generators can write an entry of that name in this font; the Add Feature picker in the running app would settle which tags it marks. |
| `font.features.addPrefix` | M8 | handbook | yes | A new prefix without a name is called Prefix, then Prefix 2, Prefix 3 and so on, and a prefix name another prefix has is refused; adding two prefixes in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.addFeature` | M8 | handbook | yes | Adding a tag the font already has is refused, although files may hold a tag twice; adding a present feature again from the picker in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.addFeature` | M8 | handbook | yes | A feature added without `automatic` is a manual entry with empty code, and the picker asks for automatic code when Update could generate the feature; adding smcp from the picker to a font with .sc glyphs in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.rename` | M8 | inferred | yes | A double-click on a sidebar entry's name renames it; a feature tag must be four characters and a name may not be taken in its group, else the command is refused; the running app would settle it. |
| `font.features.setCode` | M8 | inferred | yes | Set Feature Code refuses an automatic entry instead of switching it to manual; typing into an automatic entry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.update` | M8 | handbook | yes | An automatic entry whose generator no longer writes it (its glyphs are gone) is removed, as the handbook says automatic classes are at export; Update after deleting every .sc glyph would settle it. |
| `font.features.update` | M8 | handbook | yes | A new feature goes before the first feature that sorts after it in this order, else last: aalt, ccmp, locl, the Indic features, subs, sinf, sups, numr, dnom, frac, afrc, ordn, lnum, pnum, tnum, onum, case, the positional forms, dlig, liga, hlig, rlig, c2sc, smcp, c2pc, pcap, hist, titl, swsh, ornm, salt, ss01 to ss20, cv01 to cv99, the width and kana features, vrt2, cpsp; aalt and the Languagesystems prefix go first. Oswald and Markow store their features in this order; Update on F1 (Latin features) would settle it. |
| `font.features.update` | M8 | handbook | yes | salt is added beside ss01, as the handbook says, although none of the five corpus sources with an automatic ss01 has a salt feature; Update on a font with .ss01 glyphs would settle it. |
| `font.features.updateEntry` | M8 | handbook | yes | An entry whose glyphs are gone keeps its place with empty code rather than being removed; pressing the entry's update button after deleting its glyphs would settle it. |
| `font.features.compile` | M8 | handbook | yes | Compile reads the master the Features tab passes (the active Edit View tab's), else the first master; compiling in a two-master font whose masters differ in a number value used by the code, with the second master showing, would settle it. |
| `font.features.compile` | M8 | handbook | yes | Compile checks the code without the generated kerning and mark features; an error the reference app reports only when the generated code is added would show the difference. |
| `font.features.listErrors` | M8 | inferred | yes | The error list is the one of the last Compile and is marked out of date after an edit rather than recomputed; editing code after a compile in the reference app and watching whether the error markers move or vanish would settle it. |
| `font.features.applyFix` | M8 | handbook | yes | Four fixes of ours carry a wrench: a glyph name that matches an existing one apart from case, a class name that matches a defined one apart from case, a missing semicolon at the end of a statement (inserted before any comment), and a class used before its entry (moving the class entry up); the reference app's error list on a font with these mistakes would settle its own set. |
| `font.features.listSnippets` | M8 | inferred | yes | The built-in snippets come before the user's groups, and the groups follow file-name order; opening the snippets menu with two snippet files in the folder would settle the order. |
| `font.features.insertSnippet` | M8 | handbook | yes | A title without a group finds the built-in snippet first, then the first user snippet of that title in menu order; only the menu names snippets in the reference app, so nothing there settles it. |
| `font.features.setLabel` | M8 | handbook | yes | The language of a name is `dflt` or one of the OpenType language tags the name table can hold, and an empty name is refused; the language picker of the reference app would settle the list. |
| `font.features.complete` | M8 | inferred | yes | Completion offers glyphs in rules, classes after @, lookups after `lookup`, number values after $ and inside ${…}, registered feature tags after `feature` and statement keywords (then glyphs) at the start of a statement, exact-case matches first; typing in each of these places in the reference app would settle its lists. |
| `font.settings.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | keyboardIncrementShift is the format's keyboardIncrementBig and keyboardIncrementCmd its keyboardIncrementHuge, with 10 and 100 when the font sets none; saving after changing the two fields in the reference app settles the keys. |
| `font.settings.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Grid spacing is accepted from 0 to 10000, subdivision from 1 to 1000 and the keyboard increments above 0 up to 10000, and a value equal to the current one (stated or default) is not stored; typing the ends of each range in the reference app settles them. |
| `font.note.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | The note is returned as plain text, as stored; the Notes tab in the running app would settle whether formatting is kept. |
| `font.note.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Setting the note replaces it whole in one undo step; editing the Notes tab in the running app would settle how undo groups typing. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Shift-click adds every row between the last row clicked and the clicked one; Shift-clicking in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Copy puts the rows' JSON on the system clipboard, and a selected row of a parameter whose name repeats copies every row of that name; pasting copied rows into a text editor from the reference app settles the format. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Paste replaces the rows with the same key in the shown font, masters or exports and appends repeatable parameters; pasting a row the target has in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Cmd+Delete removes the selected fields in one undo step and each selected parameter in one of its own, and leaves built-in fields; undoing after Cmd+Delete in the reference app settles the steps. |
| `fontInfo.rows.localizable` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The plus button adds the first language the field lacks, in the name table's tag order after the default language, with empty text; picking another language in a row's menu moves its text there; clicking the plus in the reference app settles both. |
| `fontInfo.familyName.allowedCharacters` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Other characters are accepted with a warning, not refused; typing an ampersand or an accented letter in the reference app settles whether it warns, refuses or neither. |
| `fontInfo.unitsPerEm.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | font.info.set refuses values outside 16–16384 and returns a warning above 3000, above 4096 (glyph widths of CFF-flavoured fonts, given whatever the outline flavour) and above 5000; the reference app settles whether it warns at all. |
| `fontInfo.version.headAndNameId5` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The suffix is `;FontCraft <version>`, without a build number while FontCraft has none; an export from the reference app shows only its own form. |
| `fontInfo.customParameters.masterInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A parameter interpolates when its value is a number, or a list of numbers of the same length, on every master; other values are taken from the origin master. Settle with a parameter that is text on one master in the reference app. |
| `fontInfo.customParameters.masterInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A parameter that is a whole number on every weighted master stays whole, rounded half up as fontmake rounds font info; the origin master's parameters are the ones the instance has, so one missing from the origin is left out. Settle with a parameter set only on the second master in the reference app. |
| `fontInfo.metrics.defaultsByScript` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A new font whose chosen scripts include Arabic and not Latin gets the metrics Ascender 800, Alef Height 700, Joining Line 150, Baseline 0, Descender −200 and Meem Depth −250 at 1000 UPM; a new Arabic font in the reference app settles names, order and values. |
| `fontInfo.metrics.defaultsByScript` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Alef Height, Joining Line and Meem Depth are stored as named metrics without a category, with zones of 16 units (−16 below the baseline, none on the Joining Line); the file a new Arabic font saves in the reference app settles their categories and zones. |
| `fontInfo.metrics.scopeHidesLine` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A hidden metric hides its alignment zone too, and in text mode the glyph being edited decides for the whole line of text; scoping x-height to Greek and typing A and alpha side by side in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.exports.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | With nothing selected the tab acts on the first entry, and Cmd-click on the only selected entry leaves the first entry shown; the reference app settles what an empty selection shows. |
| `fontInfo.exports.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Dropping a dragged entry onto another moves it to that entry's place, and the moved entry becomes the selection; dragging in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.exports.inactiveGrayIcon` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Entries are drawn with a square icon in the accent colour, a variable font setting with an outlined square; inactive ones grey; the reference app's sidebar settles the icons. |
| `fontInfo.styleName.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | font.instance.set and the Exports tab warn, without refusing, when the style name has characters outside ASCII letters, digits, spaces and hyphens, or when family and style name together exceed 20 characters without a postscriptFontName; the reference app settles whether it warns. |
| `fontInfo.styleName.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | An empty or all-space style name is refused by font.instance.set and font.instance.add; clearing the Style Name field in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.styleLinking.sameFamily` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | font.instance.setStyleLinking and font.instance.set link anyway but warn when the base instance named in the same file exports under another family name (its familyNames field); a base in another file is not checked. Linking two instances with different familyNames in the reference app settles whether it warns. |
| `fontInfo.features.stylisticSetNames` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A name in a language without a Windows language id is left out, as fontc does (glyphsLib stops with an error), and text tokens in names are not expanded yet; a name in such a language exported from the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.fontType` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Default and Variable both use the variable-font model (fontTools' VariationModel); they agree on one axis and on full grids, and the Multiple Master model of the reference is not documented for other setups. Settle by comparing an instance of an irregular two-axis setup under both settings in the reference app (docs/plans/M9.md § 8). |
| `fontInfo.notes.markdownHighlighting` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Headings of every level take the section heading size and the strong text colour, bold takes the strong text colour, italic slants, links take the accent colour, and the Markdown syntax itself is dimmed; the reference app's Notes tab settles the styles. |
| `fontInfo.notes.markdownHighlighting` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Underscore emphasis needs a word boundary on both sides and asterisk emphasis does not, emphasis and links stay within one line, and an autolink needs an http, https or mailto address; typing the edge cases in the reference app settles them. |
| `fontInfo.notes.markdownHighlighting` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A note longer than 256 KiB is shown without highlighting so typing stays fast; a long note in the reference app settles whether it stops highlighting. |
| `fontInfo.exports.instancePreview` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The preview sits above the instance's fields, one line high, and draws the first selected instance; an instance between masters shows a message until M9 interpolates, and a variable font setting shows none; the Exports tab of the reference app settles placement. |
| `fontInfo.exports.instancePreview` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The default `Aang126` is read as characters and drawn with the glyphs mapped to them, skipping characters the font lacks; the reference app settles whether it names glyphs instead. |
| `font.axes.add` | M9 | handbook | no | A new axis is named Weight with tag wght when the font has no such axis, else a private axis `Axis N` with tag `AXSN`; settle by pressing the plus button in the reference app. |
| `font.axes.add` | M9 | handbook | no | Existing masters, instances and virtual masters get coordinate 0 on the new axis, and intermediate layers inherit it from their master; settle in the reference app's Masters tab after adding an axis. |
| `font.axes.remove` | M9 | handbook | no | Removing an axis drops its coordinate from every master, instance, virtual master, intermediate layer and alternate range; an alternate range that becomes empty on every axis stays an alternate layer `[]`. Settle in the reference app. |
| `font.master.addFromOtherFont` | M9 | handbook | no | Glyphs missing in the imported master get an empty layer for it; glyphs only the other font has are not added; axis coordinates come from the other font's master by axis tag, 0 for tags it lacks. Settle by adding a master from a font with different glyphs in the reference app. |
| `fontInfo` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Font Info opens on the tab it last showed for that document, and on the Font tab the first time; opening it twice in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | One Font Info window shows the active document, and switching documents shows the other document's information in it; opening Font Info in two documents of the reference app settles whether each keeps a window of its own. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The double-arrow button opens a prompt under the field that asks for the new units per em, starting at the current value, with Scale and Cancel buttons; clicking the button in the reference app settles the prompt's form. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The minor version shows three digits, and its stepper is a plus and a minus button after the field; the reference app's Version field settles the look. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The Creation Date is shown and typed as the file stores it (`2026-10-11 12:00:00 +0000`), next to an Update button; the reference app's date field settles the display. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The Version row stands for both version parts, so selecting it selects and copies versionMajor and versionMinor together; copying the Version row in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Until axes can be edited (M9), the Axes section lists the stored axes without a plus button, their names, tags and Hidden boxes read-only. |
| `fontInfo.fieldPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The picker lists only the fields not yet present on the selected font, masters or exports, in the order of font.info.listFields, and allows several to be selected; the picker of each tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.fieldPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A click selects one field and Cmd-click or Shift-click adds or removes one more; Add adds them in list order, each as an undo step of its own; using the picker in the reference app settles both. |
| `fontInfo.customParameterPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The list shows every catalogue parameter allowed in the tab's scope, in alphabetical order, including those already present; the search matches any part of the name, ignoring case; the picker in the reference app settles order and matching. |
| `fontInfo.customParameterPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A parameter whose appendix entry names no scope is offered in all three tabs; the picker of each tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.customParameterPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The search also matches the descriptions and the other spellings of a name, and Add gives each parameter its kind's empty value; searching for a word of a description in the reference app settles the first, adding Filter there the second. |
| `fontInfo.masters` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A master made with Add Master becomes the selection, and a dragged master stays selected at its new place; the list's selection in the reference app after each action settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masters` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The minus button is disabled while every master is selected, because a font keeps at least one master; whether the reference app disables it or refuses with a message settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masters` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The General section has the name and icon only, without a plus button, because masters take no added General fields; the masters tab of the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masterIconPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A click on a built-in icon applies it to the selected masters and closes the picker; the glyph field applies on Use Glyph or Enter; how the reference app's popover closes settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masterIconPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The 25 built-in icons are five weights (Light, Regular, Medium, SemiBold, Bold) by five widths (Condensed, SemiCondensed, normal, SemiExtended, Extended), named `Weight` or `Weight_Width` as the corpus files spell them; the names the reference app writes for each icon of its picker settle the set. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The category menu offers the file format's metric types (ascender, cap height, slant height, x-height, midHeight, bodyHeight, descender, baseline, italic angle) with readable labels, plus an empty category for a metric known only by its name; the menu of the reference app settles the list and the labels. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The scope is typed as a glyph predicate in a text field rather than built with the smart-filter rule editor; the scope editor of the reference app settles whether it shows rules. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The plus beside the Metrics heading opens this editor to add a metric, starting at the first category the font has no plain metric of; what the reference app's plus adds settles it. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A stem's actions button opens the same editor with only the scope field; the reference app's stem scope popover settles its fields. |
| `fontInfo.exports` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Active, Style Name, Weight Class, Width Class, the read-only Axes Coordinates and Style Linking sit under the General heading, above the added fields; the Exports tab of the reference app settles the order. |
| `fontInfo.exports` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A variable font setting shows Active, its name and its custom parameters, without classes, axes or style linking; selecting a variable font setting in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.exports` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The class menus list each standard value with its OpenType name (400 Regular, 5 Medium (normal)); the disclosure menus of the reference app settle the labels. |
| `fontInfo.other` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Each setting is written when its field loses the keyboard or its box or menu changes, as one undo step per change; changing a setting and choosing Undo in the reference app settles whether settings are undoable. |
| `fontInfo.notes` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The note is written when the text box loses the keyboard, as one undo step for everything typed meanwhile; typing in the Notes tab and choosing Undo in the reference app settles the undo steps. |
| `instance.manualInterpolation` (parameter) | M9 | docs | – | With manual interpolation on, the instance is the stored factors applied to the master layers; intermediate layers get no weight, alternate layers still switch by the instance's location, and the factors also weight metrics, kerning and parameters; settle with an instance using manual factors and a brace glyph in the reference app. |
| `instance.instanceInterpolations` (parameter) | M9 | docs | – | The model keeps one number per master; an x/y pair keeps its first number, so horizontal and vertical factors are the same; settle with an instance whose factors are pairs in the reference app. |

## `07-import-export.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `file.exportAll` | M2 | handbook | yes | The reference's settings for Export All are not documented; using each document's last-used export settings is our assumption, and until FontCraft remembers settings the command takes them as parameters. |
| `file.exportVariable` | M9 | handbook | yes | A glyph that has no layer for the origin master does not vary and keeps the origin's empty outline, with a warning; exporting such a font from the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.exportVariable` | M9 | handbook | yes | An intermediate layer that lies outside the axes of a variable font setting (one that disables masters) is left out of that font, with a note; exporting such a setting from the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.exportMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | The `metrics` format is our kerning-text format (`kerning-text` in 30-formats.toml), because the reference does not document its own metrics file; files in the reference format are neither read nor written. |
| `file.exportMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A metrics file holds the pairs of one direction (left to right by default, `direction` for the others), because kerning-text has one direction line; an export of a master with right-to-left or vertical pairs needs one file per direction. A reference metrics file with such pairs would settle it. |
| `file.exportMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | The metrics file's extension is .tsv; the reference's own extension is not documented. |
| `file.open` | M1 | handbook | yes | The menu label and place are our assumption, and the shortcut is the platform convention; the chapter states neither. |
| `file.importOutlines` | M3 | handbook | yes | An SVG reads as one font unit per SVG unit with SVG y = 0 at the ascender; the handbook does not say how a drawing is scaled. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | The import goes to the master given, else the first master, and `sourceMaster` defaults to the first master of the source file. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Importing kerning replaces the master's pairs in each imported direction, so export then import gives back the same table; the handbook does not say whether it merges. Importing into a master with pairs in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Import Kerning and Import Kerning Classes default to on (Import Kerning off for a Glyphs file, which cannot give kerning values), and Import Metrics to off; the handbook states no defaults. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A metrics file also sets the left, right and width keys as it gives them, an empty key field removing the key the field shows; an AFM leaves the keys as they are. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Width, Outline Centred rounds the new LSB half up, so the outline stays on whole units; the handbook does not say. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Selected Glyphs limits glyph metrics and kerning classes; kerning values are imported whole. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Selected Glyphs means the glyphs selected in Font View, also while an Edit View tab shows; the handbook does not say which selection. Ticking it with a tab open in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | A class or prefix whose name the font already has gets the first free numeric suffix from 2 (`Upper2`), and the imported code's references to a renamed class follow it; importing into a font with clashing names in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | A feature whose tag the font already has is added as a second entry with that tag, because blocks of one tag add up in the feature-file language and a suffixed tag is not a tag; the same check would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | Each run of top-level statements other than class definitions and feature blocks becomes a prefix named Prefix, and a `# Prefix: name` comment names the prefix after it; importing a file with lookups and language systems in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | Statements the split cannot place (a `useExtension` feature, an `anon` block, a class whose value is not a list) stay in a prefix as written rather than failing the import. |
| `file.exportFeatures` | M8 | inferred | yes | The reference app documents no feature-file export; the command exists for the command line and automation. |
| `file.exportFeatures` | M8 | inferred | yes | The fea export assembles the stored entries without running Update first, so automatic entries are written as they were last updated; running Update before the export gives the regenerated code. |
| `file.exportFeatures` | M8 | inferred | yes | The fea-kern export names group classes as the reference's feature code does (`@MMK_L_` for a right-side group, `@MMK_R_` for a left-side group) and leaves out pairs whose glyphs are missing or whose names feature code cannot spell, counting them in the warnings. |
| `file.newProject` | M7 | handbook | yes | A new project is saved as a native FontCraft project file (`.fontcraftproject`, JSON: the linked font's path relative to the project, the instances in the native format, the export destination); reading and writing `.glyphsproject` waits for a sample file from the reference app. |
| `file.newProject` | M7 | handbook | yes | Project edits are not undo steps: Edit › Undo acts on font documents only; undoing in a project window of the reference app settles it. |
| `file.newProject` | M7 | handbook | yes | An unsaved project is called Untitled Project (then Untitled Project 2, …); the reference app's window title settles it. |
| `project.linkFont` | M7 | handbook | yes | The link is stored relative to the project file when both share a folder tree, else absolute; linking copies the font's instances into the project only when the project has none; saving a project in the reference app settles the path form. |
| `project.linkFont` | M7 | handbook | yes | A link that no longer resolves is kept when the project opens and reported as a warning; only export and revert refuse it; opening a project whose font was moved in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.addInstance` | M7 | handbook | yes | A new project instance has the defaults of font.instance.add; a variable font setting waits for M9 and is refused until then. |
| `project.addInstance` | M7 | handbook | yes | `at` places the new instance in the list (default: the end); the plus button in the reference app adds at the end or after the selection, which observing it settles. |
| `project.revertInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | Reverting reads the font file on disk, not an open copy with unsaved changes; reverting with the font open and edited in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.moveInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | Several moved instances keep their order and land together, the first at `to` of the list without them; dragging several rows in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.duplicateInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | A duplicate keeps the original's name and goes right after it unless `to` is given. |
| `project.duplicateInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | With `to`, the copies go together at that position; a copy gets no id of its own. |
| `project.editInstance` | M7 | handbook | yes | Edits are checked as Font Info › Exports checks an export of the linked font (its axes and masters), or of a new empty font while no font is linked. |
| `project.setExportDestination` | M7 | handbook | yes | The destination is stored relative to the project file like the font link, need not exist, and is created by export; saving a project in the reference app settles the stored form. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export refuses a project with unsaved changes as well as one never saved; exporting in the reference app with an unsaved change settles it. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export reads the font file on disk even when the font is open with unsaved changes; exporting with the font open and edited settles it. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export builds TrueType outlines in plain files unless asked otherwise, and a project without an active static instance is refused rather than exporting the font's first master; the reference app's export from a project settles both. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export is refused, writing nothing, when one of its files would replace the linked font (a compiled font in the destination folder); exporting such a project in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.open` | M7 | inferred | yes | A project already open is made active rather than opened twice, as for fonts; a font link that no longer resolves is kept and reported; opening one in the reference app settles both. |
| `project.save` | M7 | handbook | yes | Saving writes only the project file, atomically, and refuses a path that is the linked font; File › Save in a project window runs this command. |
| `project.close` | M7 | inferred | yes | Closing a project with unsaved changes refuses unless `discard` is true, as closing a font does; closing a changed project window in the reference app would settle what it asks. |
| `project.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | A query of ours listing each open project's font link, destination, instances and saved state; the reference app shows the same facts only in each project window. |
| `export.fileNaming` (behavior) | M2 | memory | – | The PostScript-name stem is recalled from the reference, not read in a source; dropping characters a file name cannot hold and falling back to the source file's name are ours. |
| `export.flavourSubfolders` (behavior) | M2 | inferred | – | An export of both flavours at once puts them into ttf and otf subfolders; how the reference lays out such an export is not documented. |
| `export.instanceExportFolder` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The sub-path is used as written, `..` segments included, as the handbook says; an absolute path, an empty segment, a folder name with a character such as `:` or `\`, or a path of more than 32 segments makes export skip that instance with the reason, one trailing `/` is ignored, and the warnings name every file written outside the export destination; an Export Folder of `../x` and one of `/tmp/x` in the reference app settle it. |
| `export.instanceExportFolder` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Exports from the command line, MCP and the control channel skip an instance whose Export Folder resolves outside the destination folder; the reference app has no such callers, so nothing there settles it. |
| `export.afmIsLossy` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Group pairs are expanded to glyph pairs with the exceptions applied, so the AFM file holds the kerning that applies to every covered glyph pair; the handbook does not say. |
| `export.afmIsLossy` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | AFM values stay in the font's units and are not scaled to 1,000 units per em, so a round trip loses nothing; Adobe's note assumes 1,000. An AFM exported by the reference from a 2,048-unit font would settle it. |
| `open.alignsCompositeMetrics` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A UFO counts as an imported file and is aligned on opening, because the Source Formats page points a UFO to the importing behaviours and gives it its own Disable Automatic Alignment option in Font Info; the running app opening a UFO with an out-of-date composite would settle it. |
| `open.alignsCompositeMetrics` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Aligning on opening is not an undo step and does not mark the document as edited; the reference app's edited mark after opening a UFO with an out-of-date composite settles it. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A class read from a compiled font becomes a kerning group named after its first glyph in glyph order; no source says how the reference names recovered groups. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph keeps the first class it is in on each side, and pairs of a later class with other members are stored as glyph pairs; opening a font whose subtables use overlapping classes in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A `kern` feature record used only by right-to-left scripts fills the right-to-left kerning, any other the left-to-right kerning; opening an Arabic font in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A pair two feature records of one direction cover (for two scripts) takes the first record's value; no source says what the reference does. |
| `import.outlinesSingleInEditView` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A single file imported in Edit View is appended to the active layer; the handbook does not say whether existing shapes are kept or which layer receives them. |
| `import.metricsModes` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The default mode is LSB and width (the RSB follows); the handbook gives no default. |
| `import.metricsBreaksAutoAlignment` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Imported values that round to the derived ones (less than half a unit away) leave alignment on; importing a metrics file whose values differ by fractions in the reference app settles it. |
| `import.metricsBreaksAutoAlignment` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Alignment is switched off only on the layer of the master being imported, for every component of that layer; the other masters' layers stay aligned; importing into one master of a two-master font in the reference app settles it. |
| `paste.strokeAndFillMayDouble` (behavior) | M3 | inferred | – | A pasted shape with both fill and stroke may arrive as two contours; this is our reading of the handbook's advice, which gives no cause. |
| `dialog.exportMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The format, file name and folder sit on the tab, with the folder chooser for the folder, because the dialog framework has no save-file chooser; the handbook puts them in a save dialog after Next…. A save-file chooser in the shell would settle it. |
| `dialog.exportMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The tab shows a Master pop-up that starts on the toolbar's master, so a master can be exported without changing the toolbar; the handbook names no such control. |
| `dialog.importOutlinesMissingGlyph` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | One question covers every unmatched file, with the buttons Add Glyphs and Skip; the handbook asks once per file, with Add Glyph and OK (skip). |
| `dialog.importMetricsFile` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The two dialogs are two tabs of one window opened from the menu, with the file typed as a path, because the dialog framework has no file chooser field; the handbook asks for the file first and then shows the dialog that fits it. A file field with a chooser would settle it. |
| `dialog.importMetricsFont` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The tab can import from another open document as well as from a file; the handbook names files only. |
| `dialog.importMetricsFont` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The tab offers Selected Glyphs and a Source Master field; the handbook does not say whether this dialog has them. |
| `dialog.importFeatures` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | The file is typed as a path, as in the other import dialogs, until the shell has a file chooser limited to .fea files. |
| `dialog.pastedOutlineBounds` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The correction only translates: it moves the pasted box's bottom left to the origin. |
| `dialog.pastedOutlineBounds` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The buttons read Move and Keep Position, with the line "The pasted outline lies outside the glyph box." above the fields. |
| `dialog.saveChangedOnDisk` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | The reference app asks before overwriting a file changed on disk, with Overwrite and Cancel; saving over a file another program changed in the reference app settles it. |

## `08-spacing-kerning.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A numeric left sidebearing moves the outline, components, anchors, guides, annotations, placed image and background sideways and changes the width by the same amount, so the right sidebearing stays. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key is evaluated and applied as soon as it is stored. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A glyph-level key is applied to the edited layer only; the glyph's other layers keep their numbers, out of sync, until Update Metrics runs. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key that cannot be evaluated (a missing glyph, division by zero, an empty referenced glyph, a result out of range) is refused and nothing is stored. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key typed with one `=` into a field that shows a layer key removes that layer key, so the new glyph-level key applies on that layer. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | The sidebearing setters skip a layer without an outline and report it, since such a layer has no sidebearings. |
| `spacing.setRightSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key is evaluated and applied as soon as it is stored. |
| `spacing.setWidth` | M5 | handbook | yes | A numeric width change is absorbed by the right sidebearing; the handbook does not say how it is split. |
| `spacing.setWidth` | M5 | handbook | yes | A width key may be a glyph name, a constant or a calculation; `\|` and `@` are refused in the width field. |
| `spacing.setTopSidebearing` | M5 | inferred | yes | Setting the top sidebearing moves the vertical origin and changes the vertical width by the same amount, so the bottom sidebearing stays, as the left sidebearing does horizontally. |
| `spacing.setTopSidebearing` | M5 | inferred | yes | The top and bottom fields take the same key forms as the left and right ones, with `\|` naming the opposite vertical sidebearing; `@` is refused there. |
| `spacing.setBottomSidebearing` | M5 | inferred | yes | Setting the bottom sidebearing changes the vertical width only. |
| `spacing.updateMetricsKey` | M5 | handbook | yes | A metric whose value rounded half up already equals its key's value is left as it is, so the command makes no change there; watching the button on a fractional metric in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.updateMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Keys are evaluated in dependency order (the metrics a key reads, and the left sidebearings of the glyphs a composite's components show, come first), so a glyph whose reference or base was updated in the same run gets the new value; one Update Metrics on a two-step chain and on a composite of a keyed base in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.updateMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key that cannot be evaluated (unlinked, in error, or on an empty layer) is skipped and the other keys are still updated; running it on a font with one unlinked key in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.updateMetricsForAllMasters` | M5 | handbook | yes | Besides the master layers it updates the layers with attributes (intermediate, alternate, colour, smart), the layers the out-of-sync scan lists, and leaves out masters that take their metrics from another master; a brace layer with an out-of-sync key in the reference app would settle it. |
| `spacing.listOutOfSyncMetrics` | M5 | inferred | yes | A key is out of sync when its value differs from the layer's metric rounded half up. |
| `spacing.listOutOfSyncMetrics` | M5 | inferred | yes | Master layers and layers with attributes (intermediate, alternate, colour, smart) are scanned; backup layers and the layers of a master that takes its metrics from another master are not. |
| `kerning.setMode` | M5 | handbook | yes | A new Edit View tab starts in kerning mode; no source gives the default. |
| `kerning.setPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | A group side must be the group some glyph has on the side that faces the pair, and a glyph side a glyph of the font, so a typo is refused rather than stored as a pair that never applies; storing a pair for an unknown group in the running app's Kerning window settles it. |
| `kerning.setPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | Values beyond ±32,767 are refused, the range a compiled pair value holds; typing a larger value in a kern field settles what the reference does. |
| `kerning.removePair` | M5 | handbook | yes | Removing a pair the master does not store is refused with a message rather than ignored; clearing an empty kern field in the running app shows nothing either way, so only the scripting API's behaviour would settle it. |
| `kerning.increaseLeft` | M5 | handbook | yes | Increase adds to the kerning value (more space) and decrease subtracts; the handbook names the directions without a sign, and the reference kerning tutorial says negative values tighten a pair. The Info box kern value after one keypress, in the running app or a video, settles it. |
| `kerning.setLeftGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | An empty name clears the group, as null does; emptying a Group field in the Info box of the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.setTopGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | The top and bottom group fields check names as the left and right ones do. |
| `view.showGroupMembers` | M5 | handbook | yes | Members are drawn as thin coloured outlines behind the glyphs, in text mode and while editing outlines alike; a screenshot of the option in the reference app settles the look. |
| `view.showGroupMembers` | M5 | handbook | yes | At most 200 members are drawn for each of the two glyphs, the first in glyph order; a very large group in the reference app settles whether it caps them. |
| `window.kerning` | M5 | handbook | yes | While Font View shows, the window keeps the writing direction of the last Edit View tab shown and Font View's master; opening the window from Font View in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.resetFilter` | M5 | handbook | yes | Reset leaves the search term in the field and restores only the options; choosing Reset with a term typed in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.showPairInEditView` | M5 | handbook | yes | The pair at the cursor is the current glyph and the glyph before it; with no glyph before the current one the pair is inserted at the cursor, and the cursor ends between the two glyphs. Selecting rows in the reference with the cursor at the start of a text settles it. |
| `kerning.selectNextPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | With no row selected Down selects the first row, and at the last row the selection stays; pressing Down past the end of the list in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.selectPreviousPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | With no row selected Up selects the last row, and at the first row the selection stays; pressing Up at the top of the list in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.showAllGlyphs` | M5 | handbook | yes | The pairs replace the pair at the cursor, one line per left glyph with its pairs separated by the space glyph (none when the font has no space); running Show All Glyphs on a group pair in the reference settles the layout. |
| `kerning.renameGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | With scope `pair`, only the side of the selected pair changes to the new name and the glyphs keep their group; no source says whether the reference also moves glyphs into the new group, and the Group fields after a pair-only rename settle it. |
| `kerning.renameGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | With scope `font`, a new name that is already a group of that side is refused rather than merging the two groups; renaming onto an existing group in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.renameGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | With scope `font`, the rename reaches every master and both writing directions, since groups are shared between them; reading another master's pairs after a rename in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.copyPairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | The clipboard holds the pairs as kerning-text lines (`left<TAB>right<TAB>value`), so they also paste into a text editor; no source gives the reference clipboard format. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A pasted group side keeps its name; a group the target font lacks is pasted as it is and reported. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A glyph the target font lacks is pasted as it is and reported, like a missing group. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A pasted pair that exists with the same value is not a conflict, so it does not bring up the question; pasting an unchanged selection back into its master settles it. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | When the pasted pairs name one pair twice, the later value counts. |
| `kerning.deletePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A pair the master lacks (with all masters: that no master has) refuses the whole command; the reference can only delete listed rows, so the question does not arise there. |
| `kerning.deletePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A linked master is refused, as for the other kerning edits. |
| `kerning.cleanUp` | M5 | handbook | yes | Clean Up acts on the given master and direction only, like the Kerning window list; running it on a font with two masters and reading the other master's pairs settles it. |
| `kerning.compress` | M5 | handbook | yes | Compress acts on the given master and direction only; running it on a font with two masters and reading the other master's pairs settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Under the descender, the left sidebearing sits at the glyph's left edge, the right one at its right edge and the width centred below them; in vertical text they run along the column's left edge; a screenshot of the reference app settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A label keeps to the part of the advance a negatively kerned neighbour does not cover, so neighbouring labels do not overlap; a tightly kerned pair in the reference app settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Glyphs less than 24 screen points wide get no labels; the reference app zoomed out settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph whose advance on screen is too narrow for its two sidebearing labels side by side shows only its width label; the labels of a narrow glyph such as `i` at a small zoom in the reference app settle it. |
| `spacing.infoBoxArrowSteps` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Up and Down in a field that holds a metrics key leave the key unchanged; no source says what they do there, and pressing Up in such a field in the reference app settles it. |
| `spacing.shortcutsNeedTextTool` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A spacing shortcut on a metric that holds a metrics key changes the number and keeps the key, which then shows as out of sync; no source says whether the reference keeps the key, removes it or ignores the keypress. |
| `spacing.shortcutModifierScheme` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In right-to-left text the shortcuts address the visual left and right sidebearings, as in left-to-right text; in top-to-bottom text they do nothing in M5. No source says. |
| `metricsKey.notSyncedAutomatically` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A key whose chain of references returns to its own glyph, or runs deeper than 32 steps, is an error: the metric keeps its number and the key is reported. A forum answer by the reference developers shows the reference does not stop such cycles (thread "Possible bug with spacing formula"). Watching the reference app with such a key settles what it shows. |
| `metricsKey.notSyncedAutomatically` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Division by zero, and a result outside 0 to 65,535 units for a width or −32,768 to 32,767 for a sidebearing, are errors reported the same way; watching the reference app with such a key settles what it shows. |
| `metricsKey.calculationSyntax` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Spaces around the parts of a key are ignored. |
| `metricsKey.calculationSyntax` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A key holds at most one operation; a second operator or a parenthesis makes it invalid, and it is reported, not evaluated. |
| `metricsKey.calculationSyntax` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | `@` and its height come last, after the operation (`=H+8@200`). |
| `metricsKey.decimalOperands` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The glyph name is the longest leading part of the key that names a glyph in the font; what follows must be nothing, an operation, or `@` and a number. So `=ka-deva/1.4` reads ka-deva even when a glyph `ka` exists. |
