| `metricsKey.decimalOperands` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | When no leading part names a glyph, the key is unlinked and names the shortest leading part after which the rest is valid: `=xa-deva-5` names `xa-deva`. |
| `metricsKey.resultRounded` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Exact halves round up (toward positive infinity), as both oracles round kerning values: fontc 1.0.0 and fontmake 3.12.1 write −10.5 as −10 and 10.5 as 11. |
| `metricsKey.constant` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | On a glyph that is not aligned automatically, `=-30` and `=+30` are constants; reports on the reference forum treat `=-30` as a key that should work (thread "Build 1015 metrics keys"). On a glyph aligned automatically the form is the offset of `metricsKey.autoAlignedOffset`, evaluated from M6. |
| `metricsKey.glyphLevelByDefault` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A layer that is not a master layer (intermediate, alternate, backup) reads the referenced glyph's layer with the same layer id, else the layer of its own master; no source says. |
| `metricsKey.localPrefix` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | On its layer a local key wins over the glyph-level key of the same field. |
| `metricsKey.localPrefix` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A plain number typed into a field removes the key the field showed: a local key from the layer, a glyph-level key from the glyph. |
| `metricsKey.baselineOffset` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | When the line at the given height does not cross the outline, the key uses the bounding-box sidebearing; no source says. |
| `metricsKey.baselineOffset` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In a master with an italic angle, the crossing at the given height is moved along the italic angle to half the x-height, where the bounding-box sidebearings are measured; no source says. |
| `metricsKey.autoAlignedDisplay` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A sidebearing that holds an offset key shows the key followed by the derived value in parentheses (`=+18 (68)`) instead of the word auto; an aligned glyph with an offset in the reference app settles it. |
| `metricsKey.autoAlignedDisplay` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Emptying an aligned field that holds an offset removes the offset; the reference app with an offset deleted from the field settles it. |
| `metricsKey.autoAlignedOffset` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Offsets apply to the left and right sidebearing keys only: a left offset moves every component right and widens the glyph, a right offset widens it; an offset typed into the width field of an aligned glyph is refused, and one stored there by a file is ignored; entering =+N in the width field of an aligned glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.highlightColors` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The tint fills the gap between the two advances, from the descender to the ascender, behind the glyphs; a screenshot of the reference app in text mode settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A typed 0 is stored as a pair with value 0, which overrides a group value; both oracles keep such a pair (a 0 exception against a −60 group pair compiles to 0). Typing 0 in the reference app and reading the Kerning window settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A typed value with a fraction is rounded to a whole unit; values read from files keep their fractions. Typing a fraction in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Emptying the field removes the stored pair that applies, which can be a group pair that other glyph pairs share; emptying a kern field kerned through groups in the running app and reading the Kerning window settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Up and Down in an empty field step from zero and create the pair; pressing Up in a field showing the placeholder in the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.shortcutsNeedTextTool` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A keypress changes the kerning that applies by the step and stores the result on the sides the locks choose, creating that pair when it does not exist; no source says, and the Kerning window after the first keypress on an unkerned pair settles it. |
| `kerning.shortcutModifierScheme` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In right-to-left text Ctrl picks the pair with the glyph that is visually on the left and Cmd the pair with the glyph visually on the right; the handbook does not mention right-to-left text here, and watching the shortcuts on right-to-left text settles it. |
| `kerning.verticalGroups` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | M5 stores and edits vertical pairs and shows them in top-to-bottom text; export writes none, as neither oracle does. |
| `kerning.verticalGroups` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A vertical group side is written `@MMK_T_` with the upper glyph's bottom group and `@MMK_B_` with the lower glyph's top group, by analogy with the left-to-right prefixes; a vertical group pair saved by the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.groupMembersDisplayScope` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Each member is placed so its facing side lines up with the glyph it stands in for (left sides at the glyph's origin, right sides at the end of its advance); vertical members share the glyph's origin. Comparing members of different widths in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.groupMembersDisplayScope` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The current glyph at the start of a line has no predecessor, so only its own group shows; the reference app with the cursor at a line start settles it. |
| `kerning.lockStates` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A lock is open when the pair that applies names that glyph itself on that side while the glyph has a group there; opening a lock stores such a pair at the value that applied, so the kerning does not change. The kern field and the Kerning window right after a lock is opened, in the running app or a video, settle it. |
| `kerning.closingLockRemovesException` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Closing a lock deletes the exception pair in the current master only; the all-masters variant exists only for deletion in the Kerning window. Closing a lock in a font with two masters and reading the other master's pairs settles it. |
| `kerning.closingLockRemovesException` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Closing one lock while both are open keeps the other lock open: the glyph–glyph pair is replaced by the closed side's group against the open side's glyph, at the value that applied, unless that pair is already stored. Closing one lock of such a pair in the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.closingLockRemovesException` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Closing the left lock alone of a glyph–glyph pair is refused when the left glyph's pair with the right glyph's group exists, because that pair would apply before the group–glyph pair and the left lock would stay open. The owner chose the refusal over deleting that exception. Closing the left lock in that situation in the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.pairLookupOrder` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | When two stored pairs name the same sides in different spellings (the reference's group prefix and the UFO's), the first in stored order applies; a source with both, opened in the reference app, settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The defaults are contains, every entry kind, both sides and not limited to the Font View selection; Reset returns to them. The options menu of a newly opened Kerning window settles them. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A pair is listed when one of the searched sides matches the term, has the chosen entry kind and, with the selection option, is a selected glyph or a group one of them belongs to; a search on a running Kerning window with mixed pairs settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Does not contain keeps a pair only when no searched side contains the term; a search for a letter that occurs on one side of a pair settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The term is compared with the bare name of a group, and with its @ spelling when the term starts with @; searching for @ in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The search is case-sensitive, since glyph names are; searching for t in a font with T settles it. |
| `kerning.windowEntryColors` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A selected row draws its entries in the selection's text colour instead of blue and gold; a screenshot of a selected row in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSorting` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Before a header is clicked the list keeps the stored order of the pairs. |
| `kerning.windowSorting` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Names sort byte by byte in their @ spelling and equal entries keep the stored order in both directions; sorting a list with repeated values in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSelectionDrivesEditView` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A group side is shown by its first member in glyph order. |
| `kerning.windowInPlaceEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Return or leaving the cell confirms and Escape cancels, as in the Info box fields; a glyph name cell is not editable. Editing cells in the reference settles both. |
| `kerning.copyPasteBetweenMasters` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A paste goes into the writing direction the window shows, whatever direction the copied text names; copying from a left-to-right list and pasting into a right-to-left one in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.copyPasteBetweenMasters` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The list keeps Copy and Paste from the Edit menu until another field takes the keyboard or a click lands outside the window and the menus; choosing Edit › Copy in the reference after clicking the list, then after clicking the canvas, settles it. |
| `kerning.compressRules` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Within one run the pairs are taken in stored order and each sees the lifts made before it, so when two pairs would lift to the same group pair the first is lifted and the second meets it (absorbed with an equal value, kept as an exception with another); running Compress on such a pair list in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.compressRules` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph–group pair is not lifted or absorbed while group–glyph pairs stored for members of its group would take over those glyphs once it is gone; a pair list with T–@n and @T–m in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.compressRules` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A later pair with the same sides as an earlier one (one group in two spellings, or two entries for one master) never applies, so it is removed together with the earlier pair when that one is lifted or absorbed; compressing such a pair list in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.compressOneStepPerRun` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A run lifts the left side of every pair that can lift it, else its right side; one run on a pair list where both sides can lift, in the reference app, settles it. |
| `kerning.implicitKernFeature` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The generated lookups follow fontc's kern writer (split by script and direction, glyph pairs before class pairs, values rounded half up); the handbook gives no rules, and the GPOS of a font the reference app exports from a test source settles it. |
| `kerning.implicitKernFeature` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Until feature code is compiled (M8), a top-level include in enabled feature code leaves the generated kerning out with a warning naming the file, because the file may hold a kern feature; following the include would settle it, and an export from the reference app of a source whose included file has no kern feature shows what it writes. |
| `kerning.groupClassNames` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Only the `@MMK_L_`/`@MMK_R_` classes the code refers to and no class entry defines are written, before the first entry, with the glyphs the target exports in font order, and a group without such glyphs stays undefined so the compiler names the reference; the expanded code of a reference export settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The glyph box hangs under the current glyph's descender, centred on its advance, separate from the selection box at the foot of the canvas; a screenshot of the reference app with a selection settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Emptying a field that holds a key stores the metric's current number, so the key goes and nothing moves; emptying such a field in the reference app settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | A key that cannot be evaluated (a cycle, a division by zero, a result out of range) is drawn red like an out-of-sync key, without the update button; a cyclic key in the reference app settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | A master that takes its metrics from another master shows that master's values read only, with a line naming it; a linked master in the reference app settles it. |
| `editView.kerningModeButton` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The button sits between the writing direction buttons and the zoom controls; the reference app's bottom bar settles it. |
| `infoBox.kerning` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | A side whose glyph has no kerning group there shows its lock disabled, since opening it has nothing to make an exception from; the Info box on an ungrouped glyph in the running app settles it. |
| `infoBox.kerning` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | In right-to-left text the kern field, lock and group at the left edge belong to the pair with the glyph drawn on the left, the next glyph in logical order; a right-to-left tab in the running app settles it. |
| `infoBox.precedingGlyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The group in the preceding glyph's box is an editable field that sets the group facing the current glyph (the right group in left-to-right text); clicking it in the running app settles it. |
| `infoBox.precedingGlyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The box is not shown when no glyph comes before the current one on its line; placing the cursor at the start of a line in the running app settles it. |
| `dialog.renameKerningGroup` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The buttons read All Pairs, This Pair and Cancel; the handbook gives no captions, and a screenshot or video of the dialog settles them. |
| `dialog.renameKerningGroup` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | All Pairs (scope font) is the default button that Return presses, since editing a group name most often means renaming the group; the dialog in the reference app settles it. |
| `dialog.pasteKerningConflict` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The warning has a Cancel button besides Overwrite and Keep Existing. |
| `dialog.pasteKerningConflict` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Overwrite is the default button that Return presses; the warning in the reference app settles it. |
| `dialog.pasteKerningConflict` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The warning lists the conflicting pairs with the pasted and the current value; the handbook names no list, and a screenshot of the warning settles it. |

## `09-reusing-shapes.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | The components are the full Unicode canonical decomposition of the glyph's code point, each code point mapped to its glyph name through the glyph database (U+0301 to acutecomb), suffixes carried over and the preferred-mark rules applied, plus our own table for glyphs Unicode does not decompose (i, j and ligatures by their name parts); the composites stored in corpus files saved by the reference app measure it (M6 plan § 5). |
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | A glyph the database cannot decompose, or one whose parts are missing from the font, is refused with a message naming the missing parts and nothing changes; running it on such a glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | The hints that pointed at nodes of the replaced shapes (stems, corner and cap components) are removed with them, and hints without node references stay; running it on a glyph with corner components in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | Create Composite keeps the layer's anchors and the glyph's metrics keys; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.addComponent` | M6 | handbook | yes | The component is appended at offset zero, after every other shape, and the layer's automatic alignment places it; a component that would make the glyph contain itself is refused; adding a component in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | The suggested name is `_part.` plus the current glyph's name, with `.001`, `.002` appended while the name is taken; the dialog of the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | A name that is already a glyph is refused with a message; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | The new glyph keeps the shapes at their coordinates and the component sits at offset zero; anchors stay on the original layer; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | On each other master the shapes with the same indices move into the new glyph's layer of that master, and a master whose layer lacks them gets an empty layer; a two-master font in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | Without a name the command takes the suggested one; each layer of the new glyph takes the width of the original glyph's layer of its master; the reference app's new glyph settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | Only master layers are split: a non-master layer is refused, and images in the selection are refused; the reference app with a brace layer or an image selected settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | Hints move with the paths they point at, and a hint that links a moved and a kept path is removed; a hinted glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `component.move` | M6 | handbook | yes | A move of an aligned or locked component is refused with a message rather than silently left without effect (the font stays the same either way); a component aligned horizontally only takes the vertical part, and a purely horizontal move of it is refused; dragging such components in the reference app settles whether it shows a message. |
| `component.setAnchor` | M6 | handbook | yes | The anchor menu lists the anchors of the attachment target whose name is the mark anchor's name without its underscore or that name followed by an underscore and a suffix; when the stored name no longer exists, the component attaches to the plain name and keeps the stored value; the reference app settles it. |
| `component.setAnchor` | M6 | handbook | yes | The command accepts only names the component can attach with: one of its own underscore anchors without the underscore (`top` for `_top`), or that name followed by an underscore and a suffix (`top_viet`, `top_2`); an empty name clears the choice; the anchor menu of the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.listComposites` | M6 | inferred | yes | `recursive` adds glyphs that use the composites, to the component depth limit; it has no counterpart in the reference app. |
| `component.enableAutomaticAlignment` | M6 | handbook | yes | Enable stores 0 (decided by the layer) on a layer of components only and 1 (forced) on a layer that has paths, so the item always aligns the component; enabling alignment on a component next to a path in the reference app settles it. |
| `component.lock` | M6 | handbook | yes | Lock Component is offered only for components that are not aligned automatically, and the command refuses an aligned component with a message; the context menu of an aligned component in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyph` | M6 | handbook | yes | The anchors that decomposition brings in are the propagated anchors the layer lacks, so mark attachment is unchanged; decomposing a composite with propagated anchors in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyph` | M6 | handbook | yes | Decomposed paths take the component's place in the shape order and the layer's hints are renumbered to keep their nodes; a hinted composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyph` | M6 | handbook | yes | A base glyph whose corner components cannot be fitted is decomposed without them, as the editor draws it, with a warning; the reference app with a broken corner settles it. |
| `component.decompose` | M6 | handbook | yes | Decomposing one level adds no anchors to the layer; a smart component becomes its interpolated outline; corner, cap, segment and brush components inside the base glyph are applied before its paths are copied. Decomposing such components in the reference app settles it. |
| `component.decompose` | M6 | handbook | yes | A smart component that cannot be interpolated is decomposed from its base layer, as the editor draws it, with a warning; the reference app with incompatible pole layers settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.addProperty` | M6 | handbook | yes | A name another property of the glyph already has is refused; adding a second property with the same name in the reference app's Properties tab settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.setProperty` | M6 | handbook | yes | A rename carries the layers' poles and the values stored on every component of the glyph to the new name, and anchors named after the property keep their names; renaming a property in the reference app's settings window and saving settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.removeProperty` | M6 | inferred | yes | Removing a property drops its pole on every layer and leaves values stored on components in place and ignored; a minus button in the Properties tab of the reference app settles it. |
| `smartComponent.setValue` | M6 | handbook | yes | A new smart component stores no values and draws with the poles of its master layer, as glyphsLib does with a missing value; a property removed from the glyph leaves the stored value in place and ignored; adding a smart component in the reference app and saving settles it. |
| `smartComponent.setValue` | M6 | handbook | yes | A locked component refuses value changes; changing a value on a locked smart component in the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.add` | M6 | handbook | yes | A node that already holds a corner gets the new corner in its place, unscaled and aligned left, rather than a second corner; adding a corner to a node with a corner in the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.add` | M6 | handbook | yes | An off-curve node and the first or last node of an open path are refused, because the fitting needs a segment on each side; the context menu on an open path's end node in the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.decompose` | M6 | handbook | yes | A corner on an extra node, or one whose corner glyph does not exist, is refused instead of being removed without a trace; the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.setScale` | M6 | handbook | yes | A scale of 0 percent is refused with a message, because it collapses the corner to a point; typing 0 in the reference app's scale field settles it. |
| `components.liveUpdateFromBaseGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Re-alignment after an edit touches only the masters whose layers the edit changed, and on other masters only the layers whose components are pinned to one of them (and the composites built on those layers), so stale stored positions on other masters stay as the file has them; editing a base on one master in the reference app and reading the other masters' composites settles it. |
| `components.liveUpdateFromBaseGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A locked composite is not re-aligned when its base changes, because a lock keeps the glyph's drawing; locking a composite in the reference app and editing its base settles it. |
| `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The Unicode decomposition is expanded fully (Aringacute is A, ringcomb, acutecomb), except that a letter with a horn stays whole when the font has it (Ohornacute is Ohorn, acutecomb); the composites stored in corpus sources saved by the reference app measure it (M6 plan § 5). |
| `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Our exceptions to Unicode: a name containing commaaccent takes commaaccentcomb where Unicode has the cedilla, gcommaaccent takes commaturnedabovecomb, and dcaron, lcaron, Lcaron and tcaron prefer caroncomb.alt; composing these in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Two marks in a row become their mark ligature (circumflexcomb_gravecomb, with the same suffix preferences) when the font has it; composing Acircumflexgrave in the reference app with the mark ligature present settles it. |
| `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A base of a decomposition takes the composite's suffix and is required (agrave.sc needs a.sc), while a ligature part takes the suffix only when the font has the suffixed glyph (f_f_l.ss01 from f.ss01 and l); composing suffixed glyphs in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A glyph whose parts are added by the same Add Glyphs text is built from them; adding eacute and acutecomb together in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Add to Font in the Glyph Info window composes as Add Glyphs does; adding a composite from that window in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoComposeNeedsAllParts` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The refusal names every missing part once: a mark by its plain name, a base by the name it needed (a.sc for agrave.sc); the message of the reference app settles it. |
| `components.preferMarkWithSameSuffix` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | For an uppercase composite with a suffix the marks are tried as the suffixed mark, the .case mark, then the plain mark (Agrave.ss01: gravecomb.ss01, gravecomb.case, gravecomb); composing it in the reference app with all three present settles it. |
| `components.preferNarrowMarksForIJ` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A mark ending in .i is tried before one ending in .narrow, and both only on the dotless lowercase i and j, not on I or J; composing imacron in the reference app with both marks present settles it. |
| `components.ijBuiltFromDotless` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Composites lose the dot of i and j only before a mark above (Unicode combining class 230), so iogonek is i plus ogonekcomb; a suffixed i or j is composed only when the font has the suffixed dotless letter; composing iogonek and i.sc in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.recipeBasePlusMarks` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A part whose glyph has category Mark is a mark and attaches to the base before it; any other part is a base; a recipe holds only glyph names, no spaces (spaces separate items in Add Glyphs) and any number of plus signs; typing recipes with code points or spaces into the reference app settles it. |
| `components.recipeBasePlusMarks` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The first part of a recipe is a base even when its glyph is a mark (dotaccentcomb+dotaccentcomb=dieresiscomb sets the second on the first), and the category stored in the font wins over the database's; typing such a recipe in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.underscoreNameNotExported` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Renaming a glyph to or from an underscore name leaves its exports flag alone; renaming in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.tabCyclesComponents` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Tab steps through the layer's nodes in path order, then the components in shape order, then the anchors, then the guides; Tab on a layer with components and anchors in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.infoBoxExtraControls` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The arrow button sits at the top right of the component Info box, as the Measuring page says; the Components page says top left. A screenshot of the reference app settles it. |
| `components.swapBaseKeepsPlacement` (behavior) | M6 | inferred | – | Swapping the base glyph keeps the component's slot in the shape order, its transform, its alignment setting and its stored attachment anchor; swapping a component in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.flipViaNegativeScaleOrMirror` (behavior) | M6 | inferred | – | The mirror buttons and a negative scale in the Info box give the same stored transform; mirroring a component in the reference app and saving settles it. |
| `components.rubberBandIgnoresComponents` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A rubber band with Option takes a component when an on-curve point of its outline lies inside, not only when the whole component does; dragging across half a component with Option in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.rubberBandIgnoresComponents` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Option in a rubber band both includes components and leaves handles out, since the handbook gives Option both meanings; dragging with Option over a path with handles and a component in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.doubleClickOpensBase` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The base glyph goes into the tab text to the left of the composite, as the Info box section of the Measuring page says; the Components page says only next to it. Double-clicking a component in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.lockedCannotMove` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A lock blocks Info box position edits and the Palette's transformations as well as moves, and leaves the component's other settings editable; a locked component in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.placeholderEmptyBase` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A placeholder is a crossed box the width of the base glyph's advance and half an em tall, labelled with the problem; seeing the three placeholders in the reference app settles their look. |
| `components.placeholderCircularReference` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Anchor propagation reports a glyph on or above a component cycle as an error, where glyphsLib gives glyphs on the cycle their own anchors and glyphs above it none; a cycle in the reference app settles what it shows. |
| `components.nodesInsideComponentsSnap` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The nodes inside components show for as long as any drag of a pointer tool lasts, and not while the pointer only hovers; dragging a node next to a component in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.topBottomAnchorsPropagate` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The editor and the compiler propagate anchors exactly as fontc does, every name included, since its algorithm gives top and bottom as the handbook says and also the other names both reference compilers export; a composite that inherits an ogonek or a cedilla anchor in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.topBottomAnchorsPropagate` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A non-master layer whose base glyph has no layer with the same id reads the base's master layer of the same master, where glyphsLib interpolates the base's anchors at the layer's axis location; a brace layer in the reference app whose base lacks it settles it. |
| `components.topBottomAnchorsPropagate` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A base glyph without a layer for the composite's master passes no anchors on, where glyphsLib stops with an error; the reference app with such a font settles it. |
| `components.topBottomAnchorsPropagate` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A component pinned to another master (`masterId`) passes on the anchors of that master's layer of its base, the layer alignment places it from; a composite with a pinned component in the reference app, read through a mark attached to it, settles it. |
| `components.topBottomAnchorsPropagate` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A smart component whose pole layers' outlines cannot be interpolated still has its anchors interpolated from the anchors those layers propagate, where glyphsLib stops with an error; the reference app with such a smart glyph settles it. |
| `components.underscoreAnchorSnapsToPlainAnchor` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A component whose underscore anchors find no plain anchor among the components before it is set after them as another base, as if typed; a mark added to a base without the matching anchor in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.alternativeAnchorSuffix` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The component's anchor attribute is matched by its plain name, the part before the first underscore or period, against the mark's underscore anchor; the target is the attribute's anchor, else for a numbered name (top_2) the plain anchor of that base, else the plain anchor; a ligature with a mark set to top_1 after its second base in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoAlignDefaultOnWhenComponentsOnly` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A component that is not aligned keeps its stored position but still counts: its anchors serve the marks after it and, as a base, it moves the next base to follow its stored place; a composite with one disabled base among aligned ones in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.fontWideDisableAutoAlign` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The font-wide option also overrides components whose alignment is forced (1); a forced component in a font with the option on, in the reference app, settles it. |
| `components.importedFilesDisableAutoAlignSetting` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The setting works through the font's own Disable Automatic Alignment option, which stays on and is saved with the font, rather than by setting each component's alignment to disabled; the Font Info of a UFO opened with the setting on, in the reference app, settles it. |
| `components.autoAlignColors` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Components are shown by their outline drawn in the state colour, not filled; a composite in the reference app's Edit View settles it. |
| `components.autoAlignColors` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Every component aligned horizontally only is blue, the single Number component and a component set to horizontal alignment alike; a component set to horizontal alignment in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.singleNumberComponentShiftsVertically` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A Number-category single component with alignment 0 behaves as alignment 3, and alignment 3 gives the same horizontal-only alignment to any component; the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks the stored positions of both. |
| `components.autoAlignMetricsFromBase` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A layer with a path keeps its stored width even when it holds forced components, which are still placed; a forced component next to a path in the reference app, with the base's width changed, settles it. |
| `components.autoAlignMetricsOffsetKeys` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Other keys already stored on an aligned layer, from a file or from before the glyph was aligned, are kept but ignored: not evaluated, not listed as out of sync, not applied by Update Metrics; an aligned composite with a key such as =a opened in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoAlignMetricsOffsetKeys` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The spacing shortcuts of the Text tool skip glyphs aligned on the edited layer and list them, since alignment would put their components back; pressing Ctrl+Left on an aligned composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.autoAlignSingleComponent` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A single aligned component keeps its transform; the composite takes the base glyph's width times the length of the component's transformed x axis (so a scaled component gives a scaled width), and a horizontally flipped component is shifted so its outline keeps the base glyph's sidebearings mirrored (nine from six keeps six's width); the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks the flipped single components the sources hold. |
| `components.autoAlignBaseThenMarks` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The first base sits at x = 0 and y = 0 and keeps its scale, slant and rotation; only its offset is computed; a base flipped vertically keeps its stored vertical offset, since y = 0 would hang it below the baseline; the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks it. |
| `components.marksStackInOrder` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A mark's underscore anchor attaches to the anchor of that name the components before it give, as propagation computes it after each component (the most recent component that offers the name wins); a composite with two stacked marks and a third in the reference app settles it. |
| `components.multipleBasesSetSideBySide` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Each base after the first is placed at the sum of the advance widths before it, with no kerning, and the composite's width is the sum of the bases' widths; right-to-left glyphs are laid out the same way, in shape order; the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks it on the sources' ligatures. |
| `components.marksBelongToPrecedingBase` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A mark looks for its anchor only on the nearest base before it and the marks attached since, never on an earlier base; a ligature whose second base lacks the mark's anchor, in the reference app, settles it. |
| `components.exitEntryAnchorsConnectBases` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Only the #-prefixed anchors connect components; the plain exit and entry anchors serve cursive attachment alone; the composite's width is the connected bases' widths after the shift, ending at the last base's advance. The corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks it where sources use it. |
| `export.underscoreGlyphsOmitted` (behavior) | M2 | inferred | – | Components that use non-exporting glyphs are decomposed at export; our inference from the non-export rule. |
| `smart.widthHeightFollowBoundingBox` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A box drag sets the value whose extent matches the dragged box, read linearly between the extents at the property's two ends, unclamped and rounded like other box values; dragging a knob in the reference app settles it. |
| `smart.widthHeightFollowBoundingBox` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | With several objects selected, a smart component among them scales like any component; scaling a smart component together with a path in the reference app settles it. |
| `smart.widthHeightFollowBoundingBox` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Rotating a smart component from outside its box rotates it, since the handbook rules out only resizing; trying it in the reference app settles it. |
| `smart.handlesFromPropertyAnchors` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Dragging a handle sets the value whose interpolated anchor position is nearest the pointer, found by projecting the pointer on the line between the anchor's positions at the property's two poles, with no clamp; dragging a handle in the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.glyphNaming` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A layer with a corner that cannot be fitted (its path deleted, a zero-length segment next to it) is drawn in the editor without any of its corners, as the export leaves it; a hint whose path was deleted in the reference app shows what it draws. |
| `corner.glyphNaming` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Corners are fitted on the layer's stored shapes, whose indices the hints name, before its smart components are drawn; fontc draws smart components first and then reads the indices against the shapes that result, which differs only in a layer that has both. A layer with a smart component before a path with a corner, built in the reference app, settles it. |
| `corner.highlightAndSelection` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The tint is a disc around the corner's node rather than a fill of the corner's own outline; a corner component in the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.copyPasteOntoNodes` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Pasting a corner, cap, segment or brush onto a selection of the wrong kind (a corner with a segment selected, a cap on nodes that are not neighbours) is refused with a message and nothing changes; the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.copyPasteOntoNodes` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | One copied corner goes onto every selected node; several copied corners go onto as many selected nodes, in order, and any other count is refused. Copying two corners and pasting them onto three nodes in the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.mirrorViaPalette` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Mirroring negates the scale only and keeps the corner's alignment mode; mirroring a corner aligned left in the reference app and reading its Info box settles it. |
| `corner.entryAnchor` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The entry anchor slides the fitted corner along its own baseline until the anchor lies on the incoming stroke, and the corner is then fitted as without it; the reference app's output for `av_left_anchoroffpath` agrees, and a left anchor on a curved stem in the reference app settles the rest. |
| `corner.entryAnchor` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A corner path faces right when its second node lies right of its start node; it then takes `right` as its entry anchor and `left` as its exit anchor. A right-facing corner glyph with both anchors in the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.entryAnchor` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A mirrored corner (one negative scale) ignores its entry and exit anchors; a mirrored corner with a left anchor in the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.exitAnchor` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The exit anchor stands in for the end node when the corner is rotated and when the outgoing segment is cut (where it is nearest to the anchor); the end node stays where it is drawn and the outgoing segment runs on from it. A cupped serif in the reference app settles it. |
| `corner.scaleInterpolatesWithIntermediateLayers` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The percentages are read from the intermediate layer's name (for example `Width 150 Height 100`, a missing one is 100); on each axis the layers that vary it with the other axis at 100 % are interpolated piecewise linearly with the master layer at 100 %, scales beyond them are extrapolated, negative scales mirror after interpolation, and layers that vary both axes are not used. A corner glyph saved by the reference app with such a layer settles it. |
| `corner.extraNodesAcceptCorners` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The two crossing segments are stored with the earlier path first (then the earlier node); a source saved by the reference app with a corner on an extra node settles which segment comes first. |
| `cap.node1Node2AnchorSetup` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | With node1 and node2 anchors the cap is mapped by the similarity transform that sends node1 onto the first host node and node2 onto the second (uniform scale, rotation, translation); settled by exporting `_cap.round` on the 80- and 120-unit stems. |
| `brush.startEndAnchors` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | Set Anchors puts a brush glyph's start at (0, 0) and its end at (advance width, 0); Set Anchors on a brush glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `pixel.requiresGridSpacingTwoOrMore` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | With a grid spacing below 2 a click of the Pixel tool shows a message naming Font Info › Other › Grid Spacing and changes nothing; the reference app settles it. |
| `pixel.usesGlyphNamedPixel` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Pixel components are written with alignment off (-1), since a layer of components only would otherwise be aligned automatically; a pixel glyph saved by the reference app settles it. |
| `pixel.usesGlyphNamedPixel` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The pixel glyph exports like any glyph whose name has no underscore, and pixel components export as components (TrueType) or outlines (CFF) by the usual rules; a pixel font exported by the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.showGlyphsUsingAsComponent` | M6 | handbook | no | The glyphs open in a new Edit View tab, one per line slot in glyph order; the reference app settles it. |
| `capComponent.add` | M13 | handbook | no | The cap's open path replaces the host segment between the two nodes and is joined to the path at both ends, as a corner's open path replaces part of the host outline; settled by exporting a cap test font from the reference app and comparing the outline. |
| `capComponent.decompose` | M13 | handbook | no | Decompose Cap writes the fitted outline into the host path in place of the cap and keeps the node order of the host before the cap; settled by decomposing a cap in the reference app and comparing node order with the exported outline. |
| `capComponent.setFit` | M13 | handbook | no | With Fit on, the cap is scaled along the stroke-end direction by the ratio of the host's node distance to the cap's reference width (the distance between node1 and node2, or the cap's advance width with only an origin anchor); its other direction is not scaled. Settled by the Fit export check. |
| `capComponent.setScale` | M13 | handbook | no | The cap Info box has the corner Info box's horizontal and vertical scale fields, applied after fitting and before placement; settled by the Info box check. |
| `capComponent.setAlignment` | M13 | handbook | no | For a cap, left aligns to the incoming segment of the first node, right to the outgoing segment of the second, both to their mean direction and none keeps the cap unrotated; settled by exporting the slanted-stem cap with each mode. |
| `segmentComponent.add` | M13 | handbook | no | On a straight host segment the segment glyph's path is scaled along the baseline so start lands on the first node and end on the second, and is not scaled across; settled by the straight-segment export check. |
| `segmentComponent.add` | M13 | handbook | no | On a curved host segment each point of the segment glyph, handles included, is moved to the host curve at the same fraction of its length and offset along the curve's normal by its distance from the baseline; settled by the curved-segment export check. |
| `brush.add` | M13 | handbook | no | The brush outline between start and end follows the host segment as segments do; the parts before start and after end are moved rigidly with the host's direction at its two ends; settled by the brush export check. |
| `brush.add` | M13 | handbook | no | With nodes selected, each selected node becomes the end node of a brush on the segment that ends there; a node at the start of an open path gets no brush. Settled by the node-selection check. |
| `pixel.setPixels` | M6 | inferred | no | A pixel sits at the cell's lower left corner, at multiples of the grid spacing from the origin, and the tool finds an existing pixel by its exact offset; the reference app settles it. |

## `10-interpolation.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `compareFonts.useLeft` | M9 | handbook | yes | For a glyph row, Use Left copies the master layer's shapes, anchors and width; the copy is one undo step in the receiving document only. Settle in the reference app's Compare Fonts window. |
| `compareFonts.useLeft` | M9 | handbook | yes | A glyph the receiving font lacks is added with the giving glyph's code points that are still free; a glyph or metric the giving side lacks is refused rather than deleted on the other side. Settle with Use Left on a glyph present in one font only. |
| `compareFonts.useLeft` | M9 | handbook | yes | A font-info or kerning row the giving side has no value for removes the value on the receiving side. Settle with Use Left on a kerning pair present in one master only. |
| `compareFonts.diff` | M9 | inferred | yes | Only rows whose values differ are listed. Settle by opening Compare Fonts on two masters that share most values. |
| `compareFonts.diff` | M9 | inferred | yes | Kerning rows cover the left-to-right pairs only, not right-to-left or vertical kerning. Settle by comparing two fonts that differ only in right-to-left kerning. |
| `compareFamily.report` | M9 | inferred | yes | All lists a glyph with a non-empty value in any document; Ignore If Missing in One also requires such a value, Missing in One does not. Settle by filtering a family where a glyph absent from one document has no value in the others. |
| `axes.tagRules` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A tag of fewer than four printable ASCII characters is padded with spaces and a longer one exports its first four; settle by typing a three- and a five-character tag in the reference app's axis dialog and exporting. |
| `axes.unusedAxesDropped` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | An axis is dropped only when Disable Masters removes a master, and an axis a virtual master still varies is kept; settle by exporting F1 with a third axis all masters share, without Disable Masters, and again with a virtual master on that axis. |
| `masters.originAutomaticForStatic` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Static instances use the same variation model as the variable font, anchored at the variable origin; the result does not depend on the origin for the variable model. Settle by comparing instances of an irregular two-axis setup in the reference app (docs/plans/M9.md § 8). |
| `instances.namedInstancesInVariable` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Instances that do not export are left out of `fvar`, as the oracles do; settle by exporting F1 with one instance's export switch off and reading `fvar`. |
| `variableFontSetting.disableMasters` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A name that is not a master is ignored and reported as a warning instead of refusing the export; settle by naming a missing master in the reference app and exporting. |
| `compatibility.rules` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | An open path against a closed one with the same nodes is incompatible, images are left out of the shape order, and shapes the model keeps unread match each other. Settle by opening the Master Compatibility view on such layers in the reference app. |
| `compatibility.rules` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Anchors match by their set of names, in any order. Settle by giving two masters the same anchors in a different order and looking for the red indicator. |
| `compatibility.checkScope` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Masters needed by an instance are those with a non-zero scalar at the instance's location in the variation model; variable font settings count as needing every master they do not disable. Settle by observing the indicators with an instance between two of three masters. |
| `compatibility.checkScope` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | The scope is the union of the masters of every instance that needs two or more masters, rather than the pairs each instance needs; as compatibility is an equivalence the two differ only when two groups of masters share no instance. Settle with two instances, each between a different pair of four masters, and one pair incompatible with the other. |
| `intermediateLayer.mustBeCompatible` (behavior) | M9 | inferred | – | An intermediate layer is checked against every master layer of its glyph, not only its neighbours, because the variable font needs all of them compatible. Settle by observing the indicators with an incompatible intermediate layer between unrelated masters. |
| `virtualMaster.concept` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | On an axis a virtual master does not name, it sits at the origin's coordinate; settle by adding a virtual master that names one of two axes in the reference app and reading the exported `gvar` regions. |
| `alternateLayer.onMasterLayer` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A master layer with a range and no `[]` partner is both the master's normal shape and its alternate shape. Settle by exporting such a font from the reference app at a location inside the range. |
| `alternateLayer.onMasterLayer` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A `[]` layer whose master layer has no range is not a source. Settle by exporting such a font from the reference app and comparing the glyph with and without the `[]` layer. |
| `alternateLayer.rangeSemantics` (behavior) | M9 | inferred | – | Both bounds inclusive, as the oracles; the handbook does not say. Settle with an instance exactly on a bound in the reference app (docs/plans/M9.md § 8). |
| `alternateLayer.rangeSemantics` (behavior) | M9 | inferred | – | A range whose minimum is above its maximum is refused with a message naming the axis, rather than swapped or treated as empty; settle by typing a reversed range into the reference app's layer dialog and exporting. |
| `alternateLayer.staticExport` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | When several alternate sets contain the location, every one applies and the set with more bounds wins for a master both replace; settle with overlapping bracket ranges in the reference app. |
| `variable.originNeedsMinimalSetup` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | The check asks, for each side of the origin an axis range reaches, for a master or virtual master that differs from the origin on that axis alone, and virtual masters count; settle by exporting the handbook's three-master example with Black as the origin. |
| `axisMappings.controlPoints` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A mapping read from a file whose internal values neither rise nor fall throughout is ignored (identity) and reported as a warning; settle by giving the reference app such a mapping and exporting. |
| `axisMappings.controlPoints` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | The mapping editor accepts internal values that fall throughout as the external values rise (an axis whose internal coordinate shrinks as the user value grows), and refuses a map that rises in one part and falls in another; settle by entering a falling map in the reference app's Axis Mappings dialog. |
| `axisMappings.fontAndExportLevel` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A setting's Axis Mappings replaces the font's whole value, not axis by axis; settle by giving a setting a mapping for one axis of a two-axis font and reading `avar`. |
| `font.axes.reorder` | M9 | inferred | no | Reordering permutes every master, instance, virtual master and special-layer coordinate list with the axes, so no location changes; settle by dragging axes in the reference app's Font Info, if it allows it. |
| `file.generateInstances` | M9 | handbook | no | One new document per instance, as the Several Fonts section of the handbook says (a file per instance), holding one master with the instance's outlines, metrics, kerning and features and no special layers; settle by running the command in the reference app. |
| `interpolation.interpolateGlyph` | M9 | inferred | no | `coordinates: external` converts the location through Axis Location and Axis Mappings first; the default is internal, as the coordinate fields of the reference take internal values. |
| `interpolation.checkCompatibility` | M9 | inferred | no | The 20° warning measures the angle of each segment's chord from its start node to its end node; settle with the Master Compatibility view on a segment that turns by 19° and 21°. |
| `glyph.swapShapes` | M9 | inferred | no | A drag that ends on no other line end changes nothing; settle by observing the drag in the Master Compatibility view. |
| `view.showMasterCompatibility` | M9 | handbook | no | Shape tints cycle through eight theme colours by shape index; settle by observing the view with nine shapes. |
| `layers.setIntermediate` | M9 | handbook | no | Coordinates outside the axis range of the masters and virtual masters are refused with a message; settle by typing one in the reference app's popover. |
| `layers.clearIntermediate` | M9 | inferred | no | Unticking Intermediate in the layer's context menu runs this command; settle in the reference app's Layers palette. |
| `layers.setAlternate` | M9 | handbook | no | Choosing Alternate on a master layer with no `[]` partner under the same master adds no partner layer; the partner is made by hand as the handbook describes. Settle in the reference app. |
| `layers.clearAlternate` | M9 | inferred | no | Unticking Alternate in the layer's context menu runs this command; settle in the reference app's Layers palette. |
| `layers.reInterpolate` | M9 | inferred | no | The layer's own drawing is left out of the sources it is interpolated from; settle by observing an item of this name in the reference app's Layers palette, if it has one. |
| `layers.reInterpolate` | M9 | inferred | no | With no location given, a master layer uses its master's location and an intermediate layer its coordinates; a backup layer needs a location and is refused without one. |
| `axisMappings.setPoint` | M9 | handbook | no | A change that breaks the increasing order on either coordinate is refused with a message and changes nothing; settle by dragging a point past its neighbour in the reference app. |
| `axisMappings.nudgePoint` | M9 | handbook | no | The base step is 1 unit, Up and Down move the external value and Left and Right the internal value; settle in the reference app's Axis Mappings dialog. |
| `edit.compareFamily` | M9 | handbook | no | Each column shows the document's first master; settle by opening Compare Family on documents with several masters. |

## `11-filters.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `filter.repeatLastFilter` | M3 | handbook | yes | The last filter is the last filter.* or filter.apply run in the session journal, and it runs again silently with the same settings on the targets given. |
| `filter.apply` | M3 | inferred | yes | `args` as a list holds export-string items and as an object the filter command's parameters; the reference has only the export string. |
| `filter.apply` | M3 | inferred | yes | Without glyph or glyphs it runs on every glyph of the font, as export does. |
| `filter.extrude` | M3 | handbook | yes | The construction is ours: the swept shape is the original area plus the band each piece of a closed path sweeps. |
| `filter.extrude` | M3 | handbook | yes | The offset runs from 0 to 1000000 units and the angle from -360 to 360 degrees. |
| `filter.hatchOutline` | M3 | handbook | yes | The construction is ours: with offsetPath each line becomes a rectangle strokeWidth units thick intersected with the outline, and without it each piece of line inside the outline becomes an open two-node path. |
| `filter.hatchOutline` | M3 | handbook | yes | A layer that would need more than 10000 lines is an error. |
| `filter.offsetCurve` | M3 | handbook | yes | The offset construction is ours: corners stay sharp (mitred, bevelled beyond 4 times the offset), curves are offset as cubics within 0.25 units, and overlaps resolve by a union with the stroke of the paths. |
| `filter.offsetCurve` | M3 | handbook | yes | An open path without Make Stroke moves to the right of its direction, as the outward side of a counter-clockwise outer path does. |
| `filter.roughen` | M3 | handbook | yes | The fourth export value is read as the turn of the move box in degrees counter-clockwise (-360 to 360, default 0, 0 keeps x and y); the handbook names it without describing it. |
| `filter.roughen` | M3 | handbook | yes | The moves come from a deterministic generator (SplitMix64) seeded with `seed` (default 0), so one seed gives one result; the export string has no slot for the seed. |
| `filter.roughen` | M3 | handbook | yes | A segment of length L is cut into max(1, round(L / segmentLength)) equal pieces along the curve. |
| `filter.roundCorners` | M3 | handbook | yes | The rounding is ours: two smooth nodes joined by a cubic close to a circular arc, each radius / tan(a / 2) units from a corner of inner angle a, at most half of either neighbouring segment. |
| `filter.roundedFont` | M3 | handbook | yes | `stem` 0 means the master's widest vertical stem. |
| `filter.roundedFont` | M3 | handbook | yes | A stem ending is a straight segment between two outward corners whose neighbours are straight and parallel within 5°, at most the stem width plus 10% across and at most 20° off square; it becomes two quarter-circle cubics. |
| `glyph.transformMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Shifts and new widths are rounded to the font's grid, so an absolute sidebearing measured with a fraction lands within half a grid step of the entered value; watching the reference app on a glyph with a fractional extremum would settle it. |
| `glyph.transformMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | In sidebearing mode a layer without an outline is skipped and listed, because it has no sidebearings to set; running the dialog on the space glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.transformMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Metrics keys are left as they are, so a glyph whose key the command overrides then shows as out of sync; the reference app on a glyph with a key would settle it. |
| `glyph.transformMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A layer of a master that links its metrics to another master is changed like any other; the reference app on a linked master would settle it. |
| `path.transformations.copyPreFilterParameter` | M3 | observed | yes | It copies the same string as a PreFilter custom parameter, read from its label (11-filters open questions). |
| `path.interpolateWithBackground` | M3 | handbook | yes | An incompatible glyph is left unchanged and listed in `skipped` (11-filters open questions). |
| `path.removeOverlap` | M3 | handbook | yes | Every path operation runs each glyph layer under a 5 s time limit, single-glyph commands included; a layer that runs out of time is skipped with `glyphs` and an error with one `glyph` (docs/plans/M3.md › M3.6, rule 8). |
| `filter.target.visibleLayerOnly` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A filter changes the visible layer of each targeted glyph unless `layers` is given; the handbook says only "normally". |
| `filter.target.headlessExplicitTargets` (behavior) | M3 | inferred | – | Filters change paths only, not components, and a run over many glyphs is one undo step (11-filters open questions). |
| `filter.hatchOutline.origin` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | One hatch line passes through the origin (originX, originY). |
| `filter.hatchOutline.stepWidth` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | stepWidth is measured at right angles to the lines, from 1 to 1000000 units. |
| `filter.hatchOutline.angleConvention` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The angle is in degrees counter-clockwise from horizontal, from -360 to 360. |
| `filter.hatchOutline.openPathsWhenOffsetOff` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Each piece of line inside the outline is an open path of two line nodes, drawn in the direction of the angle. |
| `filter.offsetCurve.signConvention` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Outward is away from the filled side, read from nesting as Correct Path Direction does, so a path drawn the wrong way grows the same way. |
| `filter.offsetCurve.makeStroke` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Each side of a stroke reaches the full distances at position 50, so the stroke is twice the distance wide. |
| `filter.offsetCurve.autoStrokeKeepsVerticals` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Auto Stroke first squeezes the paths vertically about the middle of their bounds by the vertical distance at top and bottom, so the stroke ends at the original height; the method is ours. |
| `filter.offsetCurve.positionDistribution` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The left side reaches 2 · (1 − position) times the distances and the right side 2 · position times them. |
| `filter.offsetCurve.keepCompatible` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Keep Compatible moves each node to where the offset lines of its two segments meet and refits each pair of handles along the offset's directions, removing no overlap; the method is ours. |
| `filter.offsetCurve.capStyles` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The cap shapes are ours: flat cuts across the end, square extends by half the width, round is a half ellipse, round inset shortens the stroke by half its width before a round cap, and aligned cuts along the vertical or horizontal through the end node. |
| `filter.roughen.segmentsThenJitter` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Each segment is cut into equal pieces by length along the curve and the original nodes move too, so curves become polylines of line nodes. |
| `filter.roughen.maxOffsetPerAxis` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Each move is drawn uniformly from the box of plus or minus the bounds, turned by the angle, so the bounds are reached but never exceeded (11-filters open questions). |
| `filter.roundCorners.selectionRule` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Selected corners are selected on-curve nodes and both end nodes of selected segments (11-filters open questions). |
| `filter.roundCorners.selectionRule` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | An open path has no filled side, so with no selection all its corners are rounded. |
| `filter.roundCorners.visualCorrections` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | Visual corrections multiply the radius by tan(a / 2) for a corner of inner angle a, limited to between a quarter and four times the radius; the formula is ours. |
| `filter.roundedFont.usesVerticalStems` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The widest vertical stem sets the widest ending that is rounded, and with no vertical stem and no `stem` the command fails (11-filters open questions). |
| `filter.roundedFont.overshoot` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | The half circle covers the same area as the square end it replaces, so its tip stands w·(1 − π/4)/2 (about 0.107 w) past the old end; the amount is ours. |
| `glyph.transformMetrics.onBothSides` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | When half the change is not on the grid, the left side's half is rounded to the grid and the right side takes the rest; an odd width change in the reference app would settle it. |
| `filter.customParameter.thirdParty` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | FontCraft applies include and exclude itself for module filters, where the reference leaves it to each plug-in; settled by nothing in the reference, since our modules cannot see glyph lists. |
| `dialog.filterExtrude` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | First values: offset 50, angle -45 (down and to the right), Don't Subtract off. |
| `dialog.filterHatchOutline` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | First values: origin (0, 0), step width 20, angle 45, Offset Path on with thickness 5. |
| `dialog.filterOffsetCurve` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | First values: horizontal 10, vertical empty (the horizontal value), Make Stroke off, Auto Stroke off, position 50 %, Keep Compatible off, cap style flat. |
| `dialog.filterRoughen` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | First values: segment length 50, horizontal 10, vertical 10, angle 0. |
| `dialog.filterRoughen` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The Angle field is ours: the handbook's export string has an angle value, but its dialog has no such field. |
| `dialog.filterRoundCorners` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | First values: radius 20, visual corrections off. |
| `dialog.transformMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The dialog opens in sidebearing mode with LSB, RSB and Relative checked and both values 0, so opening it changes nothing; the reference app's first opening would settle it. |
| `dialog.transformMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The width field starts at half the units per em; the reference app's first opening would settle it. |
| `dialog.transformMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Each checkbox and its value field are two rows of the form ("LSB", "LSB value") instead of one row; observing the reference dialog would settle the layout. |
| `dialog.interpolateWithBackground` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | It opens at 50 %; the handbook gives no default. |

## `12-feature-code.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `font.features.reorder` | M8 | inferred | yes | Entries are dragged within their group, and the order of features is the order of feature blocks and of chained suffixes; the running app would settle whether dragging exists. |
| `font.features.expandCode` | M8 | inferred | yes | The expanded code is the assembled feature file for one master, includes inlined; the feature file the reference app writes when exporting would settle the layout. |
| `font.features.showSnippetFolder` | M8 | handbook | yes | The snippet folder is `snippets` inside FontCraft's settings folder (the folder of `settings.json`), created when Show Snippet Folder is chosen; the reference's folder is inside its application support folder, which the running app would show. |
| `font.expandTextTokens` | M7 | inferred | yes | Tokens that name nothing are left as written and listed; a name field with an unknown token exported from the running app would settle it. |
| `url.open` | M13 | inferred | yes | The command fails, changing nothing, only when the URL is malformed, over a limit or opens no file at all; a file that cannot be opened is skipped with a warning and the rest still open. Opening a show URL with one missing file and one existing file in the reference app would settle it. |
| `url.show` | M13 | inferred | yes | A tab holds one layer per glyph, so a glyph listed in several layers appears once per listing but every copy draws in the first layer given, with a warning; the reference shows each listed layer. Per-item layers in tabs would remove the difference. |
| `url.show` | M13 | inferred | yes | The new tab draws in the master of its first listed layer. Opening a show URL whose first glyph names a second master's layer in the reference app would settle it. |
| `featureEditor.outputOrder` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Classes come before prefixes, as both reference compilers assemble them; the expanded code of a reference export whose prefix uses a class would settle the reference app's own order. |
| `implicit.markerMissing` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Generated kerning follows a hand-written kern feature and a hand-written mark-family feature replaces the generated one, as glyphsLib and fontc do; a reference export of a font with a hand-written mark feature without the line would settle it. |
| `token.kinds` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A `$` inside a comment or a double-quoted string is left as it is, and any other `$` that starts no token is an error at it; a comment and a name string holding `$x` in a font exported from the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.numberValue.simple` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A master that stores no value for a number name reads 0; a source file whose master lacks one of the values, exported from the reference app, would settle it. |
| `token.numberValue.arithmetic` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Expressions that do not start with the name (`${2*padding}`, `${(a+b)/2}`, literals only) are accepted, as the feature compiler and the reference library accept them; typing one in the reference app would show whether it refuses them. |
| `token.numberValue.expressionDetails` (behavior) | M8 | docs | – | Results are rounded half away from zero, as fea-rs rounds, where glyphsLib truncates, and division by zero is a compile error at the token; an expression giving 2.5 exported from the reference app would settle the rounding. |
| `token.numberValue.inspect` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Option-click evaluates the token for the master of the active Edit View tab, else the first master; Option-clicking a token in a two-master font with the second master showing in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.glyphProperty.metrics` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | LSB, RSB, TSB and BSB are measured as the spacing commands measure them (slanted back in an italic master), and on a layer without an outline they are an error at the token; Option-clicking `${space:LSB}` and the token of an italic glyph in the reference app would settle both. |
| `token.glyphProperty.layerChoice` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Only the documented properties (width, LSB, RSB, TSB, BSB, anchors.<name>.x and .y) are accepted and any other is an error naming it; Option-clicking a token with another layer property in the reference app would show what else exists. |
| `token.predicate.boolean` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | isAligned holds when every master layer has a component placed automatically (a forced or horizontal component, or one left to the usual rule on a layer without paths), with the font setting off; a composite with one master unaligned in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.boolean` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | hasHints counts stem and TrueType hints on any layer and not corner components, caps, brushes or segments; a glyph with only a corner component in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.boolean` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | isColorGlyph holds for glyphs with colour palette layers only, and isAnyColorGlyph for every kind of colour layer; an SVG colour glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.logic` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | NOT binds tighter than AND, and AND tighter than OR, as in Apple's predicate format that the reference's language follows; this row's observe step would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.classFunction` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | In feature code a class name the font has no enabled class for is an error naming it, and in the Custom smart-filter rule it matches nothing; writing `class(Missing)` in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.classFunction` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Predicate tokens inside a class count non-exporting glyphs as members; only the token's own result leaves them out. A class built from a predicate in a font with a non-exporting match would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.resultOrder` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | The Edit View preview target also leaves out non-exporting glyphs, as export targets do; Option-clicking a predicate token in a font with a non-exporting match would settle it. |
| `featureCode.ifdefOtherSymbols` (behavior) | M9 | inferred | – | Only `#ifdef VARIABLE` and `#ifndef VARIABLE` blocks are read, without nesting or `#else`; an unterminated `#ifndef VARIABLE` runs to the end of the code, as glyphsLib reads it. Settle with nested blocks in the reference app. |
| `featureCode.conditionScope` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | The scope and coordinate rules follow glyphsLib's converter; settle with a feature holding two conditions and a bare `condition;` in the reference app. |
| `featureCode.standaloneLowering` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | The split-block lowering keeps the lookup's place in lookup order and leaves it out of the feature; a reference export of a feature with a standalone lookup, read back with a font tool, would settle it. |
| `featureCode.standaloneLowering` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A standalone lookup starts with the lookup flags in effect in its feature, as a named lookup inside a feature does in fea-rs, so the split lookup states them; a reference export of a standalone lookup after `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` would settle it. |
| `featureCode.multipleSubstitutionSingletons` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A single glyph or one-member class as the target of a rule whose replacements are larger classes is passed to the compiler, which refuses it: repeating the target gives several rules for one glyph. A reference export of `sub a by @B @C;` would settle what the reference does. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | All four classes are optional: Update refreshes them when the font has them and never adds them, because Oswald has lowercase letters and no Lowercase class, and Markow and Oswald Italic have capitals and no Uppercase class; Update on a font with capitals and no classes would settle it. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Uppercase and Lowercase hold the letters whose subcategory is Uppercase or Lowercase, and a glyph without a subcategory takes it from its case; Inter's stored Lowercase also holds 11 small capitals that store the category Letter and nothing else, which this rule leaves out. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | All leaves out .notdef and .null, as Inter's stored All does. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Which other classes Update offers would be settled in the running app. |
| `autoFeature.generalRule` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature name goes to liga when it is on the handbook's list or has `.liga`, to dlig with `.dlig`, to hlig when it contains longs or has `.hlig` or `.hist`, to rlig with `.rlig` or on the lam-alef list, and to dlig otherwise, the first match winning in that order; Update on a font with f_f_i.dlig and longs_t would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Only the scripts the locl code names get lines, as in every corpus source (Andika Mini, with Latin glyphs and no locl, has the DFLT line alone); Update on a font with Latin, Cyrillic and Greek glyphs and no locl would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | With several scripts, each script's group follows a blank line, in the order locl first names the scripts; Update on a font with .loclBGR and .loclTRK glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A language that locl names before any `script` statement gets a `languagesystem DFLT <language>;` line after the DFLT line. |
| `autoFeature.aalt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated as one `feature xxxx;` line per other feature with substitutions, in sidebar order, leaving the grouping to the compiler, as the corpus shows; cv features and features listed after aalt included. |
| `autoFeature.aalt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Left out: ccmp and all four ligature features (hlig and rlig by analogy with liga and dlig), and disabled features; Update on a font with hlig and a disabled ss01 would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated code starts with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;`, as the newer corpus files have it, and lists longer ligatures first; Update on a font with f_f_i, fi and a mark would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature goes to the first feature that claims it: its routing suffix, then the lam-alef set (rlig), then a longs component (hlig), then the default list (liga), else dlig; Update on a font with longs_t.liga would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature with another suffix joins components carrying that suffix (`sub f.sc i.sc by f_i.sc;`) and gets no rule when one is missing; Update on a font with f_i.sc would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A second ligature with the same component sequence in one feature gets no rule, and ligatures whose components are all marks get none; Update on a font with lam_alef-ar and lam_alef-ar.fina but no positional forms would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.dlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated like liga, with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` and longer ligatures first; Update on a font with f_odieresis would settle it. |
