| `autoFeature.hlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated like liga, with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` and longer ligatures first; Update on a font with such ligatures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated like liga, with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` and longer ligatures first; Update on a font with such ligatures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Ligatures of joining scripts take positional components where the font has them: `sub lam-ar.init alef-ar.fina by lam_alef-ar;` and `sub lam-ar.medi alef-ar.fina by lam_alef-ar.fina;`; Update on F2 would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2sc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | With a shared `.sc` set each capital maps to the small cap named after its lowercase letter (`sub A by a.sc;`), with a `.c2sc` set to its own suffix; Update on a font with A, a and a.sc would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2sc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A capital takes its own `.c2sc` form first, then a shared-set glyph on its own base (A.sc), then the shared-set glyph of its lowercase letter (a.sc); the case pair is found by code point, else by the name with its first letter changed. Update on a font with A.c2sc, A.sc and a.sc would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.smcp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.smcp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A `.sc` or `.smcp` glyph on an uppercase base (A.sc) serves smcp from the lowercase letter of the same character, after any glyph on a lowercase base; Update on a font with A.sc only would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2pc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2pc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Capitals are paired with petite capitals as c2sc pairs them with small capitals: `.c2pc` first, then `.pc` or `.pcap` on the capital, then on its lowercase letter. |
| `autoFeature.pcap` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.sups` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The `superior` ending is accepted on any base glyph, not only figures, since the handbook names letter forms such as ainferior too; a `.sups` glyph wins over a `superior` name for the same base. Update on a font with asuperior would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.subs` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `.subs` comes before `.sinf` and both before an `inferior` name when one glyph has several forms (sinf: `.sinf` first); Update on a font with a.subs and a.sinf would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.afrc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One ligature rule per nut fraction from the figures, slash and figures it names, in the handbook's list order; Update on a font with oneovertwo and oneoverten would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.frac` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | With `.numr` and `.dnom` figures and a fraction glyph, frac gets three lookups: slash between figures to fraction, figures to numerators, numerators after fraction or a denominator to denominators; Update on a font with both sets would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.frac` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The fallback takes the Unicode vulgar fractions in code point order, then the nut fractions in the handbook's order, one rule per input sequence; Update on a font with onehalf and oneovertwo would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.onum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Default and `.tf` figures map to `.osf` and `.tosf`, keeping proportional or tabular width; Update on a font with lf, tf, osf and tosf figures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.tnum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The unsuffixed glyph of a figure set takes the first style no suffixed form claims, in the order lf, osf, tf, tosf, decided per base name; every form maps to the form of the other width with the same height. Update on a font with lf, tf and osf figures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pnum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | pnum maps each tabular form to the proportional form of the same height, with the default figure placed as tnum describes; lines follow the glyph order of the suffixed glyph of each pair, as the corpus shows. |
| `autoFeature.lnum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `.osf` and `.tosf` figures map back to the default and `.tf` figures, keeping width; Update on a font with all four figure sets would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ordn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Letters missing from the font are left out of the `[A a]` and `[O o]` classes, and the numero rule needs N, o and period; Update on a font without capital A would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ornm` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `sub bullet from [A.ornm …];` with the ornaments in glyph order, then `sub A by A.ornm;` per letter whose base glyph exists; Update on a font with bullet and A.ornm would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hist` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.case` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Default figures that take an oldstyle style (by the tnum rule, for example when `.lf` forms exist and `.osf` forms do not) map to the lining figures of their width; the `.case` rule wins for a glyph that has both. Update on a font with one and one.lf would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.cpsp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The amount is 5 units per side, written `pos @Uppercase <5 0 10 0>;`; adding Capital Spacing in the reference app would settle the amount. |
| `autoFeature.cpsp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Without an Uppercase class entry the rule lists the capitals inline (`pos [A B] <5 0 10 0>;`); a capital is a glyph whose subcategory is Uppercase, or without category data one whose code point is an uppercase letter; adding Capital Spacing to a font without the class would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The code takes the form of the version 4 file in the corpus (Markow): one `script` section per script, one named lookup per rule set (idotaccent, commaaccent, puntvolat) after a `language` statement listing every language that shares it; Update on a font with idotaccent and Scommaaccent would settle the current form. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | When several Turkish i glyphs exist, idotaccent is used before i.TRK and i.loclTRK, the order the handbook lists them; Update on a font with idotaccent and i.loclTRK would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Catalan and Dutch rules use, in order of preference, the `.loclCAT` middle dot, the `.loclCAT` or `.loclNLD` ligature, then the legacy Ldot, ldot or Jacute, jacute in context, lowercase rule first; the ligature and Jacute forms have no corpus example and Update on fonts with each would settle them. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Dutch lookup is named ijacute and the Urdu lookup urdu; Urdu writes `sub X by X.urdu;` under `script arab; language URD;` for each `.urdu` glyph whose base exists, and six-ar alone writes nothing; Update on a font with Jacute and on one with four-persian.urdu would settle them. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Other `.loclXXX` glyphs (XXX three or four capitals or digits) get `sub base by base.loclXXX;` in a lookup named locl_<script>_<XXX>, under the script of the base glyph's code point, else of the stored script, else DFLT, after the built-in sets; Update on a font with be-cy.loclSRB would settle the shape. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Suffixed forms of the built-in glyphs (i.sc and idotaccent.sc) get no built-in rule; Update on a font with small caps would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.cvXX` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.salt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | salt copies the rules of ss01 only, as the handbook says; Update on a font with ss01 and ss02 would settle whether other sets join. |
| `autoFeature.swsh` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.titl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.init` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.medi` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.med2` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.med2` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The suffix is accepted on glyphs of any script, since the handbook only says it is used in Syriac; Update on a Latin glyph with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fina` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fin2` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fin2` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The suffix is accepted on glyphs of any script, since the handbook only says it is used in Syriac; Update on a Latin glyph with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fin3` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fin3` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The suffix is accepted on glyphs of any script, since the handbook only says it is used in Syriac; Update on a Latin glyph with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Single substitutions to the suffixed forms plus the space substitution, one line each in glyph order; Update in the reference app would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The `sub space by emspace;` line comes last and only when the feature has other rules and both glyphs exist; Update on a font with one width form would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base counts as kana by its code point (Hiragana, Katakana, their extensions and the halfwidth forms) or its script; Update on a font with a kanji and a kana with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hojo` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Single substitutions to the suffixed forms plus the space substitution, one line each in glyph order; Update in the reference app would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The `sub space by enspace;` line comes last and only when the feature has other rules and both glyphs exist; Update on a font with one width form would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ital` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base counts as kana by its code point (Hiragana, Katakana, their extensions and the halfwidth forms) or its script; Update on a font with a kanji and a kana with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.qwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Update recognises both `.qartwidth`, the handbook's spelling, and `.quartwidth`; a font with both suffixes updated in the reference app would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ruby` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.twid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.vkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.vkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base counts as kana by its code point (Hiragana, Katakana, their extensions and the halfwidth forms) or its script; Update on a font with a kanji and a kana with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.vrt2` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.abvs` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Not generated, because the handbook does not document the base and mark pairs it joins; Update in the reference app on a font of the script would settle what to write. |
| `autoFeature.akhn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The akhands are k_ssa and j_nya in every Indic script, written as consonant, halant, consonant; Update on a Devanagari font with those glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.blwf` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The rakar mark (`rakar-deva`) is formed in blwf from halant and ra, as Indic shaping expects, besides the `.below` glyphs the handbook names; Update on a Devanagari font with rakar-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.blws` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Not generated, because the handbook does not document the script-specific sequences it substitutes; Update in the reference app on a font of the script would settle what to write. |
| `autoFeature.cjct` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Conjunct rules read the half forms the half feature has made, falling back to consonant and halant; Update on a Devanagari font with k_ya-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.half` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Half forms are recognised by the name (the full form with a final `a` exists) rather than by the Halfform subcategory alone; Update on a Devanagari font would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.nukt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The rule is `sub base nukta by baseNukta;` in glyph order; Update in the reference app on a Devanagari font with kaNukta-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pref` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Not generated, because the handbook does not document the letter combinations it forms; Update in the reference app on a font of the script would settle what to write. |
| `autoFeature.pres` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Not generated, because the handbook does not document how a numbered matra width is matched to each conjunct; Update in the reference app on a font of the script would settle what to write. |
| `autoFeature.psts` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Not generated, because the handbook does not document the script-specific base and matra arrangements it substitutes; Update in the reference app on a font of the script would settle what to write. |
| `autoFeature.rkrf` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Rakar ligatures are recognised by the names `X_ra-S` and `Xa_rakar-S`, as the handbook gives no naming; Update on a Devanagari font with k_ra-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rphf` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated from the glyph names alone, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag; Update in the reference app on a Devanagari font would settle the form. |
| `autoFeature.vatu` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Not generated, because the handbook does not document the rkrf lookups it is derived from; Update in the reference app on a font of the script would settle what to write. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Latin ccmp is generated in the corpus's form: mark-ligature lookup first, then the dotless lookups with the top-accent filtering set; scripts other than Latin get no ccmp until a source shows their form. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Lookups are numbered `ccmp_DFLT_1`, `ccmp_DFLT_2` in that order, as the newest corpus sources name a lone mark-ligature or dotless lookup `ccmp_DFLT_1`; Update in the reference app on a font with both would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `@CombiningTopAccents` holds the encoded marks of canonical combining class 230, sorted by name, which matches every stored list in the corpus; a font with an unencoded top mark in the reference app would settle whether anchors count too. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Latin split lookup `ccmp_latn_1` (fi, fl, the lj, nj and dž digraphs into their letters, in a fixed order) is kept from a version 3 source, as no newer source has those glyphs; Update in the reference app on a font with fi would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.kern` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The kern lookups are split by script and direction as fontc splits them; the GPOS of a font with Latin and Cyrillic kerning exported from the reference app settles it. |
| `autoFeature.dist` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The scripts whose kerning goes to dist are fontc's (Indic, Universal Shaping Engine, Khmer, Myanmar); a font with Devanagari kerning exported from the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.syntax` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A token FontCraft cannot resolve stays in the text as written, and the compile report names it, as glyphsLib leaves it; a misspelt token in the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.versionString` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The token gives FontCraft's full release number (`0.0.1`), where the handbook's example shows two parts (`3.4`); the reference app's output for a release number of three parts would settle the form. |
| `textToken.keyPath` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The supported key paths are the font's familyName, versionMajor, versionMinor, unitsPerEm (and upm), date (as the source writes it), copyright, designer, designerURL, manufacturer, manufacturerURL, license, licenseURL, trademark, description, sampleText, vendorID, uniqueID, and the instance's name, familyName, fullName and postscriptFullName (family and style name with a space), postscriptFontName and fontName (family without spaces, a hyphen, the style name), preferredFamilyName, preferredSubfamilyName, weightClass (400 when unset) and widthClass (5 when unset); tokens tried in the reference app settle more. |
| `textToken.keyPath` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | In an instance context a key the instance does not have (designer, copyright…) reads the font's value, where glyphsLib keeps such a token as written; the same token typed into an instance's field in the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.numberValue` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A master that stores no value for a defined number value reads 0, as a missing metric value does; the reference app with a number value added after the master settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | In a font or instance context the token reads the first master's layer (an instance does not interpolate the value yet); the reference app with `{{{H:width}}}` in an instance's name settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | LSB and RSB of an empty layer name nothing and stay as written; the reference app with `{{{space:LSB}}}` settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Numbers are written without a fraction when whole and with at most three decimals otherwise; a glyph with a fractional width in the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The bounds are spelled as the reference's scripting object names them (`bounds.origin.x`, `bounds.size.width`); the reference app with those tokens settles it. |
| `regex.engine` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Patterns use the Rust regex crate, which has no look-around and no back-references inside a pattern; the reference's engine may accept them. Settled by observation. |
| `url.show.path` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A path that is empty, relative, a network share, not a file or folder, or that cannot be opened is skipped with a warning together with its glyphs, and the rest of the URL is still handled; the observe step below settles it. |
| `url.show.production` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A glyph without a production name of its own is found by its glyph name; a show URL with production=A in the reference app would settle it. |
| `url.show.defaultLayer` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | The main master is the font's first master; a font whose main master is not the first, opened by a show URL in the reference app, would settle it. |
| `url.show.encoding` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Query values are percent-decoded as UTF-8 and unknown glyphs or layer ids are skipped with a warning; the reference does not say. |
| `url.show.encoding` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A `+` stays a plus sign rather than a space, and a URL holding raw spaces, control characters or non-ASCII characters is refused; a show URL with `+` in a glyph name in the reference app would settle the first. |
| `url.show.encoding` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A `%` not followed by two hexadecimal digits, bytes that are not UTF-8 and encoded control characters refuse the whole URL; unknown parameters are skipped with a warning. |
| `url.safety` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | URLs only open and show; any other path after `fontcraft://` is refused, so a web page cannot run commands; the reference documents only `show`. |
| `url.safety` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A URL with a fragment, user information or anything after `show/` other than the query is refused; device files and network shares are not opened. |
| `snippet.builtIn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The built-in snippets are Automatic Code, Lookup, Contextual Substitution, Language System and Stylistic Set Name (a featureNames block with a substitution); opening the snippets menu in the reference app would settle the list and the titles. |
| `snippet.builtIn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Automatic Code snippet inserts `# Automatic Code` followed by a line break, so code after the caret is not commented out; inserting it in the middle of a line in the reference app would settle it. |
| `snippet.fileNaming` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The extension is matched without regard to case and the `FeatureSnippets` prefix with it; a file whose NAME is empty is skipped with a warning; a snippet folder with such files in the reference app would settle both. |
| `snippet.fileFormat` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | An entry without a text `title` or `code` is skipped and counted in one warning for its file while the file's other entries are kept; a file with such an entry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `snippet.fileFormat` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A binary property list is refused with a message; whether the reference app reads one would settle it. |
| `snippet.insertion` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A snippet is inserted at the caret and replaces the selection; inserting one over a selection in the reference app would settle it. |

## `13-postscript-hinting.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hint.ps.renderPreview` | M10 | inferred | yes | The preview rasterizes with skrifa's PostScript hinter (a port of FreeType's), not with an Adobe rasterizer; comparing with the Adobe application preview the handbook suggests would show how far they differ. |
| `hinting.ps.standardStems.privateDictMapping` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | More than twelve stems in one direction: the export writes the first twelve in list order and warns, and hint.ps.validateFontHints reports it; an export from the reference app with thirteen stems would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.alignmentZones.limits` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Overlapping zones and values that are not positive are errors and every other broken rule a warning, and the export still writes what fits the CFF limits; the reference app's font-info check would show how it ranks them. |
| `hinting.ps.alignmentZones.gridCompensation` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The check flags a zone whose size is a whole number of grid steps when the grid is coarser than one unit (it was not widened), and says nothing for finer or fractional grids, where a widened zone cannot be told apart; whether the reference app checks this at all would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.alignmentZones.blueValuesMapping` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | Too many zones (more than seven BlueValues pairs or five OtherBlues pairs): the export keeps the first pairs in ascending order and warns, and hint.ps.validateFontHints reports it; an export from the reference app with eight top zones would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.blueScale` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | As in fontmake, BlueScale, BlueFuzz and BlueShift are written only when the master has alignment zones; a reference export of a font with no zone settings would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.blueScale` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When BlueScale times the tallest zone reaches 1, the export keeps the BlueScale and warns rather than lowering it; a reference export of a font with a 30-unit zone and no blueScale parameter would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.blueShift` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | blueShift, blueScale, blueFuzz and the other hinting parameters whose scope the handbook does not give are read from the instance, else the master, else the font, as FontCraft reads every other parameter; a reference export with a master-level and a font-level blueShift that differ would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.familyAlignmentZones` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Family Alignment Zones is read where it is most specific (instance, then master, then font) and written as FamilyBlues and FamilyOtherBlues with the same rule as the master zones (a zone at 0 or of positive size to FamilyBlues); an export from the reference app would confirm the scope and the split. |
| `hinting.ps.export.autohintParameter` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The Autohint parameter is read on the instance first, then the font; when neither has it the export dialog's checkbox (default off) decides; a reference export would show whether a font-level parameter counts. |
| `hinting.ps.export.hintReplacement` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Manual hints that overlap get masks from FontCraft's own rule: the first mask applies from the start, a new one starts at the first node on an edge of a stem that overlaps a stem the current mask holds, the first of two overlapping stems at one node wins, and a stem no node touches joins every mask it fits; the reference's masks for a hand-hinted 8 would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.flex.automatic` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Flex is added to every exported glyph that qualifies, hand-hinted glyphs included, with otfautohint's flex test (the handbook's conditions plus its length ratio); an export from the reference app of a cupped serif under 20 units, hinted by hand, would settle both points. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.hintKinds` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A stem with one edge on a node and the other a number (left when a linked node is deleted) is stored with the number as place = (number, 0); a file saved by the reference app after deleting one linked node of a stem hint would show its form. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.display` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | PostScript hints are shown and edited with the Select tool while View › Show Hints is on; whether another tool is needed would be settled in the reference app. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.select` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Tab after the last hint wraps to the first and Shift+Tab before the first wraps to the last; with several hints selected Tab moves on from the last selected one; trying it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.linkOnDrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An edit that deletes the node a hint end is linked to turns that end into a number at the node's last position; deleting a linked node in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.linkOnDrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | After an edit, a linked node is found again by index, then by place and on- or off-curve class within half a unit, then by index when its path keeps the same node classes; reversing a contour and changing its start point with linked hints in the reference app would settle whether links follow the node. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.ghostFromSingleNode` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A ghost hint made from one node is a top ghost when the node lies in a top zone or nearer to a top zone than to a bottom zone, else a bottom ghost; doing it in the reference app on a node at the cap height and one at the baseline would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.masters.transferByNodeLink` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An unlinked hint end keeps its main-master number in every master and instance, and a link whose node no longer exists in a master falls back to that number; an export of an interpolated instance with one unlinked hint would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.masters.fallbackToOtherMasters` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When the main master has no hints for a glyph and several other masters do, the first of them in master order is used; a reference export of such a glyph would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.cff2.sharedHintStructure` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | In variable CFF2 output the hints come from the main master's manual hints resolved through node links in every master, or else from the multiple-master autohinter; unlinked manual hints keep one value in every master; a variable export from the reference app would settle it. |
| `autohint.ps.inputs` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | Identical zones are merged before the autohinter reads them: glyphsLib's mapping (and FontCraft's Private dict) keeps duplicates, which otfautohint rejects as overlapping zones (Oswald Italic, ascender equal to cap height); the export still writes the Private dict as fontmake does. |
| `autohint.ps.overlapCorrection` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | FontCraft treats a glyph as overlapping when its path operations change the outline, where otfautohint asks whether any point of its flattened outline disappeared; the two differ on a cross of two bars whose corners all lie outside the other bar (overlapping here, not there), and comparing both autohinters on such glyphs would settle which to follow. |
| `autohint.ps.variable.keepConflicts` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | The search that picks which conflicting stems to remove tries both choices for each conflict, as otfautohint does, but only for its first 4,096 steps; after that it keeps the first choice, so a glyph with that many order conflicts across masters can lose different stems than otfautohint would. Hinting such a glyph with otfautohint would show whether the limit is ever reached in practice. |
| `hint.ps.autohintGlyph` | M10 | handbook | no | Autohint replaces any hints the layer already has and acts on every glyph selected in Edit View or Font View; the reference app would settle both. |
| `hint.ps.addHorizontalHint` | M10 | handbook | no | With no node selected the new hint sits at the vertical centre of the glyph's bounding box with the master's first horizontal stem as its size (50 units without stems); doing it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.addVerticalHint` | M10 | handbook | no | With no node selected the new hint sits at the horizontal centre of the glyph's bounding box with the master's first vertical stem as its size (50 units without stems); doing it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.makeGhostHint` | M10 | handbook | no | Make Ghost Hint keeps the hint's origin edge and makes a top ghost when that edge is the upper one of a stem that reaches a top zone, else a bottom ghost; a ghost hint becomes a stem hint again when hint.ps.setHintValues gives it a size; the reference app would settle both. |
| `hint.ps.setHintValues` | M10 | handbook | no | Typing a value for a linked end unlinks that end and keeps the typed number; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.attachHintToNode` | M10 | inferred | no | Dropping a marker on a node links that end, and dragging it off onto empty space unlinks it, keeping the current number; the reference app would settle the unlinking gesture. |
| `hint.ps.selectNextHint` | M10 | handbook | no | Tab wraps from the last hint to the first; the reference app would settle it. |
| `autohint.ps.run` | M10 | docs | no | The autohinter is a port of otfautohint with its defaults; which autohinter the reference app runs is not documented, and a reference export compared with otfautohint's output on the same font would show whether they agree. |

## `14-truetype-hinting.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Rounding only in GDI ClearType is stored as options 3, which no source shows; a file saved by the reference app with that rounding mode would show the value. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A Snap stores its zone in the stem key (−2 automatic, −1 none, else the index), as a Stem stores its stem; a file with a Snap set to a named zone would show the key. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Stem instructions flagged as triple form groups of three in list order; a file with two triple hints in one glyph would show how they are kept apart. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A delta cell is stored as 8 (up) or −8 (down) under its PPM, in a column keyed by the instance's axis values in braces padded to six, as the one format sample shows; a file with deltas set for two instances would confirm both. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An instruction of a TrueType type that lacks a point its type needs (the corpus has place-only Snap and Stem hints and a Stem without target) is detached: kept as stored, never listed or compiled; the reference app's export of such a glyph would settle it. |
| `autohint.tt.option.info` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | With FontCraft's own TrueType autohinter (decided in M10 § 7, question 2) the name strings are left alone unless detailed or plain info is asked for; the reference app's choice between its default and No Autohint Info would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.defaultFromAlignmentZones` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Zones derived from alignment zones are named after their metric (ascender, capHeight, xHeight, baseline, descender, or a named metric's name), the master's other zones zone 1, zone 2…; Get PS zones in the reference app would show its names. |
| `hinting.tt.param.ttfZones` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | No source holds a TTFZones value, so its entries are read as {name, position, size, align, deltas = {column: {PPM: shift}}, filter = predicate text}, the stem form of the corpus's TTFStems extended; a file saved by the reference app with zones, an alignment, a delta and a filter would show the real keys. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.name` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | TT zones and TT stems are matched across masters by name; a master that lacks a name uses the main master's value for it; adding or renaming a zone or stem acts on every master; names must be unique and a duplicate is refused; the reference app would settle all four. |
| `hinting.tt.param.overshootSuppressionBelowPpm` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Absent TTFOvershootSuppressionBelowPPM: no size-based suppression beyond what the zones do on their own; a reference export without the parameter would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.param.controlValueProgram` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | No corpus source holds the prep or fpgm Table Assembly parameter, so FontCraft writes and reads them in its own assembly syntax (one instruction per line, mnemonics with bracketed flags as fontTools prints them, push values on the push's line; fontTools' layout with the values on the following lines and /* */ comments is read too); a file saved by the reference app after opening a hinted TrueType font would show its syntax, and the reader would then accept both. |
| `hinting.tt.import.glyphPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Imported glyph programs are kept per layer as assembly text with a fingerprint of the outline; they are written back while the outline is unchanged and the layer has no editor instructions, editor instructions win, and a changed outline drops the program with a warning; opening a hinted TTF in the reference app would settle how it stores them. |
| `hinting.tt.gasp.flagMapping` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Without a gasp Table parameter a font with TrueType instructions gets three records: up to 8 PPM DOGRAY + SYMMETRIC_SMOOTHING, up to 20 GRIDFIT + SYMMETRIC_GRIDFIT, up to 65535 all four bits; an unhinted font gets no gasp table, as fontc and fontmake write none; an export from the reference app without the parameter would settle both. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.gridFittedOutline` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The grid-fitted outline comes from skrifa's TrueType interpreter (FreeType's v40 interpreter in Rust), not from the Windows rasterizer the handbook names (decided in M10 § 7, question 4); comparing red outlines with the reference app at a few sizes would show the difference. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.pixelBackdrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The backdrop shows grey coverage of the grid-fitted outline, sampled 6 times across each pixel in the two ClearType intents and once down it in GDI ClearType, not coloured subpixels; screenshots of the reference app's backdrop in each intent would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.hintOrder` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Instructions cannot be reordered: the list order is creation order, and deleting and re-adding is the way to move one to the end; the reference app would show whether a reorder exists. |
| `hinting.tt.instructions.addFromSelection` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Dragging an instruction badge moves only the badge's drawn position, which is view state and not saved; dropping it on another node does not re-target it; the reference app would settle what the drag changes. |
| `hinting.tt.instructions.badgeColours` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Delta badges are orange, a colour no other instruction uses; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.snap.zoneAuto` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | In horizontal mode Auto ignores zones and rounds to the nearest pixel edge, since zones are vertical; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Path > Remove Overlap in the editor applies the documented export rule to instructions on removed nodes and on intersections, and PostScript ends on removed nodes become numbers; running Remove Overlap on an instructed glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An outline edit other than Remove Overlap removes an instruction whose node is gone instead of moving it; deleting an instructed node in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When upkeep removes one Stem instruction of a triple hint, the other two stay as plain Stem instructions; deleting a node of a triple hint in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.variable.deltasIgnored` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Zone and stem deltas reach a variable font only for the default master's column; per-instance Delta instructions are dropped as the handbook says; a variable export from the reference app would settle the zone and stem part. |
| `hinting.tt.variable.cvar` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Per-master zones and stems reach a variable font as cvt values of the default master plus cvar deltas; a variable export from the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.generatedPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | The fpgm, prep and cvt layout is FontCraft's own, written from the TrueType instruction set specification; byte parity with the reference app is not a goal and nothing from its output is copied. |
| `hinting.tt.export.instanceValues` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Composite glyphs get no glyph program of their own: their components' programs apply; Alternate and Intermediate layers that replace the main master's layer carry their own instructions, as the PostScript chapter says for hints; a reference export would settle both. |
| `autohint.tt.run` | M10 | inferred | no | The built-in TrueType autohinter is FontCraft's own (decided in M10 § 7, question 2): Snap and Stem instructions from the PostScript autohinter's zone and stem analysis, compiled with the manual-instruction compiler; ttfautohint runs instead when the TTFAutohint binary path parameter names one; the reference app's built-in autohinter is ttfautohint. |
| `hint.tt.getPsZones` | M10 | handbook | no | Get PS zones replaces the TT zone list with the master's alignment zones, named after their metrics (xHeight for the x-height metric so that it gets the x-height rounding, else the metric's name, else zone1, zone2…); the reference app would settle replace or append and the names. |
| `hint.tt.removeZone` | M10 | handbook | no | Snap instructions that name a removed zone fall back to Auto, and zones aligned to it lose their alignment; a rename updates both; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.setZone` | M10 | inferred | no | One command edits any property of a TT zone, as the sheet's row does, and refuses deltas and filters outside the main master; the reference app's sheet would settle whether other masters show those buttons at all. |
| `hint.tt.getPsStems` | M10 | handbook | no | Get PS stems replaces the TT stem list, keeping the stems' names; the reference app would settle replace or append. |
| `hint.tt.removeStemDefinition` | M10 | handbook | no | Stem instructions that name a removed stem fall back to Auto; a rename updates them; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.setStemDefinition` | M10 | inferred | no | One command edits any property of a TT stem, as the sheet's row does, and refuses deltas and filters outside the main master; the reference app's sheet would settle whether other masters show those buttons at all. |
| `hint.tt.setRenderingIntent` | M10 | inferred | no | The rendering intent is a pop-up in the TrueType Instructor's Info box beside the pixel size; Grayscale maps to skrifa's smooth normal target, ClearType to its LCD target without symmetric rendering (no vertical oversampling), DirectWrite to LCD with symmetric rendering; the reference app would settle the control and the comparison of outlines. |
| `hint.tt.setPixelSize` | M10 | handbook | no | The pixel size field accepts whole numbers from 4 to 400 PPM; the reference app would settle the limits. |
| `hint.tt.showPreviewAddress` | M10 | handbook | no | The server listens on a free port of the local network interfaces, serves only the preview page and the font, and stops when the dialog closes (decided in M10 § 7, question 5); the reference app would settle lifetime and port. |
| `hint.tt.deleteInstructions` | M10 | inferred | no | The Delete key removes the selected instructions, as for PostScript hints; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.addStemHint` | M10 | handbook | no | The context-menu label of the Stem instruction is Stem, as the key list names it; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.makeTripleHint` | M10 | handbook | no | Deleting or reversing one member of a triple hint dissolves the group and the other two become plain Stem instructions; the reference app would settle it. |
| `dialog.ttDeltas` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The table has one row per PPM from 8 to 80, the waterfall's range; its columns are the masters for zone and stem deltas and the static instances for Delta instructions; the reference app would settle both. |
| `preview.ttHintingWaterfall` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | Each waterfall row is one PPM from 8 to 80 with the tab's text set in the current instance at that size; the reference app would settle spacing and labels. |
| `dialog.ttPreviewAddress` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | Closing the dialog stops the server; the reference app would settle it. |
| `trueTypeInstructor` (tool) | M10 | handbook | – | The tool sits after the Measurement tool in the toolbar and node selection works as with the Select tool (click, Shift+click, rubber band, Select All on the layer); the custom-parameter descriptions give its shortcut as I; the reference app would settle the rest. |

## `15-color-fonts.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `colorPalette.addColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new row is opaque black (#000000FF) in every palette unless `color` or `colors` is given. Settled by pressing plus in the reference's palette editor. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Removing a row renumbers the layers that use higher rows and refuses while a layer uses the row. Settled by removing a used row in the reference's editor and reading the layers afterwards. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Palette colours of shapes (fills, strokes, gradient stops, shadows) count as uses of a row and are renumbered with the palette layers. Settled by removing a row a full-colour layer's gradient uses in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | The last colour row can be removed, which leaves every palette empty. Settled by removing every colour in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Palette colours of shapes in a layer's background count as uses of a row too: they hold it and are renumbered with the foreground. Settled by removing a row only a background shape uses in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.addPalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new palette is a copy of palette 0 (`copyFrom` defaults to 0). Settled by Add Palette in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.removePalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | The last remaining palette cannot be removed; the command is refused. Settled by removing every palette in the reference's editor. |
| `layer.convertToColorPalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | Without `index` the layer gets colour index 0, and a layer that already is a palette layer keeps its index. Settled by choosing Color Palette on a new layer in the reference. |
| `layer.convertToColorPalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | A master layer cannot become a colour layer, and converting a colour layer to another kind replaces its kind. Settled by the Layers palette context menu on a master layer and on a colour layer. |
| `layer.setColorPaletteIndex` | M11 | handbook | yes | The headless command accepts any index up to 65,533, also one beyond the rows of the font's palettes, so layers can be set up before the palettes grow. Settled by opening a file whose colorPalette index is beyond its palettes in the reference. |
| `layer.convertToIColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Without `size` a new iColor layer gets 128 pixels per em. Settled by the label of a new iColor layer in the reference. |
| `layer.setIColorSize` | M11 | handbook | yes | The layer's placed image is rescaled about its bottom-left corner so it keeps unitsPerEm ÷ size units per pixel. Settled by the size change in the observe step. |
| `layer.setIColorImage` | M11 | inferred | yes | Only the master layer's paths set the bounds, not its components. Settled by dropping an image on an iColor layer of a glyph made of components in the reference. |
| `layer.setIColorImage` | M11 | inferred | yes | The image is placed with its bottom-left corner at the bottom-left of the master layer's path bounds (the origin when the master layer has no paths) and scaled to unitsPerEm ÷ size units per pixel, so the iColor size alone sets its scale. Settled by dropping an image on an iColor layer in the reference. |
| `layer.setSvgImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | The SVG's viewBox is placed with its bottom-left corner at the layer origin, one SVG user unit per font unit, y pointing up. Settled by dropping an SVG file on an svg layer in the reference. |
| `attributes.addAttribute` | M11 | handbook | yes | Shadow and Inner Shadow have blur, offset and colour fields, as the file format stores them; Pattern Image has an image reference. Settled by adding each in the reference's inspector. |
| `attributes.addAttribute` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new Shadow or Inner Shadow is black at 50 % opacity with blur 10 and offset (10, −10) font units; Pattern Image keeps only a file reference, in a FontCraft key. Settled by adding each in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `attributes.setStrokeWidth` | M11 | handbook | yes | A stroke added to a path without one is opaque black. Settled by entering a stroke width in the reference's Attributes inspector. |
| `attributes.setStrokeAlignment` | M11 | handbook | yes | The left button is inside and the right button outside, for paths in the usual direction. Settled by choosing each in the reference and reading `strokePos` in the saved file. |
| `attributes.removeStroke` | M11 | handbook | yes | The removed stroke's colour is transparent black. Settled by reading `strokeColor` in a file saved after the minus button. |
| `attributes.removeGradient` | M11 | inferred | yes | The Gradient row has a minus button, like the Stroke and Fill rows, that removes the gradient. Settled by looking at the Gradient row of the reference's Attributes inspector. |
| `attributes.addGradient` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new gradient is linear, from the path's fill colour at offset 0 to the same colour fully transparent at offset 1, running from the left middle to the right middle of the path's bounds. Settled by adding a gradient in the reference. |
| `attributes.addGradient` | M11 | handbook | yes | Conic gradients and the extend mode, which the version 4 file format has and the handbook does not describe, are accepted as parameters; the inspector shows only linear and radial. Settled by looking for them in the reference's inspector. |
| `attributes.addGradient` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new radial gradient is centred in the path's bounds and runs from radius 0 to half the bounds' width; a new conic gradient is centred and turns 360°. Settled by switching a new gradient to radial in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `attributes.setGradientType` | M11 | handbook | yes | Switching the type places the gradient as a new gradient of that type and keeps its stops and extend mode. Settled by switching a moved gradient between linear and radial in the reference. |
| `attributes.addGradientStop` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new stop takes the colour of the gradient at its offset. Settled by clicking the gradient bar in the reference. |
| `attributes.setGradientStopColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | A stop can be moved along the bar, which `offset` does. Settled by dragging a stop knob sideways in the reference. |
| `attributes.setGradientPosition` | M11 | handbook | yes | Without `end`, the end moves by as much as the start, so the gradient moves as a whole. Settled by dragging the start knob of a linear gradient in the reference's Edit View. |
| `color.fourKinds` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph may mix kinds: its palette and full-colour layers feed COLR, its iColor layers sbix and its svg layer the SVG table, each written by its own rule; an svg layer wins over a conversion by Color Layers to SVG for that glyph. Settled by exporting a glyph with a palette layer and an svg layer from the reference. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Other glyphs are drawn with the colour layers of the current master, in palette 0. Settled by selecting a colour layer in a tab of several colour glyphs in the reference. |
| `color.layered.toSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The SVG document stacks the masters as Color Layers to COLR does, each filled with its Master Color, opacity included. Settled by exporting the layered test font with Color Layers to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.layered.toColr` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The masters with a Master Color become palette layers of one glyph in master order, the last master at the bottom and the first on top (as Edit View stacks them), each in a palette entry made from its Master Color; the fallback outline is the instance's own master. Settled by exporting the layered test font with Color Layers to COLR from the reference. |
| `color.palette.perMaster` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A palette command given `master` edits that master's own Color Palettes, and creates it from the font's palettes when the master has none. Settled by checking whether the reference offers the palette editor on a master's Color Palettes and what it starts with. |
| `color.export.colrFromPaletteLayers` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The first palette layer in the file paints at the bottom, as both oracles write it. Settled by exporting the palette test font from the reference and comparing its layer records with the Layers palette order. |
| `color.export.colorPaletteForCpal` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Every palette is written, with the chosen palette moved to position 0, so the font keeps its alternatives and renders the chosen one by default. Settled by exporting the palette test font with Color Palette for CPAL = 1 from the reference. |
| `color.fullColor.layerNaming` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Glyphs 2 files mark full-colour and svg layers by the names `Color` and `svg` alone, as the labels read. Settled by saving a colour font from the reference app as a Glyphs 2 file. |
| `color.fullColor.defaultGrayFill` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The gray is 50 % gray, opaque (#808080FF), written to the path's fill colour when the path is made. Settled by reading the colour picker of a new shape in the reference. |
| `color.fullColor.gradientTypes` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A radial gradient with an end radius of 0 reaches from its centre to the farthest corner of the shape's bounds, which is how both oracles draw a version 3 `circle` gradient; a version 4 file writes such a gradient without `endRadius`. Settled by saving a version 3 radial gradient from the reference app as version 4. |
| `color.export.fullColorToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | One uncompressed SVG document per glyph whose group has the id `glyph<ID>` and a transform that flips y at the baseline; centred strokes are SVG strokes, inside and outside strokes are expanded to outlines. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font with Color Layers to SVG from the reference and reading the documents. |
| `color.export.fullColorToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A conic gradient, which SVG cannot draw, is written in the colour of its first stop, with a warning. Settled by exporting a conic gradient with Color Layers to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.export.fullColorToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph whose document cannot be made (a palette row beyond the palette, a shape that cannot be drawn) is left out of the SVG table with a warning and keeps its master outline. Settled by exporting a palette layer whose index is beyond the palette with Color Layers to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.export.fullColorToColrV1` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A full-colour Color layer exports as COLR version 1 without any parameter, as both oracles do; the handbook names only SVG for these layers. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font from the reference with no parameters. |
| `color.export.colrVersionChoice` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | Solid-only full-colour layers export as COLR version 0, as fontc does, not version 1 as fontmake does. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font with only solid fills from the reference. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | Gradient geometry and stop colours vary when they differ between masters, as fontmake writes them. Settled by exporting the two-master palette font and a two-master gradient font as variable fonts from the reference. |
| `color.export.strokeInColr` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A stroke becomes its own layer glyph painted in the stroke colour, above the path's fill, expanded with the path's stroke position, caps and joins. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font from the reference. |
| `color.sbix.bitmapFormats` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | PNG and JPEG data are stored as read, with the graphic types `png ` and `jpg `; TIFF is converted to PNG. Settled by exporting the sbix test font from the reference and reading the graphic types. |
| `color.sbix.sameAspectRatio` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Unequal ratios are exported as they are, with a warning from export and from `color.describeExport`. Settled by exporting the non-square image in the sbix test font from the reference. |
| `color.sbix.iColorNaming` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Glyphs 2 files mark sbix layers by the name `iColor n` alone, as the label reads. Settled by opening a Glyphs 2 sbix file from the reference app. |
| `color.layers.widthFollowsMaster` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Palette and full-colour layers follow the master's width as iColor and svg layers do. Settled by changing a master width in the reference with a palette layer present. |
| `color.export.sbixAutomatic` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph whose image file is missing at export is left out of that strike, with a warning naming the glyph and the file. Settled by exporting the sbix test font from the reference with one file moved away. |
| `color.export.sbixToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Each bitmap becomes an `<image>` element with embedded PNG data, placed where the image sits on its iColor layer. Settled by exporting the sbix test font with SBIX to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.svg.imageTransform` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The placed SVG's transform is written as a group transform inside the glyph's document, combined with the y flip at the baseline; the SVG's own content is kept as usvg normalises it. Settled by exporting the SVG test font with a moved and scaled svg layer from the reference. |
| `color.export.exportSvgTable` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The SVG table is written whenever svg layers exist or a conversion asks for it, without Export SVG Table, as the appendix says; the parameter set off removes it. Settled by exporting the SVG test font from the reference with no parameters. |
| `color.export.exportSvgTable` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | When no glyph's SVG document can be made, the font gets no SVG table, with a warning, rather than an empty one. Settled by exporting from the reference a font whose only svg layer names a missing file. |
| `color.open.compiledColorTables` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Opening a compiled colour font recovers palettes and vector colour layers and reports bitmap and SVG glyphs as lost. Settled by opening a COLR font in the reference. |
| `palette.layers.paletteColorChooser` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | The chooser and the Edit View preview always use palette 0. Settled by opening the chooser in a font with two palettes in the reference. |

## `16-settings.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `settings.get` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own command; the reference has no headless access to settings, so there is nothing to settle. |
| `settings.set` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own command; the reference has no headless access to settings, so there is nothing to settle. |
| `addons.repo.httpBasicAuth` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | Credentials are taken from the URL itself and stored with the setting, not in the operating system's keychain; the reference's storage is not described. |
| `settings.addons` (panel) | M13 | handbook | – | The section is called Extensions, because FontCraft has no add-on kinds that need Python; the reference calls it Addons. |
| `appearance.displayUnicodeValue` (setting) | M4 | handbook | – | On shows the code point, off the small marker, as the handbook's wording implies. Settled by observation. |
| `appearance.displayUnicodeValue` (setting) | M4 | handbook | – | Off by default (the small marker), as the handbook gives no default. Settled by observing a fresh install. |
| `sampleStrings.groups` (setting) | M5 | handbook | – | The built-in default holds groups of our own choosing: spacing strings built around n, o, H and O with placeholders, figures, and traditional pangrams for Latin, Greek and Cyrillic; no source lists the reference defaults we may copy. |
| `addons.useSystemConsoleForScriptOutput` (setting) | M13 | handbook | – | With the setting on, the log still shows in the Command Console as well as on standard error, where the handbook says the reference sends it to the system console instead; keeping both loses nothing and the switch stays off by default. |

## `17-extensions.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `script.run` | M13 | inferred | yes | A macro runs as one undo step that is undone as a whole, like command.batch; the reference undoes script edits per glyph and does not say how a script's changes group. |
| `script.run` | M13 | inferred | yes | One top-level macro run executes at most 1,000,000 steps, the steps of the macros it runs included, and reads at most 256 MiB of macro files; past either it fails as a whole. Our own limit; no source describes one for scripts in the reference. |
| `script.rerunLast` | M13 | inferred | yes | The label of the item at the foot of the Script menu is Re-run Last Script followed by the script's title; the handbook gives no label. |
| `script.openScriptsFolder` | M13 | inferred | yes | The macros folder is created when missing and its path returned, and sessions without one (headless runs, the web app) have the command disabled; the reference app's behaviour when the Scripts folder is missing would settle the first part. |
| `script.reloadScripts` | M13 | inferred | yes | It is always shown in the Script menu and returns the new list, since the folder is also watched; files that cannot be read stay listed with their error. Holding Option over the reference app's Script menu settles only where the item shows. |
| `window.macroPanel` | M13 | inferred | yes | A run-style line with several commands runs them as one transaction and one undo step, and may name engine commands only, so a failing line changes nothing; the command-line tool runs them one by one and simply does not save after a failure. |
| `window.macroPanel` | M13 | inferred | yes | Clear empties the console's pane but keeps the session's command log, which other readers use; the reference's clear behaviour is not described. |
| `window.macroPanel` | M13 | inferred | yes | The history lives for the session only and is not saved between launches; watching whether the reference keeps typed text after a relaunch would settle it. |
| `script.recordMacro` | M13 | inferred | yes | A recording also stops taking steps once their parameters reach 64 MiB as JSON, and says so as for the step limit. Our own limit: the reference has no recorder. |
| `pluginManager.scriptsLoadWithoutRelaunch` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Folders are checked by comparing file names, sizes and modification times when the window regains focus and every 5 seconds, rather than with an operating-system file watcher, so the same code works on every platform; a reference behaviour is not involved. |
| `pluginManager.automaticUpdates` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Updates are offered, not installed silently, unless the user turns automatic updates on per index; the reference installs them automatically and does not say whether that can be turned off (open question in docs/spec/17-extensions.md). |
| `pluginManager.repositories` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | When two indexes list the same id, the index listed first in the settings wins; the reference does not say how it merges entries. |
| `script.menu.mirrorsFolder` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Script menu items are sorted by title, case-insensitively and with numbers in numeric order, folders before items; the handbook does not give the order. |
| `script.file.pyExtension` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Macro files end in `.fcmacro` and hold JSON; nothing in the reference decides it (the format is ours, open decision D8). |
| `script.importExistingPython` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | The repository ships no Python client package, only documented examples, because the code base is Rust only; nothing in the reference decides it. |
| `plugin.pluginsFolder` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | The folders are `Macros` and `Modules` in the per-user application data folder; the reference's folder names and location are not given in the handbook (open question). |
| `pluginManager.install` | M13 | inferred | no | An install from an index entry whose checksum does not match the downloaded bytes is refused and nothing is written; the reference's handling of a failed download is not documented. |
| `view.showReporter` | M13 | inferred | no | Reporter entries are listed at the foot of the View menu in the order of their names, below a separator; the handbook does not give the order. |
| `window.pluginManager` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | The type buttons are Filters, Reporters, Checks and Macros in place of Plugins, Scripts and Modules, because FontCraft has no Python modules; settled by nothing in the reference (our own extension model, decision 0005). |
| `window.macroPanel` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | The console's input is one line with a history, not a multi-line editor, because commands are one line of JSON; the reference's panel layout is not described (open question). |
| `window.macroPanel` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | Tab takes the first completion suggestion and Up and Down stay with the history, because the history is the row's documented use of those keys; nothing describes completion keys in the reference. |

## `18-custom-parameters.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `customParameters.shortAndLongNames` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A parameter added under another spelling is stored under the catalogue's spelling (the short name), and set and remove find rows of either spelling; the saved file of the observe step settles which spelling the reference app keeps. |
| `customParameters.unknownKept` (behavior) | M7 | inferred | – | Unknown parameters are shown and kept, not hidden or dropped; opening a file with an unknown parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Add Class` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | A leading @ on the class name is dropped, and the white space after the semicolon is not part of the code (glyphsLib's split); exporting with the parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Color Palettes` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | An edited Color Palettes keeps the form it was stored in; a new one is written as version 3 integer lists, or as the version 4 dictionary in a document read from version 4. Settled by editing the palettes in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `Color Palettes` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | A CMYK palette colour is converted to RGB without a colour profile, as (1 − c)(1 − k) and so on. Settled by exporting a CMYK palette from the reference and reading CPAL. |
| `Compatible name Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | When on, every name record is also written on the Macintosh platform (Roman encoding, English), with name ID 1 holding the family and style name together and ID 2 the style name, the layout older Macintosh applications group by; exporting with the parameter in the reference app and reading the name table settles it. |
| `Compatible name Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Compatible name Table writes its Macintosh records even when Export Mac Name Table Entries is absent, and none when that parameter is present and off; both parameters together in the reference app settle it. |
| `compatibleFullNames` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Without the field no name ID 18 is written, as both oracles do, though the handbook says the reference derives it from family and style name; an export in the reference app settles whether it writes ID 18 by default. |
| `Decompose Components` (parameter) | M6 | handbook | – | A decomposed glyph keeps its own contours first, then its components' contours in the order the compiler decomposes a glyph that mixes contours and components (fontc's order); a composite exported by the reference app with the parameter on would settle it. |
| `Decompose Glyphs` (parameter) | M6 | handbook | – | A text value is read as glyph names separated by commas or spaces, besides the documented list; the reference app with a text value would settle it. |
| `Default Layer Width` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | Entries are looked up in the master's parameters before the font's, as for the other per-script parameters; the reference app with the parameter on a master would settle it. |
| `Default Layer Width` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | A value that is not a number from 0 to 65535 is ignored; the reference app with such a value would settle it. |
| `Default Layer Width` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | The CJK scripts are han, hangul, kana and bopomofo; a new layer of a glyph of each script in the reference app would settle it. |
| `Export Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Export Glyphs accepts the names, wildcards and queries of Remove Glyphs and applies after Rename Glyphs, to the names glyphs are exported under; exporting a renamed glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export kern Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The table is version 0 with one format 0 subtable per run of at most 10,920 pairs (the subtable's 16-bit length), class pairs expanded to every member pair, exceptions overriding, zero pairs left out, only the default (horizontal) kerning of the exported master; pairs past 65,535 in total are dropped with a warning; an export with the parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export kern Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | When the table is full, the pairs kept are those found first in lookup order (glyph–glyph exceptions, then glyph–group, group–glyph, group–group), so exceptions survive; an export of a font past the limit in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export kern Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A CFF export with the parameter on writes no kern table and reports it in a warning; an OTF export with the parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export Mac Name Table Entries` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Macintosh records are written only for name IDs whose text the Mac Roman encoding can hold; an export from the reference app read with ttx settles which IDs it writes. |
| `Export STAT Table` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The parameter is read from the variable font setting first and from the font when the setting has none; exporting a font with it only in Font Info settles whether the reference reads it there. |
| `Feature for Feature Variations` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | A value that is not a feature tag puts the substitutions in `rvrn` with a warning, as fontc does; exporting with such a value from the reference app would settle it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Locked glyphs and locked paths are filtered at export like any other, since a lock guards editing; a Filter on a locked glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Before decomposing, each composite takes the anchors its components pass on, so mark attachment is kept; reading the mark feature of a filtered export from the reference app settles it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The filter runs on every glyph of the instance with its components decomposed first, so a TrueType export of an instance with a Filter has no composites; a Transformations Filter on an instance with a composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A Filter on a master applies to every export of that master; on the font it applies to every export, which fontmake ignores but no source forbids; the reference app's picker settles where it may be added. |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The path is relative to the host file's folder unless absolute; the imported glyphs are read when the host opens and before each export, shown locked, never saved into the host; a missing or unreadable file is a warning on opening and at export, not an error; the reference app settles the path form and the missing-file case. |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A linked glyph's layers go to the host master with the same id, else the one at the same position, and layers of other masters are left out; the linked file's kerning, features and groups are not imported; the reference app settles both with font B (m7-two) linked into font A (m7-one). |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A linked file with other units per em than the host is used unscaled, with a warning; linking a 2048-unit file into a 1000-unit font in the reference app settles it. |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The linked glyphs are locked, so Scale Font to Units per Em and adding, duplicating or removing a master are refused while any are present, naming the locked glyph; the reference app settles whether it scales or extends linked glyphs instead. |
| `Import Master` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The value is the other file's path, a semicolon and the master's name or id, or a dictionary with `Path` and `Master` (the form Import Font's samples suggest), resolved as Import Font resolves paths; with no master named, the file's first master; the reference app settles the value syntax. |
| `Import Master` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The linked master keeps its id unless the host uses it (then it gets a fresh one), takes its axis values by axis tag, its metric and stem values by definition, its layers for every glyph the host has and its kerning; its layers can be edited but are not saved into the host; the reference app settles whether it is read-only. |
| `Instance Preview` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The value lists glyph names separated by spaces or line breaks, at most 64 drawn; names the font lacks are skipped; a disabled parameter is ignored; the reference app settles separators and missing names. |
| `InterpolationWeightY` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Applied to the axis tagged wght (ignored without one) and to y coordinates of outlines, component offsets, anchors, vertical metrics, horizontal stems and alignment zones; settle with an instance that sets it in the reference app. |
| `Italic Style Linking` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | On links the value at 0 on the ital axis (the font's own or the table-only one) to 1 when it has no link; off removes every link on that axis; read from the variable font setting, else from the font. Exporting an upright and an italic family with the parameter on and off and reading STAT settles it. |
| `Keep Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | When both Keep Glyphs and Remove Glyphs are present, Keep Glyphs applies first and Remove Glyphs then removes from what is kept, with a warning; the reference app settles it. |
| `Keep Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | .notdef and space are kept whatever the list says; the reference app settles it. |
| `Keep UI-Font Bounding Box` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | The parameter has no effect because the generated cjct never stacks conjuncts; Update in the reference app on an Oriya font with and without the parameter would show what it changes. |
| `Link Metrics With First Master` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | Spacing and kerning commands refuse to edit a linked master and name the master it follows; no source says what the reference does with such an edit. |
| `Local Interpolation` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The listed glyphs are interpolated at the given location with every other part of the instance unchanged, including their widths; settle with an instance that sets it in the reference app. |
