| `Optical Size` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | The values are whole decipoints from 0 to 65535 and the code is a `size` feature added at the end of the list, replacing the code of a `size` feature the font already has; exporting with the parameter in the reference app and reading GPOS settles it. |
| `Optical Size` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | The menu name is written for Windows (English) and, when it is plain ASCII, for the Mac (Roman, English); a subfamily of 0 writes no range and no name, as the feature file specification asks; the exported name table of the reference app settles it. |
| `Optimize Variable Deltas` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The handbook drops the deltas of a contour whose nodes all move half a unit or less; FontCraft drops the deltas IUP can infer within half a unit, as both oracles do. A variable font exported by the reference app with the parameter on and off would settle it. |
| `Point To Unit Ratio` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The copied SVG gives coordinates in points, font units divided by the ratio, and declares its width and height in pt; without the parameter the SVG keeps one font unit per user unit with no unit on its size; copying into a drawing application from the reference app settles the scale. |
| `Point To Unit Ratio` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The master's parameter wins over the font's, and a ratio that is not a number from 0.001 to 1,000,000 is ignored; copying from a font with the parameter on a master in the reference app settles it. |
| `Point To Unit Ratio` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Only the SVG flavour is scaled: the clipboard fragment that edit.paste reads keeps font units, so copy and paste inside the app keeps the size; pasting a drawing copied from the reference app back into it settles what its own paste does. |
| `PreFilter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | On a variable font setting PreFilter is skipped with a warning, not an error; the reference app settles it. |
| `Prevent Name ID` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Name IDs 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 can be prevented like any other, with a warning that the font may not install; preventing ID 1 in the reference app settles whether it refuses. |
| `Prevent Name ID` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The parameters of the font, the master and the instance add up, as Name Table Entry does; an instance with one ID and a font with another, exported from the reference app, settles it. |
| `Preview Descender` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | The default reads winDescent, not the winAscent the handbook names, because that text reads as a misprint; the reference app with a font whose winAscent and winDescent differ settles it. |
| `Preview Descender` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | A value of either sign is a distance below the baseline; the reference app with a positive and a negative value settles it. |
| `Reencode Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Reencode Glyphs applies after Rename Glyphs, to the names glyphs are exported under, and when two lines give the same code point the later line wins; the reference app settles both. |
| `Remove Features` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | Besides a list, one text with the names separated by commas or line breaks is accepted, and every entry of a listed name is left out; the reference app's parameter editor settles which forms it stores. |
| `Remove Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A removed glyph that a kept composite uses is decomposed into that composite, as non-exported glyphs are (M2 preflight); removing the base of a composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `Remove Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Queries compare text values exactly except for `*`, are case-sensitive, and an unknown key is a warning that matches nothing; `unicode` compares hexadecimal text in either case, and a glyph without the property compares as empty text; the reference app settles case, empty values and unknown keys. |
| `Remove Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Remove and Keep Glyphs apply after Rename Glyphs, to the names glyphs are exported under, as the handbook's recipe for replacing a glyph needs and as fontmake does; the reference app settles it. |
| `Rename Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | An entry naming a glyph the font lacks renames the existing glyph to the new name, keeping its code points, with a warning; an entry whose old name is missing is skipped with a warning; the reference app settles both. |
| `Rename Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Code points and production names stay with the names while the drawing, export flag and kerning go with the glyph, and composites keep the drawing they used (fontmake rewrites component references), although the handbook's Decompose Glyphs entry suggests that composites show the swapped drawing; check 38 in the reference app settles it. |
| `Replace Feature` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | Only the first feature of that tag is replaced, its labels are kept and it counts as hand-written from then on, so a replaced `kern` still gets the generated kerning after it; exporting a font with a stylistic-set name and a replaced `kern` in the reference app settles it. |
| `Replace Feature` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | A Replace parameter that names no entry does nothing, without a message, as the handbook limits these parameters to existing entries and glyphsLib skips them; the reference app's export report settles it. |
| `Scale to UPM` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The instance's value wins over the master's and the font's; a value that is not a whole number from 16 to 16384 is skipped with a warning, and a scaling that cannot be done refuses the export; a Scale to UPM on a master and another on its instance in the reference app settle the first part. |
| `styleMapStyleNames` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The value must be regular, italic, bold or bold italic in any case, as the UFO key it comes from; another value is ignored with a warning; the reference app settles it. |
| `TTFAutohint options` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | No corpus source holds the parameter; FontCraft stores and reads it as ttfautohint command-line options in one string, which is how fontmake reads it from an instance (split on spaces); a file saved by the reference app with the options sheet filled in would show the real form. |
| `Update Features` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | Export regenerates only the automatic entries the font already has and adds none of the entries Update would add; an export of a font whose Features list lacks an automatic feature its glyphs call for would settle it. |
| `Use Arabic Presentation Form Unicodes` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | An exported glyph without code points gets the legacy code point the glyph database records for its name (`unicodeLegacy`: beh-ar.init FE91, lam_alef-ar FEFB) when it lies in FB50–FDFF or FE70–FEFF and no other glyph has it; glyphs with code points keep them; only cmap changes; custom glyph data is not read for it; an export from the reference app settles which glyphs it covers. |
| `Variable Font Family Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Looked up on the variable font setting first and on the font second; an export from the reference app with the parameter on each would settle it. |
| `Variable Font File Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Without it the file is named as glyphsLib names it: the font's family name, a hyphen and the setting's name unless that is Regular, then `VF`, spaces removed (the default font of a source without settings: family name and `VF`); an export from the reference app would settle it. |
| `Variable Font Optimize Deltas` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Treated as a second name of Optimize Variable Deltas, as the appendix describes both alike; settle by setting one of them off in the reference app and comparing the exported gvar. |
| `Variable Font Style Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Read as a style name, not a family name as its description says: on a setting or the font the variable font's default style, on a static instance its `fvar` name; settle by exporting with it set in each place. |
| `Variable SubfamilyName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Without it the variable font's default style name is the origin master's name, as the oracles build it from the default master; settle by exporting F4 (three named masters) without the parameter and reading name IDs 2 and 17. |
| `variablePostscriptFontName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | On a setting without it, the setting's PostScript Font Name property is used next; settle by exporting a setting with only that property set. |
| `variablePostscriptFontName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | A static export of an instance that has it takes it as name ID 6, ahead of postscriptFontName, as fontmake reads it; exporting such an instance as a static font in the reference app settles it. |
| `variableStyleName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Read as a style name, not a family name as its description says: on a setting or the font the variable font's default style, on a static instance its `fvar` name; settle by exporting with it set in each place. |
| `variableStyleNames` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Only the default-language text is written to `fvar`; settle by exporting an instance with two languages and reading the name records of its `fvar` entry. |
| `Variation Font Origin` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Treated as an older name of Variable Font Origin naming the master by name, as glyphsLib reads it; settle in the reference app. |
| `variationsPostScriptNamePrefix` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Without it no name ID 25 is written, as both oracles do; the typographic family fallback the description names is what a consumer computes. Settle by exporting without it and listing the name records. |
| `versionString` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | `%d` takes the major version and `%03d` (or any printf integer form) the minor, in that order; more than two `%` forms leave the text as written with a warning; the reference app settles other forms. |

## `20-cli.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `collection.unpack` | M12 | docs | yes | A member whose file name another member already takes gets the collection name plus -fontN, N its own index or the next number no other member uses; otc2otf on such a collection would settle it. |
| `collection.unpack` | M12 | docs | yes | When a later file cannot be written, the fonts this call created before it are removed and the error says so; no reference tool documents a partial failure. |
| `woff.decompress` | M12 | docs | yes | extractMetadata and extractPrivateData that name the input, the font output or each other (compared as resolved paths) are refused before anything is written; no reference tool documents the case. |
| `outlines.correctDirection` | M12 | inferred | yes | Without flavour a source gets the PostScript convention (the editor's) and a TrueType font the TrueType one; a reference run on a source drawn for TrueType outlines would settle the source default. |
| `outlines.correctDirection` | M12 | inferred | yes | Composites are left alone, since their direction comes from their bases; no reference documents whole-font direction fixing of composites. |
| `outlines.addExtremes` | M12 | inferred | yes | minSegment accepts only 3 (the editor's fixed threshold) or 0 (every extreme forced); a reference run with other values would settle whether other thresholds are wanted. |
| `outlines.roundCoordinates` | M12 | inferred | yes | Halfway values round away from zero to the grid; fontTools' otRound rounds them up, which differs only for negative halves on a grid coarser than 1 unit. |
| `outlines.roundCoordinates` | M12 | inferred | yes | On sources advance widths stay, as in the editor's rounding command; on compiled fonts they are rounded with the points. |
| `outlines.decompose` | M12 | docs | yes | Several inputs whose outputs resolve to one file, or an output that would replace another input, are refused before anything is written; no reference tool documents the case. |
| `font.proof` | M12 | docs | yes | The HTML specimen has no script: feature switches are checkboxes and each axis is a row of stops (nine even values plus the default) that CSS turns into font settings, so the axes are not free sliders. Settled by the owner if a scripted slider is wanted. |
| `layout.decompileFeatures` | M12 | docs | yes | A lookup a feature uses is named feature_script_language_index after the first language system that uses it, and any other lookup GSUB_index or GPOS_index; the round-trip measure on the corpus would show whether stabler names are needed. |
| `layout.decompileFeatures` | M12 | docs | yes | Glyph sets of two or more glyphs used at least twice become named classes (@first_last); sets used once stay inline. Reading the decompiled text of a few corpus fonts would settle the threshold. |
| `layout.decompileFeatures` | M12 | docs | yes | Mark classes are named @MC_lookup_subtable_class, one set per subtable, so a mark that has different anchors in two subtables of one lookup never collides. |
| `source.merge` | M12 | inferred | yes | A merged designspace is refused when it would be written beside BASE without inPlace, because its UFOs would replace BASE's; a user workflow that wants that would settle it. |
| `cli.global.abbreviations` (behavior) | M12 | inferred | – | A subcommand or long option may be shortened to any prefix that names exactly one subcommand, or one option of that subcommand; an ambiguous prefix is a usage error listing the candidates. No source fixes the rule; a user complaint about a clash would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without -o or --output-dir the dump goes to standard output, where ttx writes FONT.ttx beside the input; a user of the reference command line would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The JSON form is the TTX element tree, each element an object with name, attributes, text and children; no reference tool writes JSON, so readers of it settle the shape. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The hex form lists each table with a header line, then offset, 16 bytes in two groups of eight and their ASCII, as hexdump -C does; no reference tool settles it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A dump past 1 GiB of text (the JSON form counting its element tree) stops with an error, and a simple glyph whose contour end points decrease is written as hex with the decompilation error, where ttx would write repeated points; a font that needs more would settle the limit. |
| `tables copy` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Copying a table the target already has replaces it without a prompt, as tables add does; a test against the reference workflow (sfntedit extract then add) shows the same result. |
| `glyphs rename` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Names a GOADB or map lists that the font lacks are reported as missing, not refused, since one GOADB serves a whole family. Settled by running tx -gf with a GOADB that names an absent glyph. |
| `glyphs reorder` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --sort unicode puts encoded glyphs first by their lowest code point and keeps the unencoded ones after them in their old order. Settled by tx's +b ordering on a font with unencoded glyphs. |
| `glyphs reorder` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --sort size sorts by the byte size of each glyph's outline data (glyf entry or charstring), smallest first. Settled by finding a reference tool that documents a size order. |
| `glyphs reorder` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A post version 3 TrueType font, which stores no names, gets the names glyphNNNNN; fontTools derives names from cmap instead. Settled by comparing glyph orders with fontTools on such a font. |
| `metrics` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The text form adds statistics, font-statistics and kerning sections in the same bracketed style as the glyph lines; a comparison with a future tx release or user feedback would settle the layout. |
| `metrics` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Glyph names come from post, then the CFF charset, then uniXXXX from the cmap, then glyphNNNNN, with #n added to repeats as fontTools does; tx's own fallback names (g1, g2) were not followed. |
| `metrics` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With several fonts on the command line the text forms print a '## FONT' line before each report. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | While variation subsetting is not built, a variable font loses every variation table together (fvar, avar, gvar, cvar, HVAR, VVAR, MVAR, STAT), so no subset claims axes without the data behind them; M12.19 replaces this. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Unused CFF subroutines stay in the subset (pyftsubset removes them); outlines are the same, the file is larger. Comparing sizes on the corpus would show whether pruning is worth porting. |
| `collection pack` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A --share-table that names a font past the inputs, or a font without that table, is refused; otf2otc ignores it silently. A user report of a script that relies on otf2otc's behaviour would settle it. |
| `collection unpack` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | When two members have the same PostScript name, the later ones are named STEM-fontN; otc2otf writes them to the same path, so the last one wins. A user report would settle whether the reference behaviour is wanted. |
| `outlines remove-overlaps` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --min-area (default 25) only drops contours of glyphs whose overlaps were merged, so glyphs without overlaps match the fontTools result; checkoutlinesufo's behaviour on tiny contours of unmerged glyphs would settle it. |
| `outlines remove-overlaps` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --keep-contour-order has no effect: the editor's merge always keeps the order and start points of the contours it can match; checkoutlinesufo --ignore-contour-order output on a corpus font would show whether the difference matters. |
| `outlines direction` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | The default flavour of a source is the PostScript convention (the editor's); a source written for TrueType outlines would need --flavour truetype. |
| `outlines round` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Halfway values round away from zero to the grid (Rust's rounding); fontTools' otRound rounds them up, which only differs for negative halves on a grid coarser than 1 unit. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | `--gasp smooth` writes one range up to 0xFFFF ppem with flags 0x000F, the value the font-quality checks expect on an unhinted font; the reference app's export of an unhinted TrueType font would settle it. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A WOFF, WOFF2 or collection input gives a plain font file of the chosen face; keeping the container would need a decision once collections and WOFF writing are commands. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With `--keep-font-hints` the TrueType hdmx, VDMX and LTSH tables still go, since they record the hinted glyphs; only fpgm, prep, cvt, cvar and the maxp limits stay. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A palette cell is one glyph size wide and tall, plus two label lines (name, then code point and advance) at a tenth of the glyph size; the glyph is scaled so the font's ascender-to-descender height fills the cell. Settled by measuring a fontplot page of the reference script on our own font. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | An explicit --glyphs list is shown in --sort order (glyph order by default), not in the order given. Settled by running the reference script with an out-of-order -g list. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A waterfall without --text shows the characters of the selected glyphs, split into groups that fit one line at the largest size; each group is set at every size before the next group starts. Settled by a waterfallplot run of the reference script. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Pages of an SVG proof are numbered with at least three digits after the name (A.proof-001.svg); a one-page proof keeps the plain name. Nothing in the references fixes it. |
| `features list` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The text layout is ours (one line per table and feature, then an indented script/language line each, lookups after a colon; --by script swaps the two levels), since no reference prints this list in one format; JSON is the stable form. A request for spot's or comparefamily's layout would change it. |
| `variations build-avar` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without a designspace the font's own version 1 avar is read back as user-space maps and built again (fontTools' deprecated varLib.avar prints the maps instead); a version 2 avar is refused. Comparing with a user's expectations of the reference tool would settle it. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | `--read-only` admits only commands that declare themselves queries; `--allow` and `--deny` take shell-style patterns over command ids, deny winning. No reference has such options; settled by the sibling apps' conventions. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | File access is unrestricted by default; `--automation-read-root` and `--automation-write-root` each take one folder, grant read and write access separately, and are an error with `--connect`. No reference has such options; settled by the sibling apps' conventions. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | Macro, recent-document and URL commands are refused while a root, `--read-only`, `--allow` or `--deny` is set, because their files and steps are not in their parameters; agents' needs would settle whether a checked macro runner is worth building. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | No log notifications are sent until the client calls logging/setLevel; the protocol leaves the default to the server, and client feedback would settle it. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | The five prompts are start_family, add_master, build_accented_glyphs, kern_pair_list and prepare_release, with the steps of docs/mcp.md; agents' use of them would settle the wording. |
| `bench` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Bench's options come before the timed subcommand and everything from the subcommand on is timed, so a subcommand's own options (such as --json) never clash with bench's; the reference benchmark modules take no such command line, so use will settle it. |
| `tables show` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Records are expanded three levels deep unless --depth says otherwise; a user's need to see deeper by default would settle it. |
| `sources diff` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Masters and instances are matched by axis location first and by name second, and glyphs by name; a renamed glyph is reported as a rename when its outlines and code points are unchanged. A comparison with the companion tool on two versions of one font would settle it. |
| `sources diff` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | A change id is the part name and a hash of the change's place and both values (`glyphs:3f2a1c9b04de`), so it is stable between runs and changes when either side changes; the companion tool's own way of naming changes would settle it. |
| `sources diff` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Kerning groups are compared per glyph under the kerning part, and the non-kerning glyph groups of a UFO under the features part; how the companion tool groups them would settle it. |
| `sources merge` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Merging is two-way only (base and other), with no common ancestor; a three-way merge needs version history, which FontCraft does not keep. A user workflow that needs three-way merging would settle it. |
| `sources merge` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Without -o or --in-place the merge is refused rather than written beside BASE under a derived name; a user who expects a default output name would settle it. |
| `tableViewer.show` | M12 | inferred | no | The Table Viewer opens from the Window menu with no shortcut; the companion tool is a separate application, so nothing settles a menu place. |
| `sourceDiff.show` | M13 | inferred | no | Compare with File… sits in the File menu with no shortcut; nothing in the reference app settles a menu place. |

## `21-qa-checks.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `audit.run` | M3 | docs | yes | A source's instances are compiled as TrueType for the binary checks whatever outline format the source exports, and at most 32 of them; a run of the reference auditors on a source would settle whether the CFF flavour or every instance should be read. |
| `audit.run` | M3 | docs | yes | On a source, the family checks receive every instance compiled from it, the variable font included; checks that should compare only the static instances would need the reference tools' behaviour on sources to settle it. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | An outline fix on a composite glyph only reports unless `safeOnly` is false (21-qa question 4). |
| `audit.profile.save` | M12 | inferred | yes | Without store the profile goes to the user's settings rather than the project; a user report would settle which store people expect by default. |
| `audit.profile.delete` | M12 | inferred | yes | Deleting a profile that another profile of its store includes is refused rather than removing the include; a user report would settle it. |
| `audit.profile.setDefault` | M12 | inferred | yes | target defaults to the document, so a source carries its own default; a user report would settle whether the application default is expected instead. |
| `audit.ignoreIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | An entry made from a result keeps table-level keys but not outline addresses, which change with every edit; a user report would settle whether point-level entries are wanted. |
| `audit.unignoreIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | A call that matches no entry is refused rather than doing nothing, so a mistyped check id is noticed. |
| `audit.listIgnored` | M12 | inferred | yes | Whether an issue still occurs is judged only against the last kept run, with null when that run did not include the check, rather than running the check again. |
| `audit.export` | M12 | docs | yes | Without `format`, the format follows the extension of `path` (.json, .txt, .md, .html) and is JSON for any other; a look at how the command line is used would settle it. |
| `audit.export` | M12 | docs | yes | A result document can be passed in as `results` instead of naming a target, so a run on files can be exported without being kept; settled once the run on compiled fonts keeps its results. |
| `audit.export` | M12 | docs | yes | The Markdown and HTML reports end with the checks that were skipped or failed to run, with their reasons; the reference tools' reports would settle whether to list them. |
| `audit.checkOutlines` | M12 | docs | yes | A fix of a file given by path is refused; the command line opens the source, fixes it and saves it, so the engine command only fixes the open document. |
| `audit.checkInterpolation` | M12 | docs | yes | `showAll` lists the glyphs without problems in `glyphsWithoutProblems` instead of one line per glyph as the reference prints; its JSON output would settle the shape. |
| `audit.compareFamily` | M12 | docs | yes | The label is Check Family and the results go to the Font Audit panel, never to the Compare Family window, so the two features with similar names stay apart; the id stays for the command line (21-qa.md question 13). |
| `audit.report.metrics` | M12 | docs | yes | The underline thickness, which comparefamily does not print, is listed after the underline position and sorted the same way; the row's summary asks for it. |
| `audit.report.panose` | M12 | docs | yes | Two misspelled value names of comparefamily's tables are spelled correctly (Borders, Literals); a reader comparing text output would notice the difference. |
| `audit.toggleLiveAudit` | M3 | inferred | yes | Live audit is off when the app starts; the default is ours. |
| `audit.toggleMarkers` | M3 | inferred | yes | Markers are on when the app starts; the default is ours. |
| `audit.glyphSelectionSyntax` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | A piece that is a glyph name is read as a name even when it holds a hyphen, and `/N` names the glyph `cidNNNNN` (five digits) before glyph index N; a CID-keyed source in the reference tool would settle it. |
| `audit.familyGrouping` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | On a compiled font without its source the style comes from name IDs 2 and 17 and the fsSelection bits, never from the file name; only check.files.canonicalFileName reads the file name (21-qa.md question 9). |
| `audit.validatorKnownIssues` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | W2106 (OS/2 version below 3) is treated as wrong for OS/2 version 2 and later, and a ssNN tag counts as registered for 01 to 20 and cvNN for 01 to 99; a run of the validator on fonts with those values would settle it. |
| `audit.exitStatus` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | Results hidden by --min-severity still make the exit status 1 when they reach --fail-on; the reference tools have no separate display threshold, so nothing settles it. |
| `check.outlines.missingExtremes` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `minDistance` of 1 unit is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.almostStraightCurve` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxDeviation` of 2 units is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.almostOrthogonalHandle` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The defaults `maxAngle` 2° and `maxOffset` 2 units are first guesses to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.nearCoincidentPoints` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `minDistance` of 1 unit is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.coordinatesOutOfRange` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxEmMultiple` of 4 em is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.nearAlignmentZone` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `margin` of 2 units is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.kinkedSmoothNode` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxAngle` of 1° is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.unbalancedHandles` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxRatio` of 4 is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.components.nested` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Only glyphs that export are checked, since the reference check reads the compiled font; checking the exported TrueType font with the reference check would settle it. |
| `check.components.transformed` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A source does not say whether its TrueType export will be hinted, so a mirrored component (negative determinant) is a failure and any other transform a warning; the reference check makes both failures on hinted fonts and only mirroring on unhinted ones. |
| `check.anchors.unknownName` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | Anchors on corner, cap, segment and brush component glyphs are not checked, because their roles are not documented; observing which anchor names those glyphs use in the reference app would settle it. |
| `check.metrics.monospaceFlags` (check) | M12 | docs | – | One rule decides monospacing: at least 80% of the ASCII glyphs share one width, as the conformance checker of the opentype profile does; comparefamily's all-glyph hint is not reported separately (21-qa.md question 8). |
| `check.metrics.verticalMetricsScheme` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The `scheme` parameter defaults to the Google Fonts scheme (0); no reference names a default, so the owner's choice of house scheme would settle it. |
| `check.names.lineBreaks` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We treat a carriage return as a line break as well as a line feed; a FontBakery run on a name with a lone carriage return would show whether the verdicts differ on purpose. |
| `check.names.versionString` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The house-rule messages (Version prefix, three decimals, above 1.000, the word Development) are informational by default, so the opentype verdict equals FontBakery's opentype/font_version; a profile can raise them. Comparing verdicts on corpus fonts with short version strings would show whether that suits users. |
| `check.names.subfamilyMatchesStyleBits` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We read the style bits from OS/2 fsSelection rather than head.macStyle; a comparefamily run on a font whose two bit sets disagree would show the difference. |
| `check.names.macRecordsComplete` (check) | M12 | docs | – | With keepMacNames set, a font with no Macintosh records at all gets every missing record reported, where comparefamily checks Mac records only when the font has some; the project setting says the records are wanted. |
| `check.names.legalAndCredits` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We ask for Macintosh designer-note and URL records only in fonts that keep Mac records, since check.names.noMacRecords asks for none; a comparefamily run on a font without Mac records shows the reference's stricter rule. |
| `check.variations.pathCount` (check) | M12 | docs | – | An intermediate layer is compared with the nearest master layer of its glyph; running the reference tool on a designspace that holds intermediate layers as sparse sources would settle whether its pairs agree. |
| `check.variations.instanceOutsideAxisRange` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | Instances that do not export are left out; whether the reference app's own audit counts them would be settled by an inactive instance placed beyond the masters. |
| `check.variations.statItalAxis` (check) | M12 | docs | – | One font at a time, an italic variable font fails without an ital axis in STAT and a missing upright sibling is not reported; FontBakery pairs the fonts of a family. Comparing verdicts on corpus families with an upright and an italic variable font would settle whether the family checks must cover the pairing. |
| `check.variations.statValueQuality` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The values an axis must name are the OS/2 weight classes (100 to 900) and width classes (50 to 200) inside the fvar range; the vendor check takes them from its own axis registry, with names and flags. Comparing verdicts with that check on the corpus variable fonts would settle it. |
| `check.variations.staticInstantiation` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Instancing is tested by computing every glyph's outline and advance at the middle of each axis and reading the variation tables, not by running an instancer; comparing verdicts with FontBakery on fonts whose instancing fails would settle whether this catches the same faults. |
| `check.hinting.zonesOverlapInSource` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A zone counts as holding a metric line when the master's position of any metric except the italic angle, or 0, lies inside it or on its edge; how the reference app judges zones that serve no metric would settle it. |
| `check.hinting.stemsUndefined` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A direction counts as undefined when none of the master's stems of that direction has a positive value; stems of value 0 count as missing. How the reference autohinter treats a zero stem would settle it. |
| `check.kerning.kernTable` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The fix only removes the table (tables.delete); regenerating a format 0 table from GPOS for encoded glyphs is not built, and nothing says which of the two a user expects first. |
| `check.kerning.tabularKerning` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Without a tnum feature every glyph except marks is checked against the figures; FontBakery also adds marks whose Unicode category is a number or a symbol, which a comparison on fonts with such marks would settle. |
| `check.kerning.groupsDifferBetweenMasters` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Only kerning groups (public.kern1 and public.kern2) are compared, member order ignored, one result per differing group; FontBakery compares every group of the file and reports once per source. |
| `check.kerning.missingGlyphReference` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A group side with no member on the side facing the pair counts as dangling, since it is what removing the group's glyphs leaves; observing the reference app's export would settle whether it treats it the same. |
| `check.kerning.redundantPairs` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A pair's fallback is the next pair that applies without it, in the order glyph-glyph, glyph-group, group-glyph, group-group, as the UFO specification orders them; the reference app's kerning window would settle whether it orders exceptions the same. |
| `check.kerning.excessivePair` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A group side stands for its narrowest glyph in that master, glyphs without width are left out, and vertical kerning is not checked; nothing documents the reference's rule. |
| `check.family.uniqueStyleLinking` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Bold and italic come from OS/2 fsSelection, or head macStyle without an OS/2 table (the legacy family comparison reads macStyle); comparing the two tools' results on the legacy corpus families would settle it. |
| `check.family.consistentFamilyName` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The per-language comparison sits behind a `strict` threshold (default 0) so the opentype profile keeps the basic rule; the measure against the legacy comparison runs with strict 1. |
| `check.family.panoseFamilyType` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The legacy rules (all zero, first four only) sit behind a `strict` threshold (default 0) because the check's home is the opentype profile; the measure against the legacy comparison runs with strict 1. |
| `check.family.superfamilyVerticalMetrics` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The sibling families are the typographic families among the fonts given in one run, since a family check sees fonts, not folders; the folder listing becomes one informational result naming the families. |
| `check.family.layoutConsistency` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Fonts are compared within typographic families rather than across every font given, which is the same when one family is checked; running both tools on a folder holding two families would show any difference. |
| `check.family.glyphWidthRatio` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Glyph names come from the post table or the CFF charset; fonts without names get made-up names per glyph id, which compare by glyph order; checking a post format 3 family against the reference would settle it. |
| `check.family.familyBlues` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The zone limits are those of the CFF specification (14 values for FamilyBlues, 10 for FamilyOtherBlues); the reference allows 14 for both, so a family with 11 to 14 FamilyOtherBlues values would show the difference. |
| `check.family.italicsHaveUprights` (check) | M12 | docs | – | An italic font's partner is an upright font of the same typographic family with the same weight and width classes (any upright variable font for an italic variable font), read from the style bits instead of the file names the reference reads; running both on a family with a missing upright would settle it. |
| `check.family.tabularFigureWidths` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Inconsistent widths fail, as in the stricter of the two reference checks, while the suffix rule and the half-width note follow the other; comparing both reference checks on the corpus families would settle it. |
| `check.files.fileSize` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A font compiled in memory is measured by its uncompressed sfnt bytes, so a WOFF2 export can warn here and pass in FontBakery; measuring the compressed output would settle it. |
| `check.files.sanitiser` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Unsorted, duplicated, misaligned, overlapping and out-of-bounds tables are failures and wrong search fields a warning, as we read the sanitiser's published behaviour; running the sanitiser on fonts built with each fault would settle it. |
| `check.files.roundTrip` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A table that reads back with different bytes is not reported, because FontBakery reports only errors of the dump and the read; comparing verdicts on the corpus would settle it. |
| `check.files.rasterises` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Every glyph is drawn, where FontBakery loads one character with FreeType, so this check can fail where FontBakery passes; the verdict measure on the corpus would show how often. |
| `check.files.externalValidator` (check) | M12 | docs | – | One face audited alone counts as part of a family, so the allowance for a slanted face without the italic bit (italicAngleWithoutItalicBit) never applies here; running the validator on a single slanted face and comparing would settle it. |
| `check.files.canonicalFileName` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The family comes from name ID 16, else 1, and the style from ID 17, else 2, with spaces removed, and an empty style counts as Regular; axisregistry's build_filename would settle it. |
| `check.files.canonicalFileName` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A .woff or .woff2 file keeps its extension; any other file is expected to end in .otf for CFF outlines and .ttf otherwise. |
| `check.sources.consistentGlyphSet` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The check runs on every source with several masters, not only on designspace documents as in FontBakery; for .glyphs sources it can repeat a missing-glyph finding of the variations checks. |
| `check.sources.requiredInfoFields` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A master's metric counts as unset only when its value list ends before it; a value stated as 0 between others cannot be told from an unset one. |
| `check.sources.recommendedInfoFields` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Underline thickness and position and the version are never reported: the model gives them the reference's defaults, so they always have a value. |
| `check.sources.unnecessaryInfoFields` (check) | M12 | docs | – | versionString and uniqueID are reported in .glyphs sources too, since they compile to the same name records as the UFO keys FontBakery names. |
| `audit.showPanel` | M12 | inferred | no | The Font Audit panel opens from the Window menu with no shortcut, because the reference app has no audit panel; a later decision on shortcuts would settle it (21-qa.md question 1). |
| `audit.nextIssue` | M12 | inferred | no | No menu item and no shortcut: the panel's arrow buttons and the Edit View marker badges run it, because the reference app has no audit panel (21-qa.md question 1). |
| `audit.previousIssue` | M12 | inferred | no | No menu item and no shortcut: the panel's arrow buttons and the Edit View marker badges run it, because the reference app has no audit panel (21-qa.md question 1). |

## `30-formats.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ufo.openTypeCategories` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | glyphsLib classifies after propagating anchors into composites; a layer without anchors of its own counts its components' attaching anchors, which gives the same class for the usual accented composites. Settled by comparing with glyphsLib's UFOs of the corpus. |
| `ufo.openTypeCategories` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | Custom glyph data files do not change the classes, as glyphsLib reads only its built-in data here. Settled by a reference export with a custom GlyphData file. |
| `glyphs3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | A gray colour `(gray, alpha)` is read as an RGBA colour with equal components, and the shape remembers which of its colours the file wrote as gray so that they are written as gray again. Settled by watching whether the reference app writes a colour picked from the gray swatches as gray. |
| `glyphs3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | A version 3 `strokePos` stored as text that is not a plain number stays a stored value, because it cannot be converted to the version 4 convention. Settled by finding a file whose stroke position is an expression. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A font-info value that ufoLib cannot convert (an unknown `fontStyle`, `widthName` or `msCharSet`, or a value of the wrong type) is dropped and the file opens; ufoLib and norad refuse the file instead. A UFO 1 from the tools of that time with such a value would settle whether refusing is better. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The feature code is joined as defcon joins it (a line break between the classes and each feature, tags sorted when the lib has no order); norad leaves out the line breaks between features. Both texts compile the same; a UFO 1 whose features only work with one of the two joins would settle it. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A tag the feature order lists twice is written once, so a crafted order cannot multiply the feature code; defcon writes it each time. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Font-info keys a UFO 1 does not define are dropped, as ufoLib drops them, so the saved UFO 3 holds only valid keys. |
| `variable-ttf.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | A variable font beyond the limits (more than 256 avar points on an axis, more than 2,000,000 layers to make) opens as its default instance with the reason among the warnings rather than being refused; settled by checking what users expect when a huge variable font opens. |
| `variable-ttf.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | A peak becomes a master when every coordinate is -1, 0 or 1, or when at least half of the glyphs with variations use it; any other peak becomes an intermediate layer of the glyphs that use it, on the nearest master. Settled by opening fonts built from sources with known masters and intermediate layers and comparing. |
| `variable-ttf.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | A master takes the name of the named instance at its location, else its axis names and design coordinates; an intermediate layer is named by its coordinates in braces. |
| `cff2.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | Every region of the CFF2 variation store becomes a master, since the charstrings do not say which glyphs use which region without evaluating them. Settled by opening CFF2 fonts built from sources with intermediate layers and comparing. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Vertical metrics are measured on the glyphs d, H, x and p (falling back to the FontBBox), since a Type 1 font has no such fields; the reference app's import of a .pfb would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Ghost hints become TopGhost and BottomGhost hints with place (edge, 0), the edge being y for a top ghost and y + dy for a bottom ghost as tx reports them; the reference app's import of a hinted .pfb would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Standard stems are named hStem0, hStem1… and vStem0…; the reference app's import of a .pfb would show its names. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A font whose glyphs together hold more than 20,000,000 hint and path operations, or take more than 500,000,000 charstring steps to run, is refused rather than read in part; no limit of the reference readers is documented, and real fonts stay far below both. A font past them in the reference app would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A repeated glyph name gets .1, .2…; when the name is too long for its suffix, it is cut to 247 bytes first so the result stays within 255. Importing a font with repeated long names into the reference app would settle it. |
| `afm` (format) | M5 | docs | – | A glyph's code is its first Unicode value when that is at most 255, else -1; Adobe's note means a code in the font's encoding. An AFM exported by the reference would settle it. |
| `pfm` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A word before the pair list equal to `etmKernPairs` is a count and is skipped (and gives the count when `etmKernPairs` is 0); the DDK describes no such word, but files made for PostScript printers are said to have it. A PFM file from a font vendor would settle it. |
| `pfm` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Character codes of a font whose `dfCharSet` is not 2 (symbol) are read as Windows-1252; a symbol font's code c is looked up as U+F000 + c, then as c. PFM files of symbol fonts from a vendor would settle it. |
| `pfm` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Kerning amounts are in `etmMasterUnits` per em (1,000 when the field is 0, as the DDK says PostScript drivers assume) and are scaled to the font's units per em, rounded half up. |
| `cff` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A font whose glyphs together hold more than 20,000,000 hint and path operations, or take more than 500,000,000 charstring steps to run, is refused rather than read in part; no limit of the reference readers is documented, and real fonts stay far below both. A font past them in the reference app would settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | An open subpath in a glyph's path data is read as closed, since SVG fonts fill every subpath; no reference reader was available to settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A glyph without glyph-name is named from its characters through the glyph database (uniXXXX when it cannot be loaded), ligatures joined with underscores; a reference importer of SVG fonts would settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Only the first missing-glyph element is read, as .notdef; later ones are left out with a warning. A reference importer of SVG fonts would settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A repeated glyph name gets .1, .2…; when the name is too long for its suffix, it is cut to 247 bytes first so the result stays within 255. Importing a font with repeated long names into the reference app would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A contour is closed when its last point equals its first and open otherwise; the page does not say how an open contour is written. A file from the originating editor with an open path would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | In a quadratic layer a `c` line is read as a quadratic segment when both controls are the same point or are exactly two thirds of the way to one point; the page does not say how quadratic splines are written. A quadratic file from the editor would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | An on-curve point flagged 0x80 that lies halfway between two control points is dropped, so runs of quadratic control points come back. A TrueType-derived file from the editor would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The point flags of a closed contour's start come from its `m` line, not from the line that returns to it. A file with a smooth start point would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | `Refer` and `Kerns2` glyph numbers are the third number of a glyph's `Encoding` line (its original position), or its position in the file when that is missing; a reference that matches no glyph falls back to its code point. Files with reordered glyphs would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph without a `Width` line is as wide as the em. A file with such a glyph would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Anchor names follow the reference's convention: the class name for base and base-mark anchors, `_` before it for marks, `_1`, `_2`… after it for ligature components, `entry` and `exit` for cursive anchors. The page names only the classes. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Pair positioning is lookup type 258 (0x102), read from the page's example; subtables no lookup declares count as kerning. A file with several lookup types would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Glyph pairs take precedence over class pairs, and an earlier class table over a later one, as in the model; in OpenType the order of subtables decides. A file with overlapping subtables would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A kerning class becomes a group named after its first glyph; a glyph already in a group on that side kerns by name instead. Files with a glyph in classes of two tables would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Cap height and x-height, which the format does not store, are the tops of H and x, or 70% and 50% of the em; overshoots are zero. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Hhea, typo and win values marked as offsets from a computed value are not kept, because the page does not say how the value is computed. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The digits after the point in `Version` are thousandths (`1.5` is version 1.500). |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A TrueType instruction block (`TtTable`, `TtInstrs`) ends at a line starting `EndTt` or at the first line that is neither a mnemonic nor numbers; the page names no end line. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph layer the font does not declare (the multi-layered Type 3 form) is merged into the master layer. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph listed in more than one class on one side of a class kerning table belongs to the first of them; the later listings are skipped and reported. A table from the originating editor with such a repeat would settle which class it keeps. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Unscaled placement keeps one point per unit and puts the top edge of the bounding box (else of the drawing) at the given top, matching how SVG files are placed; importing an EPS of known geometry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Paths that are only used to clip, or ended without painting, are left out and counted as dropped; the reference may import every path, which importing a file with a clipping path and an unpainted path would settle. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Arcs (arc, arcn, arct), text and images make the file fail with a message rather than being skipped; an EPS with an arc or a text object imported in the reference app would show whether it skips them or converts them. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | A path filled inside gsave/grestore and stroked again afterwards is imported once; a reference import of such a file would settle it. |
| `proof-html` (format) | M12 | inferred | – | The default sample is an English pangram with digits when the font maps all of its characters, else the first 64 characters of the character set. Settled by the owner. |

## `40-tables.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `compile.instanceInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | Copied from fontmake's instancer: a glyph with no layer in the origin master is left out of the instance; a glyph whose layer is empty in some masters but drawn at the origin interpolates without the empty ones; outlines are not rounded before the compile; a glyph that does not export and cannot interpolate keeps its nearest layer instead of failing the instance. Settle each with an instance export from the reference app. |
| `compile.instanceLayoutInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A variable font's kerning groups are the font's glyph groups in every master; a UFO master whose own groups differ is read with the glyph groups. A designspace whose masters' groups differ would settle it against fontmake. |
| `compile.instanceLayoutInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | When a master's mark rules differ from the origin's (an anchor missing in one master), the mark positions keep the origin's values with a warning instead of failing the export. An export from the reference app of such a font would settle it. |
| `compile.removeOverlapsStatic` (behavior) | M2 | docs | – | A composite glyph keeps its components at export, so components that overlap each other keep their overlaps, following fontmake; how the reference app exports such a glyph is not documented. |
| `compile.glyphsOutlinePreSteps` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | A glyph whose anchor propagation fails keeps its own anchors and the build goes on with a warning; exporting a source with a component cycle from the reference app settles what it does. |
| `compile.productionNames` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | Use Custom Naming (disablesNiceNames) keeps the source names at export, as the handbook's Don't use Production Names entry says it has the same effect; the reference compilers ignore it. Settled by exporting a font with Use Custom Naming on from the reference app. |
| `compile.productionNameSanitising` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | A name that is empty after cleaning, or longer than the 255 bytes post can store, becomes glyphNNNNN (by glyph id) with a warning; fontc would write the empty or long name. Settled only by a reference that writes such a font. |
| `compile.kernGroupReconciliation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A static instance interpolates kerning as fontmake's instancer does (fontMath): a pair one master lacks takes the value that master's groups give it (the pair, the second side's group, the first side's group, both groups, else zero: the compiled kern lookup's order, where fontMath tries the first side's group before the second); a master read from its own UFO uses that file's groups; a pair that comes out zero is dropped unless one side is a glyph in a group; values are rounded half up. The common refinement of the groups is for variable fonts (M9.9); settle by comparing an instance of a source whose masters group glyphs differently with fontmake. |
| `compile.kernFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | Where the featureWriters setting asks for what fontc refuses to build (ignoreMarks off, a quantization other than 1, kernFeatureWriter2), FontCraft writes the kerning with the standard settings and warns; a build with fontmake would settle what the setting does there. |
| `compile.kernFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | The featureWriters setting is read from the font's user data, then from the built master's; fontc reads only a .glyphs font's user data, while glyphsLib also carries a master's into its UFO lib. |
| `compile.markFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | A top-level include in the feature code keeps all five generated features out until M8, as it keeps the generated kerning out, since the included file may hold any of them; compiling the included file (M8) settles it. |
| `compile.markFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | Anchors whose names the reference compilers refuse (`_`, `_top_1`, a number 0), whose ligature number is above 1,024, or whose positions do not fit 16 bits are left out with a warning, where fontc stops the build; an export of such anchors from the reference app would settle it. |
| `compile.gdefClasses` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | A static master's classes are worked out from that master's anchors only, as fontmake does for each master's UFO, where fontc looks at the anchors of every master; a source whose masters differ in anchor names would settle it. |
| `compile.gdefLigatureCarets` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | Carets at the same position are all kept, as fontc keeps them, where ufo2ft keeps one; a ligature with two carets at one position in the reference app's export would settle it. |
| `compile.bracketLayerGlyphs` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | When the font already has a glyph named like an alternate glyph (`a.BRACKET.varAlt01`), the alternate takes the next free number instead of replacing or clashing with it; settle by exporting such a font from the reference app and reading the glyph order. |
| `compile.bracketLayerGlyphs` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A kern pair on a glyph with alternate layers is copied to its alternate glyphs the way glyphsLib copies it, not to every combination of alternates as fontc does; settle by exporting a variable font with a pair between two glyphs that both have alternate layers and reading its kerning at a location inside both ranges. |
| `gasp` (table) | M10 | docs | – | A CFF export gets no gasp table even with the parameter, as ufo2ft writes gasp for TrueType output only and the handbook describes the parameter for TrueType fonts; a CFF export from the reference app with the parameter would settle it. |
| `gasp` (table) | M10 | docs | – | A gasp Table parameter with no usable range counts as no source, so a hinted font gets the default ranges; an export from the reference app with an empty parameter would settle it. |

## `90-secondary.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `path.selectTunniLines` | M13 | docs | yes | A segment counts as inside the selection when one of its handles is selected, both its end nodes are, or the segment itself is, as for Fit Curve; FontLab does not define it, and selecting nodes in FontLab with Tunni lines on would settle it. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | Where a segment's handles are parallel or diverge, so they have no intersection, tension is shown as a dash and Set Curve Tension leaves the segment unchanged with a warning; FontLab does not say (open question 9 in docs/spec/90-secondary-references.md). |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | A zero-length handle has no direction, so its segment counts as having no Tunni point and is left unchanged too; running the command on such a segment in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | With no segments named the command acts on every curve segment of the layer, and it also takes a list of glyphs; FontLab documents only the selection. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | An absolute tension must be 1 to 100 percent and a relative step keeps each handle within 1 to 100; FontLab gives no range, and entering larger values in its Node panel would settle it. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | A relative step is added to each handle's own tension, so unequal handles stay unequal; FontLab does not say whether it balances them first. |
| `path.harmonizeNodes` | M13 | docs | yes | At an inflection (the far handles on opposite sides of the tangent) the two curvatures are made equal in size, since equal signed curvature is impossible there; running Harmonize on an S-curve in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.harmonizeNodes` | M13 | docs | yes | A smooth node with a straight side, or a selected node that is not between two curve segments, is left unchanged and listed in the result; FontLab does not say. |
| `path.harmonizeHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | G2 keeps the sum of the two handle lengths at the node and only divides it differently; FontLab does not say which lengths it keeps, and comparing handle lengths before and after Harmonize Handles in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.harmonizeHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | G3 changes the lengths of the two neighbouring handles along their own directions, by the smallest change found, so the neighbouring nodes keep their tangents; FontLab does not describe Superharmonize's method. |
| `path.harmonizeHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | With no node selected both orders act on every smooth node of the layer, as Harmonize Nodes does; FontLab documents only the selection. |
| `path.makeInkTrap` | M13 | docs | yes | The two copies move half the width each, perpendicular to the corner's bisector, with the copy ending the incoming segment on that segment's side, and their neighbouring handles move with them; the reference only says the nodes move apart 'intelligently', so watching it trap an inside and an outside corner would settle it. |
| `path.makeInkTrap` | M13 | docs | yes | Without an inkTrapWidth parameter the width is 2% of the em; the reference's default for a new font would settle it. |
| `path.loopCorner` | M13 | docs | yes | Each segment goes on past the corner by `size`, which defaults to half the master's first vertical stem (4% of the em without stems); the reference does not say how far its loops reach, so measuring one would settle it. |
| `path.replaceWithCorner` | M13 | docs | yes | One segment or gap per path per call, and neighbours whose extensions meet behind them are refused rather than shortened; trying both in the reference would settle it. |
| `path.alignToMask` | M13 | docs | yes | Points snap to background nodes and handles alike, and a moved on-curve node takes its unselected handles along; aligning a curve's node to a background node in the reference would settle it. |
| `path.collapsePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | The centre is the middle of the selected nodes' bounding box, rounded to the grid, and at least two on-curve nodes are needed; collapsing three uneven nodes in the reference would settle it. |
| `path.scaleToSameSize` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph counts as uppercase when its case says so or one of its code points is an uppercase letter; trying a glyph without code points in the reference would settle it. |
| `edit.storeSelection` | M13 | docs | yes | Selected anchors and components are stored with the nodes, by anchor name and shape index; FontLab documents node selections only, and its panel on a selection with a component would settle it. |
| `edit.storeSelection` | M13 | docs | yes | Storing an empty selection is refused; FontLab's Add button with nothing selected would settle it. |
| `edit.getStoredSelections` | M13 | inferred | yes | A query that maps stored selections onto any layer is our addition for agents and scripts; FontLab has no such command. |
| `edit.recallSelection` | M13 | docs | yes | On a layer where only part of a stored selection resolves (an incompatible master), the parts that resolve are selected and the reply counts the rest as missing; recalling where nothing resolves is refused. FontLab's panel on an incompatible master would settle it. |
| `component.copyTransformation` | M13 | docs | yes | A target entry without `glyph` or `layer` means the source glyph and the source layer's id; the reference copies to whatever is selected, so it has no such default. |
| `component.copyTransformation` | M13 | docs | yes | An automatically aligned target takes the scale, rotation and slant but keeps the position alignment gives it on the aligned axes; the reference has no automatic alignment, so nothing settles this but use. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A match needs every point of the other glyph's outline within 0.5 unit after the best translation, or after a translation and flip when `flips` is on; FontLab gives no tolerance. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Only glyphs whose layer has closed paths and no components serve as bases; running it in the reference app on a font whose base glyphs are themselves composites would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Bases are tried from the most nodes down, a base must have fewer nodes than the target or as many and come earlier in the glyph order (no cycles), and among bases of equal size one whose name (without suffix and 'comb') appears in the target's name goes first; comparing the reference app's choice between identical outlines (period and dotaccentcomb) would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `layer` or `layers` every master layer is searched, each on its own, and a glyph whose masters got different components is warned about; the reference app's behaviour on a multi-master font would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A mirrored match is a mirrored copy drawn in the opposite direction, as a mirrored component draws once decomposed; the offset is rounded to the font's grid when the match still holds. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A detected component keeps the default automatic alignment when aligning puts it where the outline was (within the tolerance) and leaves the width as it is; alignment is turned off on the components it would move, and on all new components of a layer whose width it would change, as the primary reference's Import Metrics does for metrics that alignment would change. Checking the alignment setting of components the reference app detected, with and without matching anchors, would settle it. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | The first glyph in font order whose same-id layer has only closed paths equal to the selection wins, within 0.5 unit after a translation (and a flip with `flips`); selecting an outline shared by two glyphs in the reference app would settle which one it picks. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | No matching glyph is an error that changes nothing; the reference app's message or silence when nothing matches would settle it. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | The new component keeps the default automatic alignment unless aligning would move it beyond the tolerance or change the width, and then alignment is off on it; alignment is judged on the layer as it ends up, so a component next to remaining paths keeps the default. Checking the alignment setting of a component the reference app detected would settle it. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | `masters` defaults to every master, which covers the reference's companion action that copies the recipe to all masters; seeing whether that action is a command of its own would settle it. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `recipe`, a glyph keeps the recipe one of its layers has, else takes the components its name decomposes into by Create Composite's rules; the reference's default recipe is not documented. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | An automatic layer holds only the recipe's components: paths or components added by hand are replaced at the next rebuild; whether the reference lets one draw on an automatic layer would settle it. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | A recipe whose components contain the glyph is refused, and a layer whose recipe becomes circular later stays empty. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | Recipe parts are glyph names only, without placement suffixes such as `@anchor`. |
| `anchor.removeExpressions` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyph` or `glyphs` it acts on every glyph, and locked glyphs are skipped and listed; the reference acts on the selection. |
| `anchor.setExpressions` | M13 | docs | yes | Expressions are set by a command of their own, where the reference types them into the anchor's coordinate fields; a syntax error refuses the command. |
| `script.runActionSet` | M13 | docs | yes | Action sets and macros are one file format and one command; FontLab keeps action sets in its Actions folder apart from scripts, a split FontCraft does not copy. |
| `script.saveActionSet` | M13 | docs | yes | An existing file is replaced only when `replace` is true, otherwise the save is refused; FontLab's documentation does not say what saving over an existing set does. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | Both edges of a stem move by half the change, so the stem keeps its centre line; the secondary reference running a rule on a stem away from the sidebearings would settle it. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | A horizontal stem's edge on the baseline, x-height, cap height, ascender or descender stays and the other edge takes the whole change; the secondary reference on a serif sitting on the baseline would settle it. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | A round stem (the sides of o) is adjusted by moving the extreme nodes on both sides with their handles; one without a node at either measured point is left alone. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | A rule that would make a stem narrower than 1 unit makes the whole command fail; the secondary reference with an offset larger than a stem would settle it. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on. |
| `file.exportWithProfiles` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `fonts` the active document is exported, not every open one; the reference's Export Fonts As dialog would settle the default. |
| `file.exportWithProfiles` | M13 | docs | yes | A profile's subfolder may not climb out of the destination with `..`, for any caller; the reference's profile settings would settle whether it can. |
| `file.exportWithProfiles` | M13 | docs | yes | Two profiles that would write the same file refuse the whole export instead of keeping one; the reference's behaviour with two profiles sharing a folder would settle it. |
| `file.saveExportProfile` | M13 | docs | yes | A copy made with basedOn writes into a subfolder named after the copy unless `settings` names a folder; the reference's Duplicate would settle it. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | SVG uses the io-svg writer, PNG the render crate and PDF a minimal writer of our own; FontLab's options beyond the three formats are not followed. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | A picture spans the advance width and the master's descender to ascender, widened to the outline where it reaches further, so pictures of one font line up; exporting a few glyphs in the reference would settle the frame. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | SVG and PDF use one user unit or point per font unit (a PDF is scaled down to at most 14400 points a side); PNG is black on a transparent background at 256 pixels per em by default. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | Characters a file name cannot hold become `_`, and names that differ only in case get `_2`, `_3`; the reference's file names for such glyphs would settle it. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyphs` every glyph of the font is exported; the shell passes the selection. |
| `view.filterByEncodingFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Encoding files are read in three line forms (`name slot`, `0xSlot 0xCodePoint`, and one name or code point for the next slot) with `%%FONTLAB ENCODING:` and `%%FONTLAB CODEPAGE:` titles; FontLab's own encoding and code page files would settle the forms it accepts. |
| `view.filterByEncodingFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `path`, `name` picks a built-in code page (ASCII, Latin 1, Windows 1252, Mac OS Roman); with `path` it names the filter. FontLab's list of bundled encodings would settle which ones ship. |
| `view.filterByEncodingFile` | M13 | docs | yes | A code-point slot the font has no glyph for lists the glyph data's name for that code point, and running the command again with the same name replaces the list filter instead of adding a second one. |
| `font.compareInTable` | M13 | docs | yes | Each font is read in its first master, and without `fonts` every open document is compared; FontLab's Font window comparison mode would settle which master and fonts it uses. |
| `font.compareInTable` | M13 | docs | yes | Measured values are rounded to hundredths of a unit before rows are marked as differing; the reference's own comparison display would settle the precision. |
| `path.findIntersections` | M13 | docs | yes | Crossings of a path with itself count too, and touching curves get a node like crossing ones; the manual speaks only of overlapping contours. |
| `path.findIntersections` | M13 | docs | yes | Locked paths get no nodes but still cross the others; the manual does not mention locks. |
| `path.makeParallel` | M13 | docs | yes | With two lines, the moved line keeps its length and turns about its fixed end to the nearer of the two parallel directions; the manual only says the lines become parallel. |
| `path.spacePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | The lowest and highest points stay and the others keep their order; the manual only says the points are spaced regularly. |
| `path.spacePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | Regions join points whose coordinates along the spacing axis are at most `within` apart (default 100 units), and the centres of the regions' extents are spaced evenly; the manual gives no distance and no reference point. |
| `path.spacePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | An on-curve node's handles move with it unless they are selected too. |
| `path.roundToCluster` | M13 | docs | yes | x and y are clustered on their own, every node and handle included; a cluster grows while values are at most `within` (default 1 unit) above its lowest, and takes the value most of its members have (ties: nearest the mean, then the lower). The manual gives no distance and no rule for the shared value. |
| `path.makeArc` | M13 | docs | yes | Of the many ellipses through the two nodes with the path's directions there, FontCraft takes the quarter ellipse framed by the two tangent lines (a quarter circle when they meet square at equal distances), and half an ellipse when the directions are opposite; the manual does not say which ellipse it picks beyond preferring one with a node on an axis. |
| `path.makeArc` | M13 | docs | yes | At an open end without a neighbouring segment the segment's own direction is used, and the result is always a cubic segment. |
| `path.insertNodeAt` | M13 | docs | yes | A node goes in at every place the segment crosses the coordinate, and a segment that does not reach it is refused; the manual does not cover several or no crossings. |
| `path.nameContour` | M13 | docs | yes | An empty name removes the name; the manual does not say how a name is cleared. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | The offset moves nodes and handles without adding nodes, so curves are only approximately parallel to the original; an outline comparison against the secondary reference would settle how close it must be. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Method auto picks cjk for CJK ideographs, Hangul and kana and lcg for everything else; the secondary reference's documentation on auto would settle it. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Default zones: bottom at the baseline, top at the x-height for lowercase glyphs (by code point, else by the first letter of the name) and the cap height for the rest, serif height 0; the secondary reference's dialog defaults would settle them. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Counters auto means retain with lcg and squish with cjk; retain moves each vertical stem right by the amount times its index so the counters keep their widths, and the advance width grows by the amount times (stems − 1). |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Squish keeps the advance width, so the outline grows into both sidebearings by half the amount each. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Cleanup (Remove Overlap after the change) is off by default. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | The counter add applies only to counters between two stems; the parts of the outline left of the first stem and right of the last (arms, serifs) only scale. The secondary reference on a T would settle it. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph with no vertical stem scales as one piece by the counter scale. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | Counters scaled below zero width become zero width. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on. |
| `filter.changeXHeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Above the x-height band the outline stretches so the master's ascender stays where it is, and nothing above the ascender moves; the secondary reference on b or h would settle whether ascenders move instead. |
| `filter.changeXHeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Below the serif height nothing moves (descenders included) and the band within the serif height of the x-height shifts rigidly. |
